- R4 decisions beyond §2 (2026-09-12, `e77ff0f`), pinned by `src/components/editor/use-tier.test.ts`, `editor-layout.test.tsx`, `deck-editor.test.tsx` (the first RTL render of `DeckEditor`), `editor-header.test.tsx` (the Tools button), `search-pane.test.tsx` (`onInspect`, coarse rows), `src/app/not-found.test.tsx` and `error.test.tsx` (the footer), `src/components/deck/ambient-art.test.tsx` (`creditInset`) and `deck-text-view.test.tsx` (coarse rows on editable rows only):
  - The tier model. CSS decides every width: the editor root is `h-dvh` at EVERY tier (`lg:` is gone from it) and the panes container is a Base UI Tabs root that is `md:grid md:grid-cols-[minmax(18rem,22rem)_minmax(0,1fr)]` and `wide:grid-cols-[minmax(18rem,20rem)_minmax(0,1fr)_minmax(18rem,20rem)]`; `--breakpoint-wide: 75rem` in `globals.css`'s `@theme inline` compiles to `@media (min-width: 75rem)` (12 rules in the dev CSS) and fires at 1200 px inclusive. Measured on dev: 1440 → 320 / 800 / 320; 1200 → 320 / 560 / 320; 1199 → 352 / 847 (two panes, the tools section `display: none`, the header's Tools button shown); 1024 → 352 / 672; 768 (tablet preset) → 352 / 416 — the R3 block's 256 px deck squeeze at 1024 is paid (672 there, and never under 416 from 768 up). One hook decides the rest: `useTier()` (`src/components/editor/use-tier.ts`) is `useSyncExternalStore` over `matchMedia("(min-width: 48rem)")` and `("(min-width: 75rem)")` → `phone | md | wide`, both lists subscribed once, server snapshot `wide` (the value never paints on prod — `/decks/new` is static and its HTML carries the Suspense fallback, no `data-tier`; in DEV the page renders per request and SSRs the editor with that snapshot, then hydration re-reads the client value). The tier decides only where the tools content mounts (the third grid cell at `wide`, the Drawer at `md`, the Tools tab on phones) and which of the two sheets exists — so crossing 768 or 1200 px REMOUNTS the tools content and nothing else: a panel refetches once if its tab is active (measured: Suggestions fetched once at 1440, once more after the 1440 → 1024 crossing with Suggestions selected, and never again across drawer closes). Lifting the panels' fetched state into `DeckEditor` to avoid that is not cheap and was not done. The root carries `data-tier` for tests and measurements.
  - `EditorLayout` (`src/components/editor/editor-layout.tsx`, "use client") owns the tier plumbing: props `tier`, `gameId`, `header`, `dialogs`, `search`, `deck`, `tools`, `sheet`, `sheetTitle`, `ambient`, `activePane` + `onActivePaneChange`, `toolsOpen` + `onToolsOpenChange`, `sheetOpen` + `onSheetOpenChange`, `deckCount`; `DeckEditor` keeps composing the panes and hands them over. The three sections ARE the Tabs panels (`TabsContent render={<section aria-label=… />} keepMounted`), so Base UI wires each tab's `aria-controls` to its section. Base UI's own `hidden` and `inert` on inactive panels are overridden (`hidden={false} inert={false}`) at every tier and visibility is CSS from the root's `data-pane` (`hidden group-data-[pane=search]/panes:block md:block`; the tools section `md:hidden wide:block`) — two reasons: Tailwind's preflight rule `[hidden] { display: none !important }` cannot be beaten by a utility (an `!important` in an earlier cascade layer wins), and Base UI un-hides the leaving panel only after its exit animation, which would paint two panes for a frame. From `md` the panels also drop `role`, `aria-labelledby` and `tabIndex` (`mergeProps` lets an explicit `undefined` remove them), so the sections read as the labelled regions they were; on phones the active panel keeps `role="tabpanel"` and `tabIndex=0`. Measured at 1440 and 1024: three sections, none `hidden`, none `inert`, `role` null; at 375: three sections in the DOM, the inactive two `display: none` (no `hidden` attribute), the active one a tabpanel controlled by its tab.
  - The scroll model: each section is its own scroll container (`min-h-0 overflow-y-auto`), so the sticky search block and the sticky group headers stick inside their pane everywhere (measured on the phone: the search block at the section's top, 117 px, while the list was scrolled 300 px) and the phone's document never scrolls (`scrollHeight` 812 = the viewport). `display: none` drops a pane's scroll position, so every section records `scrollTop` in an `onScroll` handler (a `Map` in a ref) and a layout effect on `[activePane, tier]` restores it — measured: 300 → 0 while hidden → 300 on return; pinned in RTL. The sections carry `pb-8` so their last rows scroll clear of R2's credit chip (the R2 block's observed overlap — the chip spans the whole pane width on a phone; measured: the empty state's action bottom at 691 under the chip's top at 728). The panes container is padded by `--editor-bottom-inset` so the active pane ends above the bar.
  - The tab bar: Base UI `TabsList variant="indicator"` as `fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0 z-40 border-t bg-background pb-[env(safe-area-inset-bottom)] md:hidden` (`aria-label="Editor panes"`), three `h-14` triggers with a lucide icon over the label (Deck with the live count as text — "Deck · 34", never color), `TabsIndicator` moved to the bar's TOP edge (`top-0 bottom-auto`); Deck opens first; the bar's tabs are buttons, so `/` and `?` from a focused tab still reach the editor's listener. Measured on the mobile preset: the bar at y 755, 57 px with its border; triggers 122 × 56; the indicator 2 px on the top edge; arrow keys move focus between tabs (Base UI's manual activation — Enter or Space selects). The bar is display:none from `md` (and the tablist with it, so the regions are the a11y structure there).
  - Add cards: a primary `Button` beside the "Deck" heading on phones only (`md:hidden`) AND the empty state's action at every tier (it focuses the search box the way "Choose commander" does); both call the editor's `focusSearch`, which on a phone sets the active pane to Search and focuses the box in a layout effect (`useLayoutEffect` on `activePane` + a pending-focus ref) — still inside the tap's gesture, so the software keyboard may open; a plain Search-tab tap does NOT focus the box. The pane's `autoFocus` stays: it targets a `display: none` input on phones and never takes focus (measured: `activeElement` is the body on load at 375, on dev and prod) and keeps firing at `md` / `wide` (the box focused on load at 768 and 1440). `SearchPane.add()` refocuses the box after each add as before — the repeated-adds flow: three adds on the phone without leaving Search, the box focused after each.
  - Explicit vs passive: `showCard(card, explicit = false)` — passive (`SearchPane`'s response and arrow previews through `onPreview`, `handleAdd`) sets the preview and flips the tool tab to Card silently at every tier; explicit (`inspectCard`: the search row's click through the new `onInspect` prop, deck rows, grid cards, the leader card, validation chips, dealt cards) also opens the phone's card sheet or the `md` drawer — the only ways either opens. `openCuts` → phone: the Tools pane + the Cuts tab; md: the drawer on Cuts; wide: the inline tab (the stacked page's `scrollIntoView` is gone with the stacked page); pinned by RTL on a loaded 101-card deck at all three tiers.
  - The phone card sheet: a bottom `Drawer` (`swipeDirection="down"`, modal, `showSwipeHandle`, NO snap points — the primitive's `max-h calc(100dvh − 6rem)` plus an inner scroller with `pb-[env(safe-area-inset-bottom)]` fit the card, its text and the tag editor, and a peek state would only add a gesture nobody asked for) rendering a SECOND `CardDetailPane` for `preview` with `tagging`; `DrawerTitle` sr-only (the pane's own `h2` is the visible name — two headings by design, pinned); a 44 px Close control; unmounts when closed. `Drawer.VirtualKeyboardProvider` sits INSIDE the sheet's root — it reads the drawer's store (`useDialogRootContext`) and would throw around the editor root; the viewport consumes it, so the tag input scrolls clear of the software keyboard. Closes on swipe, backdrop press, Escape and Close; measured on the mobile preset: opened by a search row (named "Sol Ring", modal, `data-game="mtg"`, the swipe handle, the tag input, Close 44 × 44), a deck row, the leader ("Atraxa, Praetors' Voice"), a dealt card, and the One Piece leader ("Enel"); closed by Escape, Close and a backdrop press; never opened by typing (the Card panel updated silently), by an add, by `/` (which switches to Search and focuses) or by a tab change; its settled box spans y 98 → 812 at 375 × 812.
  - The `md` tools drawer: `Drawer swipeDirection="right" modal={false} disablePointerDismissal showSwipeHandle` — non-modal with pointer dismissal off, chosen over `'trap-focus'`: a trapped focus would fight every deck-row click, and without `disablePointerDismissal` a non-modal drawer closes on the first outside press. Measured at 1024: opened by the header's Tools button (`hidden md:inline-flex wide:hidden`), a `role="dialog"` named "Tools" (visible title + a Close control), 384 px wide docked at the right edge, `data-modal="false"`, `data-game` on the popup so the strip's indicator and rings take the accent; a deck-row click while open updated the Card tab without closing it. `DrawerContent` gained a `keepMounted` passthrough to `Drawer.Portal`: the popup gets `hidden` when closed and the panels keep their state — Suggestions fetched 3 times before a close, 3 after two close / reopen cycles. At 768 the drawer covers all but 32 px of the 416 px deck pane (a LATER row, below); at 1024–1199 it leaves 288–463 px of the deck visible. No `Drawer.SwipeArea` (it would sit on the deck pane's right edge and could steal a scroll; no trigger, no row).
  - Tools by tier: `wide` and `md` render R3's Tabs exactly as before; the phone's Tools tab renders Analytics and the sample hand ABOVE the tool Tabs (`DeckListPane extras={tier !== "phone"}` drops them from the deck pane there), so a 768 px crossing remounts both and a dealt hand resets — disclosed. The compact attribution (`EditorAttribution`) ends the tools content at every tier. Measured on the phone: Analytics · Sample hand · Card / Suggestions / Combos / Cuts · the attribution (Magic); Analytics · the single "Card" panel · the ©BANDAI line (One Piece).
  - The footer: `SiteFooter` moved from the root layout to the (site) layout (after `{children}`), the new-deck picker branch, the root `not-found.tsx` and `error.tsx`; `global-error.tsx` never had it. `(site)/layout.tsx` still reads no request data; `/c/`, `/l/`, `/cards/[id]` still `●`. Measured on dev: no `<footer` on `/decks/new?game=mtg` or `/decks/<id>/edit`; one each on `/`, `/cards`, `/c/atraxa-praetors-voice`, `/d/q9j9uxphwp9n`, `/account`, the picker (`/decks/new`), `/nonsense` (1 header, 1 footer in the DOM) and `/c/zz-no-such-commander-zz` (flight-encoded in the HTML like its header, 1 header + 1 footer once hydrated); pinned in RTL for the root 404 and the error page (and its absence on the group's 404 body). `EditorAttribution` (`src/components/editor/editor-attribution.tsx`): Magic gets the Scryfall / Fan Content line at `text-[0.65rem]`; One Piece gets `OptcgPostureLine` — the editor joins the ©BANDAI list (P4.1 / P4.6 require it wherever One Piece card images render; the editor was the gap). It branches on `adapter.id === "optcg"` the way the deck share page does (P4.6) — the adapter contract carries no attribution hook and adapters are plain TS.
  - The bottom inset: `EDITOR_BOTTOM_INSET_CLASS` = `[--editor-bottom-inset:calc(3.5rem_+_env(safe-area-inset-bottom))] md:[--editor-bottom-inset:0px]` on the editor root AND on the portaled toast viewport — the viewport renders outside the root, where the root's variable cannot reach (the prompt's `var()` on the viewport alone would have read 0). `EDITOR_TOAST_VIEWPORT_CLASS` adds `bottom-[calc(var(--editor-bottom-inset)_+_1rem)] z-60` (above the `md` drawer's z-50, which docks where toasts land); `AmbientArt` gained `creditInset` (a `style.bottom`, no behavior change) and the editor passes `calc(var(--editor-bottom-inset, 0px) + 0.5rem)`. Measured on the mobile preset: the inset `calc(3.5rem + 0px)` (the pane has no safe-area inset; the class carries `env()`), the toast viewport's bottom 72 px and a toast's bottom at 748 above the bar's top at 755, the credit chip's bottom at 748 (`bottom: 64px`); at 1024 and 1440 the inset is 0 and both sit 16 / 8 px above the viewport's bottom as before.
  - Touch targets: `pointer-coarse:` rules only, so the `wide:` mouse density is byte-identical. Measured on the mobile preset (`(pointer: coarse)` true, Android UA): the bar's triggers 56; search rows 52 with the Add and Commander buttons 48 × 44 and 95 × 44 always visible; deck rows 48 with the steppers and remove 44 × 44 at `opacity: 1` (the hover reveal cannot work on a finger) and the pip column narrowed to `min-w-12` (48 px); segmented items 39 × 44 (a `touch` prop on `Segmented` — the share page passes nothing); Add cards 101 × 44 and 83 × 44; the tool strip's row 56 with a 48 px list and no list padding, so each trigger clears 44 (47 measured); the Analytics trigger 44; the sheet's and the drawer's Close 44 × 44. `DeckTextView` and `LeaderZone` gate their rules on the editable shape, so the read-only share page changes nothing; the sample hand's buttons and the validation chips are untouched (R6's audit and the share-page fence).
  - `viewport`: `{ width: "device-width", initialScale: 1, viewportFit: "cover", interactiveWidget: "resizes-content" }` exported from the two editor `page.tsx` files (page-level; the root layout keeps none, so the (site) pages keep Next's default). `interactive-widget=resizes-content` decided: Chrome for Android then shrinks the layout viewport under the software keyboard, so the `h-dvh` root, the bar and the toast stay above it while typing; iOS ignores the key and the keyboard provider handles the sheet's field there; no conflict — with `resizes-content` the visual viewport equals the layout viewport and the provider's keyboard math is a no-op. Measured: `viewport-fit=cover, interactive-widget=resizes-content` on `/decks/new` (the picker included) and `/decks/<id>/edit`, the default on `/`.
  - Hotkeys: unchanged. `/` still focuses search while the `md` drawer is open (harmless — the drawer is non-modal); `?` is inert inside the sheet and the drawer (`isTextTarget`'s dialog check); `/` from the deck section reached the combobox at 1440, and on a phone it switches to Search and focuses.
  - Budget: 0 DB, 0 API, 0 wire, 0 migrations, 0 dependencies; the editor's request count per action unchanged (search GETs, the one POST, the PUT autosaves, the panels' GETs) plus one refetch per tools panel per tier crossing while its tab is active. Route table: 55 glyph lines before and after, diff empty (`/decks/new` `○`, `/decks/[id]/edit` `ƒ`, the ISR routes `●`). Tests 684 → 704 (94 → 97 files); the same 6 pre-existing warnings.
  - The `md` drawer over the deck at 768 px is the one thing deferred: a LATER row with a trigger (a tablet builder reports it); a narrower `--drawer-content-width` below 900 px or a peek state would be the fix.
  - Wired: drawer (the `md` tools drawer and the phone card sheet, with `Drawer.VirtualKeyboardProvider`). Still installed and unwired: tooltip, skeleton, input, select, toggle (only through toggle-group), scroll-area, sheet, separator, progress, popover, navigation-menu.
  - Environment notes: the hidden browser pane NEVER dispatches `matchMedia` `change` events (a probe listener stayed empty across 1440 → 1024 → 1200 → 375 while `.matches` flipped) — the "update the rendering" step that reports media-query changes is skipped for hidden documents, like rAF — so `useTier` reads correctly only on load there; the CSS half of a live resize was measured without a reload at 1024 / 1199 / 1200 / 375 / 1440 (the grid, the bar, the query "signet" with 12 results, the same input element, the preview, the tool tab, "Saved", one POST throughout), and the JS half (the tools relocating) is pinned by `deck-editor.test.tsx`'s stubbed change event, phone → wide → phone. `window.BASE_UI_ANIMATIONS_DISABLED = true` makes Base UI treat exit animations as finished (the closed drawer's `hidden` settles; without it a reopen from the stalled `data-ending-style` state remounted the content and refetched once), but an ENTER transition still parks a Drawer popup at its closed transform (the `data-starting-style` frame never runs) — remove the attribute to read the settled box. The pane's browser profile does not survive a session gap (localStorage came back empty, the claim token with it). A first hit of a dev route compiles it (search 2.2 s, recommendations 3.8 s) — wait past the 200 ms debounce. In the deck list, the steppers and remove carry `aria-label`s and the name button does not, so `li button` picks a stepper (two fixture cards left the deck that way during the pass — no bug). The mobile preset (375 × 812) emulates a coarse pointer and an Android UA and needs a reload; the tablet preset (768 × 1024) keeps a fine pointer.

- R6 decisions beyond §4/§7 (2026-09-12, `fe0fa40`), pinned by `src/components/surface-skeleton.test.tsx`, `src/components/deck/analytics-blocks.test.tsx`, `src/components/deck/sample-hand.test.tsx`, `src/components/cards/card-search.test.tsx`, `src/components/ui/button.test.tsx`, `src/components/deck/deck-grid-view.test.tsx`, `src/components/deck/leader-zone.test.tsx` (the severity text), `src/lib/games/mtg/analyze.test.ts` (the curve target) and `src/lib/games/optcg/analyze.test.ts` (none on the DON!! curve):
  - Skeleton anatomy (C9): ONE server-safe `SurfaceSkeleton` (`src/components/surface-skeleton.tsx`) parameterized by `kind` (hub / card / deck) and `game`, rendered by the four `loading.tsx` files. It renders the REAL `SurfaceHeader` (the gradient band — the element the page paints before any banner) at `SURFACE_BAND[kind]`, a new export of `surface-header.tsx` that the card page and the share view now read too, so a shell cannot drift from its band; the page's own container and vertical padding; a back-link bar where the page has one (`h-6` — the inline anchor sits in `main`'s 24 px line box; the first cut's `h-5` measured 4 px short); the hero box (`w-72` at 488 / 680 with the page's own `-mt-*` overlap); title bars at the type scale's line heights; NO text (`aria-busy`, `aria-label="Loading"`); and `min-h-dvh`, so the site footer starts below the fold while the page streams — the footer was the ONE layout-shift source measured (0.099 on `/d/` and `/c/` when the short fallback swapped for the tall page; 0 after). `Skeleton` is `motion-safe:animate-pulse`. The editor routes get no shell (client-rendered; "Loading deck…" stays) and neither do the index pages (the list IS the shell). Measured with the fallback HTML (the streamed prefix up to the first `S:` chunk, scripts stripped) in a `srcdoc` iframe beside the live page at 390 and 1440: the band's top and height and the h1's top identical on the deck page (81 / 128 → 225; 81 / 192 → 289), the hubs (129 / 144 → 602; 129 / 224 → 369) and the card page (129 / 112 → 586; 129 / 176 → 321); `/l/` reads 1 px off after the Bandai scan loads (its natural aspect, R1b's design). The streamed proof on dev: every surface page's HTML carries `data-slot="skeleton"` at ~11 kB before the content at ~55 kB, in 4–7 hidden `S:` chunks.
  - The 404 regression the skeletons caused, and its fix. With a `loading.tsx`, a `notFound()` thrown inside the page can no longer set the status — the shell has already streamed as a 200 (Next's `not-found` docs: "the resource has to be checked before the response streams"). Measured: the four unknown ids (`/c/zz-…`, `/l/zz-…`, `/cards/0000…`, `/d/zzz…`) answered 200 on dev while prod (R4 code) answered 404, and `hubs-smoke`'s "unknown slug → 404" and "malformed slug → 404" went red. Fix: a segment `layout.tsx` per route (`c/[slug]`, `l/[slug]`, `cards/[id]`, `d/[publicId]`) that awaits the existence check ABOVE the Suspense boundary through the same React-cached lookup the page and `generateMetadata` use, now in colocated modules (`leader.ts` ×2, `card.ts` — `loadCard` moved out of the card page verbatim —, `deck.ts`), and returns its children untouched. Counted: the hub still runs 8 `[db]` statements, the deck page 6, the card page 6 (React `cache` dedupes the layout's call); the six unknown ids answer 404 again with no skeleton in the body; the real pages still stream the skeleton first; `hubs-smoke` green; ISR untouched (`●` on `/c/`, `/l/`, `/cards/[id]`; `/d/` `ƒ`). A private deck passes through the layout to the page's client gate as before. Layouts are not routes: the table is unchanged.
  - G6's grow-in mechanism: Tailwind's `starting:` variant over `animate-in`. `HISTOGRAM_BAR_MOTION_CLASS` = `origin-bottom scale-y-100 motion-safe:transition-transform motion-safe:duration-400 motion-safe:ease-out motion-safe:starting:scale-y-0` — the bar's height stays inline (an inline `height` cannot be overridden by a `starting:` utility, a scale can), it plays on first style without JavaScript, on the server-rendered share page and the hub's staples curve alike, and it compiles to `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference) { @starting-style { … } }` (verified in the dev CSS), so a reduced-motion reader never gets it; the pane caught a bar mid-flight (`scale: 1 0.9915`). The value label above each bar and `rounded-t-sm` are unchanged. Browsers without `@starting-style` (before 2024) simply skip the grow-in.
  - G9's data shape: `target?: { label: string; values: readonly number[] }` on the histogram block (`types.ts`), attached by `mtg/analyze.ts` as `{ label: "Curve template", values: MTG_CURVE_TEMPLATE }` — one import between two pure adapter modules, pinned as the SAME array `recommend.curve.buckets` reads (one editorial skeleton, three views with the Cut Coach). The renderer draws a dashed `aria-hidden` outline behind each nonzero target bucket and ONE visible legend line ("Curve template" with a swatch), on a max shared by bars and target: Queza's 19-card peak caps the scale and the ghosts read 13 / 19 (68 %); a 2-card draft's bars read 1 / 13 under full-height ghosts — nothing overflows. The DON!! curve carries no target: no outlines, no legend (measured on the phone Tools tab and the One Piece share page). Rejected: reading `recommend.curve` inside the component — the renderer would then know the adapter ("analytics are data, not components"). Not a wire change: `analyze()` runs in-process on both surfaces.
  - F4's prompt: the state machine is `none → dealt → kept`. "Draw sample hand" deals; a dealt hand shows the game prompt — "Keep this hand?" in a `role="group"` with **Keep** (primary) and **Mulligan** (outline); Keep freezes the hand (the same list element, `data-kept`) and the `aria-live` slot reads "Hand kept." or "Hand kept after N mulligan(s)."; Mulligan redraws seven and counts ("After N mulligan(s)"); from a kept hand only "New hand" remains, which restarts at zero mulligans; no draw-next (LATER's goldfish row stands). Every deal bumps a nonce that keys the `<ul>`, so a new hand or a mulligan replays the deal. The stagger: `animation-delay: i × 30 ms` per slot (the seventh card starts at 180 ms) with `motion-safe:animate-in motion-safe:fade-in-0 motion-safe:slide-in-from-bottom-2 motion-safe:fill-mode-backwards motion-safe:duration-200` — the whole deal ends at 380 ms, inside §1's 400; under reduced motion (read AT the deal by `prefersReducedMotion()`, the new `src/lib/theme/motion.ts`, never during render) no delay is written and the cards simply appear. Measured on the share page: delays 0 … 180 ms, computed `enter / backwards / 0.2s`; the same widget on the share page and the phone's Tools tab (R4's placement); a seeded draft with no main deck still renders nothing (an empty library). The three buttons are 44 px on coarse pointers through the Button base rule.
  - `/cards`: `<fieldset>` + `<legend>` groups — Name · Type · Color identity (Magic; One Piece says Colors) · Traits (One Piece) — with every control labelled (`aria-label` "Card name" / "Card type" / "Trait"); the selected filters as a chip row (`ul` "Active filters", each a button "Remove filter: {Group} {Value}" with an X) plus a Clear all (`xs` ghost); a skeleton grid (ten `Skeleton` boxes at 488 / 680 in the same grid classes, `aria-hidden`) while the FIRST page is in flight — a re-search keeps the previous results under the live count, and Load more keeps its own "Loading…" label; the URL params still land preset from hub links (measured `/cards?game=optcg&color=within:RG&type=Character&traits=Straw Hat Crew` → chips Type Character · Color Red · Color Green · Trait Straw Hat Crew, 126 cards; `/cards?q=sol` → Name sol); the canonicals and the Scryfall / ©BANDAI lines intact (`seo` and `optcg` smokes green). `autoFocus` kept: a page whose purpose is search focuses its box, and phone browsers do not raise the keyboard for `autofocus` without a gesture. The test stubs `fetch` under fake timers.
  - Touch targets: ONE base rule in `buttonVariants` — the text sizes gain `pointer-coarse:min-h-11 pointer-coarse:min-w-11`, the icon sizes `pointer-coarse:size-11` — so every Button on every surface (the dialogs, forms and panels the signed-out pane cannot open included) clears 44 px on a coarse pointer while the mouse density is byte-identical; R4's per-site rules stay (harmless). The owner's open looks stay untold: the `162.252.x.x` get-session rows (03:31–04:10 Z) coincide with R4's prod pass from the pane on the owner's machine (the same address minted the 09-07 account deck), and the `24.10.x.x` rows end at 02:01 Z with the 01:58 Z account deck on R5b code — no evidence of the owner opening R4 or R6 code yet.

## 7. Acceptance checks and boundaries

### Functional checks

- Opening the builder, changing theme or appearance, and changing tabs create no server deck and no dirty state.
- The first real edit still creates exactly one deck.
- Keyboard additions, quantity changes, import and export, autosave retry, sharing, history restore, and leader replace with Undo retain their behavior.
- Mobile tab changes and responsive transitions preserve editor state.
- Magic and One Piece keep their own terminology, counts, validation, and tools; the One Piece single-panel tool pane renders correctly.
- Private decks and owner-only information stay protected across headers and deck collections.
- Every former `Modal` traps focus, closes on Escape, and restores focus; `/` and `?` are inert while a dialog is open.
- `.don-cost` renders on every cost surface. One Piece OG unfurls stay artless.

### Artwork checks

Cover no leader, one leader, partner leaders, leader replacement and removal, a selected printing, double-faced cards, missing artist metadata, failed image loads, and rapid selection changes. A stale request must never replace the latest leader's artwork. The attribution chip is visible whenever art_crop is on screen. One Piece never requests art while its adapter declares none.

### Visual and accessibility checks

Review at 390, 768, 1024, 1200, 1440, and 1920 px with long names, populated and empty decks, and both themes. Verify keyboard access, dialog focus, touch targets of at least 44 px, that reduced motion disables every decorative animation, text contrast per §1, and status indicators that never rely on color alone. Artwork and thumbnails never shift content (explicit width and height) and never block editing.

Run `pnpm check` for every package. Focused tests: tokens against CSS, the hotkey dialog guard, the search-state reducer, `thumbnailUrl`, the art resolver and stale guard, `colorSwatches`, and the responsive tab container's mount and preserve behavior. React Testing Library is installed and `src/lib/seo/jsonld.test.tsx` is the precedent. Visual review covers styling.

**Boundaries:** keep Next.js, Tailwind, Base UI and shadcn, the database, and the hosting approach. Preserve public URLs and guest building. No Vercel-proprietary SDKs, no image optimizer, no stored image URLs or artist column (Neon budget), and premium never gates card data. This redesign improves presentation and interaction; collection management, recommendation systems, and additional games stay under their own plans.

## Addendum — Wave-2 supersessions (added 2026-09-19, W1 step 0)

The W-series (`WAVE2.md`, build plan §6c) re-keys the brand this contract was written on. Recorded here the way §6 records R-series decisions — the sections above stay as written; this addendum says which of their statements no longer hold:

- **W1 supersedes §1's "The brand is the indigo already in `src/app/icon.svg`."** The brand is now the crest palette: forest green `#23483c`, gold (`#c9a96a` dark / `#ba9b61` light), charcoal `#242c2f`, ivory `#f7f4ee`. Gold is the dark-theme brand text/focus color; green is the action fill (light-theme brand text); the primary button's gold hairline is structural, not decorative — the green measures 1.84:1 on the dark page and cannot stand alone as a fill. §1's token tables describe the R-series state; `WAVE2.md` D0 is the live table, mirrored as ever by `src/app/globals.css` + `src/lib/theme/tokens.ts` under `tokens.test.ts`.
- **W1 supersedes §1's "Typography stays Geist and Geist Mono."** Literata (variable, via `next/font/google`, token `--font-display`) carries the wordmark, page/section titles on site pages, empty-state titles and the Warden lines. Body, builder, controls and data stay Geist / Geist Mono; the type scale's sizes are unchanged. `--font-heading` stays sans in W1 (it feeds dialog/sheet/drawer titles the builder opens); whether modal titles go serif is W2's recorded decision.
- **W1 supersedes the `BrandMark` docblock** ("the indigo shield is the brand"): the mark is now the crest — three forest-green cards with gold outlines fanned behind a charcoal shield with a gold border and downward sword — with a `shield` variant for ≤ 20 px (tab icon, Warden line). Colors stay literal so the mark is identical in both themes; on the dark page the charcoal shield reads by its gold border, by design.
- **W4 supersedes §2's "An empty leader zone shows Choose commander / leader, which focuses search."** The primary action becomes a link to the browse page (`/commanders`, `/leaders`, via the adapter's `display.leaderBrowse`) with a sessionStorage pick-intent way back for saved decks; "Search by name" stays as the secondary action (today's focusSearch). Recorded at W1 step 0, effective since W4 (`077401a`, 2026-09-19).

### W1 deviations from `WAVE2.md` D0 (measured ratios)

**None — D0 shipped verbatim (2026-09-19, `e40342d`).** The four R6 light-theme fixes were re-measured on the new ivory surfaces and all hold with no changes: placeholders (`--muted-foreground` `#565e5b`) 6.08:1 on `#f7f4ee` / 6.56:1 on card; inactive tab triggers 5.62:1 on muted `#f0ebe1`; the banner credit 13.17:1 at its worst case (85 % ivory over white art); the amber legality chip (`text-amber-800` on `amber-500/15`) 5.83:1 light / 8.68:1 dark. Also measured: the gold hairline ≈ 3.01:1 against the primary green fill (non-text boundary, ≥ 3:1); focus rings at 50 % — gold 2.95:1 dark, green 2.57:1 light; the emerald Warden line 5.0:1 light / 9.72:1 dark. `tracking-tight` kept on every serif title (nothing read cramped in the dev pass).

### W2 audit + decisions (2026-09-19, `ce9d7c2`)

**Serif modal titles: NO.** `--font-heading` stays `var(--font-sans)` and the tokens.test pin stays where W1 put it. Compared side by side on the editor's Import dialog: at the 14 px/600 modal-title scale Literata's serifs don't resolve — the title reads fussier, not more branded — and dialog/sheet/drawer titles are the builder's working chrome, which stays Geist by D0's own rule ("controls and data are sans").

**Both-theme contrast audit** — WCAG formula in the pane (canvas-parsed computed colors, alpha composited over the real ancestor stack, large-text ≥ 24 px or ≥ 18.66 px/700, disabled controls exempt), at 390/768/1200/1440 on the editors and 390 + 1440 elsewhere. **Zero failing text pairs.** Worst measured pair per surface (dark / light): MTG + OP editors 6.76 / 5.89 (all four widths byte-identical pairs); Import dialog pass / pass (dark 3.61 is the *disabled* Next button, exempt); home 7.78 / 6.08; `/cards` 8.46 / 6.08; card page 7.16 / 5.74; `/c/` hub 7.16 / 6.08; `/l/` hub 8.46 / 6.08; `/d/` OP share 8.46 / 4.89; `/d/` MTG share 7.22 / 4.89; `/account` (guest) 8.46 / 6.08. Re-measured thin pairs, unchanged and passing: amber-700 on ivory 4.58:1 (4.95 on light card); amber-800 on the tint 6.4:1; amber-400 dark 15.2–16.5:1. Focus rings at 50 % re-confirmed: gold 2.95 dark, green 2.57 light, lavender 2.98, teal 3.46 — non-text indicators, 3 px, paired with `focus-visible:border-ring` at full opacity on buttons; `:focus-visible` verified firing with the ring painted in both themes. Art-underlay pairs (banner credit) keep R6's worst-case numbers. **Audit gotcha for the next pass:** a hidden Browser pane freezes CSS transition clocks, so a theme-class flip never finishes compositing and reads garbage mid-transition ratios (1.0–2.0) no matter how long you wait — inject `*{transition:none!important}` before measuring.

**Generic band ramp:** green→charcoal shipped at 45/15/35 alphas (via stop 55 %) — deliberately heavier than the accent bands' 25/10/5 because the crest green is itself dark; side-by-side in the pane, 30/10/20 read as unbranded murk and 55/20/45 overweighted the light theme.

## Addendum — Wave-3 supersessions (added 2026-09-27, X1 step 0)

The X-series (`WAVE3.md`, build plan §6d) changes three recorded decisions about the header and one about the hub filter. As with the Wave-2 addendum, the sections above and `WAVE2.md` stay as written; this addendum says which of their statements no longer hold:

- **X1 supersedes §2's "'My decks' leads to `/account` for signed-in users and to the browser's guest-deck section on home for guests."** My decks is `/account` for everyone. Signed out, that page is the sign-in page with this browser's decks listed under the buttons (owner's answer, 2026-09-27). The nav reads no session any more, so the link is the same string in the server HTML and after hydration; only the account slot reads the session. Routes and guest access are still unchanged.
- **X1 supersedes §6's note that `id="your-decks"` is "the header's guest 'My decks' target since R1b".** Home keeps the section and both components keep the id; it is content, no longer a nav target. No link in `src/` points at `/#your-decks`.
- **X1 supersedes `WAVE2.md` D1's "Bobandis6 ← label, not an item."** The name is the menu's first item: a link to `/account` that lands at the very top of the page (owner's idea 6). The four section links below it are unchanged.
- **X1 supersedes `WAVE2.md` D1's "guests need it for `/#your-decks`."** My decks stays in the top nav for everyone; a guest's target is `/account`.
- **X2 supersedes W4's `LIKE` filter on `/commanders` and `/leaders`** (`nameNormLikeCondition` in `src/lib/hub/queries.ts`, deleted): the filter reads the shared name matcher (`src/lib/search/name-match.ts`, `WAVE3.md` D2), so `atraxa praetors` and `kiki jiki` find their commanders, and a filtered list is ranked — best match first, then play — instead of play alone. Recorded at X1 step 0, effective since X2 (`d88c70e`, 2026-09-27). The filter box itself is now the predictive island inside the same GET form; its label, placeholder and hidden `colors` input are unchanged.

### X1 decisions and deviations from `WAVE3.md` D1 (2026-09-27, `e42d6ad`)

D1 shipped as drawn. What it left to the package, and what was decided:

- **The name row's mechanism: `href="/account"` plus a scroll to the top in the same click** (`landAtTop` in `account-slot.tsx`). Measured in a real browser on Next 16.3.2, on a signed-out page standing in for `/account`: after a plain-anchor hash (`history.state` null — the router never hears of it) a `Link` to the path you are on clears the hash and lands at `scrollY` 0, and does so again on a second click from 1,500 — but from `scrollY` 40 it stays at 40, because Next scrolls only when the page's top edge is out of view. The header is not sticky (`position: static`, 57 px), so whoever opens the account menu has the header on screen: exactly the range in which the link alone does nothing. `/account#top`, the other candidate, lands at 0 once and then fails the second click in a row (1,500 stayed 1,500) and the 40 case. The row therefore scrolls on every primary click, `behavior: "instant"`; a modified or middle click opens a tab and leaves the page where it is.
- **The name row's text: the name alone.** No "Account" hint.
- **The island's words: the contract's, with a singular.** "On this browser · 2 decks" / "Sign in to keep them on every device."; one deck reads "1 deck" / "keep it". The count is what the server verified, not what the browser holds. Tiles are home's (`BrowserDeckTiles`), two columns at most at the reading width.
- **The return is automatic**, once the claim settles; a claim that fails or claims nothing returns anyway (the next visit to `/account` retries); the history entry is replaced. The status line reads "Signed in — taking you back…" with a "Go now" link.
- **Signed in with a valid `next`, `/account` renders only the claim step.** Not in D1: the visitor is about to leave, so the account's sections and their six queries are skipped. The claim still runs first — that is the reason the return passes through `/account`.
- **`next` may be any safe root-relative path** except `/account` and `/api/` — no list of allowed prefixes. `safeNextPath` refuses `//` anywhere, a backslash, control characters, the same things in percent-encoded form, and anything over 200 characters; an invalid value is ignored silently.
- **Which prompts send `next`:** Like, Bookmark and Fork. The header's Sign in, home's copy, the editor's collection hint and the `/u/` and `/f/` prompts stay plain `/account`.

### X2 decisions and deviations from `WAVE3.md` D0 and D2 (2026-09-27, `d88c70e`)

D2 shipped as drawn — the measured `atr` dropdown on `/commanders` is D2's sketch row for row. What it left to the package, and what was decided:

- **The order stays prefix-first** (the owner's answer, 2026-09-27): `ring` offers Rings of Brighthearth before Sol Ring. Inside a class: most played, then leaders, then the name, then the card number — one order for both games; "leaders first" decides only One Piece rows and Magic's unranked ones.
- **A word starts after a space, a hyphen, a period, a double quote or an opening parenthesis** — wider than D2's space-or-hyphen, measured: 273 One Piece names join words with a period (Monkey.D.Luffy), and under D2's rule none of the 17 Luffys reached the `/leaders` dropdown.
- **Nothing is highlighted until an arrow key, literally**: Base UI's hover highlight is off; hover is a CSS tint only, so a resting pointer never arms Enter.
- **A pick navigates and never writes into the box**: every row is a link (no prefetch); the one non-link row is the footer, which submits the form.
- **The footer row** ("Show every commander matching “…” ↵") sits under a border, not a separator element (a listbox owns options only), and wraps at the 18 rem floor. Its list is always a superset of the dropdown: the filter accepts classes 1–4 and, from four characters, near misses.
- **Enter-to-submit needed a default button.** Base UI's Root renders a second, unnamed text input beside the visible one; HTML implicit submission ignores Enter in a form with two text fields and no submit button — with or without script. The island renders an invisible default submit button whenever it is a named form field. Found in the pane (jsdom has no implicit submission).
- **Esc** closes and keeps the text; a second Esc with the popup shut clears the box, as a search box always did.
- **`/cards`**: the Name box is the island, still controlled by the grid's `q` and still `autoFocus`; its input keeps the field class the Type and Traits controls share (the primitive's `unstyled` escape). A typed name asks the grid for `sort=best`, the dropdown's order, so the first tile is the first suggestion; the grid's WHERE is the translator's, untouched.
- **Rows**: a 26 × 36 thumbnail (the `small` rendition; One Piece keeps a same-width spacer while `thumbnailUrl()` returns null), the name, and one quiet detail — color chips on `/commanders`, the card number on `/leaders` and One Piece `/cards`, the type line on Magic `/cards`.
- **Width**: the popup is the input's width (`--anchor-width`), never under 18 rem, never wider than the viewport allows — 358 px under the 358 px phone box, 288 px under the 224 px desktop index box.

### X3 decisions and deviations from `WAVE3.md` D3 (2026-09-28, `69636a4`)

D3 shipped as drawn — the dev pane's sheet for Kiki-Jiki, Mirror Breaker + Zealous Conscripts is D3's sketch row for row: "Build around this combo", the lead naming the combo, "Combo pieces · 1 — always kept", "Add 98 cards". What it left to the package, and what was decided:

- **One seeder per draft, one precedence rule: `from` > `surprise` > `combo` > `leader`**, decided in the editor (`draftSeed`), not the chooser — the chooser now passes every latched seed through. The first kind present runs alone and alone settles the `?autofill=1` latch. The race the contract warned about was reproduced in RTL first: before X3 the sheet opened on the plain leader seed with `keep: []`. One Piece, which declares no combos, reads `combo` as noise — its leader seeds as if the parameter were absent, with no toast.
- **The door** is a link styled as an extra-small outline button (`buttonVariants` on a plain `Link`, no prefetch — up to ten per page), right-aligned on each row's "How it works" line and wrapping under it on a phone (154 × 44 px under a coarse pointer, 154 × 24 on a mouse). **No icon**: D0's "reuses Autofill's `WandSparkles`" had no referent — no door on the site renders that icon — and ten identical glyphs down one list would be noise beside the hub's two icon-less build buttons. Its gate is the build CTA's (a legal commander, an adapter that declares autofill) inside the combo section's own; a not-legal commander's hub (Spock, Logical Choice) lists its combos with no door.
- **The route**: `GET /api/combos/[key]` → `{ combo, cards }`, the cards in the pieces' name order (the route re-orders `loadCardWires`' database order), with Commander legality — the only combo source is Commander Spellbook. Three statements; the lookup is one probe of the external-key UNIQUE index, 0.05–0.08 ms warm (planning ≈ 3.5 ms). An unknown key is a **404 that carries the hour-long cache header** — deliberately unlike `cards/[id]/combos`' empty 200: a Spellbook key probes nothing private, the seeder must tell a gone combo from an empty one, and the miss caches like a hit (prod: MISS, then HIT, for both). A combo holding a removed piece answers the same 404 (none exist today). A key off `^[0-9]+(-{1,2}[0-9]+)*$`, or longer than 64 characters, is a 400 before any statement.
- **The toasts**, in the unknown-`from=` voice: "Couldn't find that combo — starting an empty deck instead." (an unknown or deleted key, or a failed request) · "That combo link names no commander — starting an empty deck instead." (no request is made) · "That combo doesn't include that commander — starting an empty deck instead." (the named commander is not a piece, or is a piece that cannot be a commander). With `?autofill=1` a failed seed still opens the sheet at its no-commander sentence — W9c's pinned degradation, which spends no POST (LATER row 126).
- **The pinned context lives in the editor** (`autofillPin`, a `ComboPin` built by `comboPin()` in `src/lib/combos/view.ts`). The combo seeder sets it only when `?autofill=1` will open a sheet; the Radar's buttons set their own; closing the sheet clears it; the empty-deck door and More → Autofill… (a door the contract did not list, found in the pane) always open the plain sheet. The pin holds every piece id, the commander included; the sheet keeps leader-zone ids out of `keep` and out of its counts.
- **The sheet with a pin**: the title "Build around this combo"; the lead "{pieces}. The rest comes from real play data — every pick shows why."; the "Combo pieces · N" group first — no checkbox, "always kept", each row "✓ name · Combo piece · $price"; "Keep my N cards" counting and governing only the other cards; and, for a combo with a non-card requirement, the Radar's amber "Also needs …" line above "A starter shell, not a tuned list". The ✓ is the Radar's emerald, not `text-primary`: measured 1.49:1 on the dark sheet, now 7.82:1 dark and 5.36:1 light.
- **"Suggest full list"** (the owner kept the default, 2026-09-28), titled "Keeps these pieces and suggests the rest of the deck", on "With your commander", "In your deck" and "One card away" — the last adds its missing piece first, through one resolve by external key. The adds stay real edits: on dev, Cancel left Repercussion in the deck (101 cards, saved).
- **A kept piece outside the commander's colors is not refused**: the planner answers 200 and `issues` carries a `COLOR_IDENTITY` error naming it (the real planner, `smoke:autofill`). The door never offers one — the hub list is fit-filtered.

### X4a decisions and deviations from `WAVE3.md` D0 and D4 (2026-09-28, `335576c`)

D4 shipped as the owner's answers of 2026-09-28 amended it: `/cards?set=emn` reads "Eldritch Moon — the 71st expansion set", then "Most played in Eldritch Moon" (12 tiles, Geier Reach Sanitarium first that day), then "All 208 cards, in collector-number order". What it left to the package, and what was decided:

- **The filter is its own kind, not a `FieldTarget`.** `SearchFieldDef` gains `{ kind: "set", target: { printing: "set_code" } }` (a `SetTarget` type), so no `"column" in` branch had to learn a third shape. The translator emits a bound `EXISTS` over the card's live printings in one released paper set, lowercases the code, refuses a code off `^[a-z0-9]{2,12}$` with a warning and a constant FALSE — the one malformed value that scopes to nothing instead of being dropped, since dropping it would list every card under the chosen set — returns an optional `scope`, and its switch now ends in an exhaustive `never` default. Magic declares the field; One Piece declares none, so its `set=` changes the answer by not one byte.
- **"Released" is one SQL fragment** (`releasedPaperSet`, `src/lib/sets/sql.ts`): the set's own date ≤ today in UTC — pinned in the SQL, not left to the session's zone — and not digital-only. The scope, the list and the empty-answer probe all read it, so the picker never offers a set the scope refuses, and the URL takes only what the picker offers: an upcoming, digital-only or unknown code is an honest empty answer with a warning (`set: "fra" is not released yet`, `set: "hbg" is a digital-only set`, `set: no set has the code "zzz"`).
- **The in-set printing is the lowest collector number, by one rule** (`collectorNumberOrder`: the leading integer, none last; then the full text; then the printing id). The route LEFT JOINs a `DISTINCT ON` table of the set's printings where it otherwise joins the default printing; the translator's `EXISTS` alone decides which cards are listed. Measured warm through the route: Bloomburrow 2.8–3.2 ms, Secret Lair Drop 17 ms, The List's 5,584 printings 34–43 ms (planning 6–10 ms) — against 170 ms for the unscoped first page. A per-card LATERAL pick was measured and refused: 280 ms on Secret Lair Drop, its probe ANDing a bitmap of the whole set for every card.
- **The wire and the orders.** A scoped row carries `printingId` (its last key); an unscoped row does not, so every unscoped response stayed byte-identical — eight saved responses, dev and prod, before and after. `sort=number` orders by the in-set printing, `dir` ignored; without a set it falls back to the default order, like `best` without a name. The route's own default is unchanged when scoped; `/cards` asks for `sort=number` with a set and no name, `sort=best` with a name, and `sort=pop&limit=12` for the strip.
- **Statements**: a scoped request is one (two with `format`); an empty scoped answer adds one probe of `sets_game_code` to word its warning; a malformed code is one statement that joins nothing. `GET /api/sets` is one aggregate (135–145 ms warm, edge-cached a day, MISS → HIT on prod), none for One Piece.
- **The picker is a text box, not D4's ▾ button** — the owner types the set's name there, the Name box beside it is a text box, and it stays one tab stop; the ▾ is drawn inside the box. It opens on a press, typing or ↓, at zero characters too — the list is local, and D0's two-character rule exists for the suggest endpoint. Rows are grouped "Main sets" / "Other products", best match first (the exact code, then name starts, word starts, code starts, name contains; newest first inside each class), at most 50 a group with "Showing N of M — keep typing to narrow." under them — all 706 rows took ~210 ms to open in dev, and one typed letter still matches ~500 sets. The popup is ~8 rows tall and scrolls (D0's "at most 8 rows" as a height), as wide as the box and never under 18 rem; rows are 44 px on coarse pointers. A row: the name, "2016 · 71st expansion set", the code, and a ✓ on the chosen set. The box shows the chosen set's name; typing filters; leaving the box, a press outside or a pick puts the chosen name back; Esc and a bare Enter keep the text. The scope changes only by a pick, the chip's ×, or Clear all.
- **One source for the chip and the header**: `GET /api/sets`, fetched the first time the picker opens, or at mount when `/cards?set=` arrives preset — mount with no preset set still makes exactly one request. The chip reads "Set: Eldritch Moon (EMN)", the code alone until the list lands; a code the list does not hold heads the grid with "No released set has the code “ZZZ”." and the count "0 cards".
- **The words of a place.** Numbered lines: "the 71st expansion set", "the 21st Commander set", "the 13th draft innovation set", and masters as "the 13th reprint set" — Scryfall's own definition of the type, and the only word true of all of it (Chronicles, Renaissance and The List are masters-typed; none is a Masters set). Core sets carry no number ("a core set"; Tenth Edition would read 12th). The added main kinds: "a Universes Beyond Eternal set", "a bonus sheet", and Secret Lair Drop "the Secret Lair series". Other products name their type ("a promo set", "a boxed set", "an Un-set or funny promo set", …; a type Scryfall adds later reads as its own words). A set skipped by the same-day rule has no number ("an expansion set"). The numbering is TypeScript (`lineOrdinals`, pinned against the real expansion line) and `smoke:sets` recomputes Eldritch Moon's place in SQL independently (a line's distinct release days up to the set).
- **A day's sets by line**: newest first, and on one day the expansion leads its Commander decks, then the rest — Marvel Super Heroes before Marvel Super Heroes Commander, which has 616 cards to its 281.
- **"Most played in {set}"**: 12 tiles by EDHREC rank, unranked cards dropped (a rank is what "most played" claims), hidden while a name is typed and when the whole list fits in 12, captioned "Ranked by EDHREC play data via Scryfall." — the staples table's source line. A denser grid than the list: 4 columns on a phone, 6 from `sm`, all 12 in one row from `lg`.
- **The count line** in the set view reads "All 208 cards, in collector-number order" ("84 cards, in collector-number order" beside another filter; "0 cards" when nothing matched).
- **The Name box's dropdown stays unscoped** while a set scopes the grid — a suggestion is "go to this card" (LATER row 124).
- **Every tile links its in-set printing**, `/cards/<id>?printing=<printingId>`, strip included; the card page lands on it after hydration (one tail fetch for a printing at 101–250 of the card's printings). Past the gallery API's 250 cap — the five basic lands of 177 older sets — the page falls back to the default printing (LATER row 88).
- **No `Layers` icon**: D0 names it for sets, but X4a's surfaces carry text labels (the "Set" legend, the chip); X4b's Browse entry and page are its natural home.

### X4b decisions and deviations from `WAVE3.md` D4 (2026-09-28, `879db10`)

D4's `/sets` sketch shipped as the owner's answers of 2026-09-28 amended it — the owner chose to build the page ("a page option when you click the browse tab to view every set if someone doesn't know what sets they are"), with main sets first and one click for all, and kept all four of X4a's place-word defaults. What it left to the package, and what was decided:

- **The row names its place in its line, not D4's type word**: "The Hobbit · HOB · 114th expansion set · Aug 14, 2026 · 193 cards", one link to `/cards?set=hob`. `setPlaceShort` is the picker's and the `/cards` header's vocabulary, so the three surfaces say one thing. From `md` the row is one line of five aligned columns (the name, then fixed widths for the code, the place, the date and the count); below it, two lines — the name and the count, then "HOB · 114th expansion set · Aug 14, 2026". The phone line's dots are CSS (`content: '·' / ''`), silent to screen readers and gone from `md`. The code is lowercase in the markup and uppercase by CSS, as in the picker. No arrow and no icon on the row: the whole row is the link, with a hover tint and a focus ring; 44 px and more on coarse pointers.
- **Years**: `h2`s under the serif `h1` "Sets", newest first (34 of them, 2026 → 1993), in the precons' group-heading style; inside a year, the list's own order (newest first, a day's sets by line). A year with nothing to show is hidden with its rows.
- **Rows reach the island as props.** Correction 3 offered server-rendered rows with an island that only hides them as the lighter choice; measured, it is the heavier one — the App Router repeats a server component's rendered markup in the RSC payload (on `/tournaments` every row's class string appears in both the HTML and the payload), while a client list ships the markup once and only the row data in the payload (`/sets`: 142 KB of payload, 0 of 706 class strings). The page: 712,289 B raw, 43,666 B gzip, 41,908 B brotli on prod; the markup is ~800 B a row (LATER row 134).
- **The first paint is the default view.** The island's own initial state — "Main sets only" on — is what the server renders: every row is in the HTML, the 465 other products `hidden` (Tailwind's preflight makes `hidden` win over the row's `flex`), so nothing collapses on hydration and the two renders cannot differ; no hydration warning on a clean load, dev or prod. Without JavaScript the box and the checkbox do nothing (LATER row 135); crawlers get every link either way.
- **The box matches like the picker and keeps the page's order.** `matchSets` decides what shows (the exact code, the name's start, a word's start, the code's start, the name anywhere; one normalizer); its ranking is ignored — a year-grouped page keeps its years, the `/precons` behavior. `blo` shows Bloomburrow and Bloomburrow Commander, and with the box unticked its promo and token sets too; `blb` shows Bloomburrow alone; `moon` shows Eldritch Moon.
- **The words around it**: the lead "Every released Magic set, newest first. Pick one to see its cards." (D4's); the placeholder "Filter sets…" with the label "Filter sets by name or code"; the checkbox "Main sets only"; a `role="status"` line, "Showing 241 of 706 sets" / "Showing all 706 sets". Two empty states: "No sets match" with "Clear filter", and — when only other products match while the box is ticked — "No main sets match" with "Show N other products", which unticks it. A source line under the list: "Set names, types and release dates via Scryfall."
- **Typing is deferred, rows are memoized**: the box answers each keystroke at once and the list re-decides `hidden` in a render the next keystroke may interrupt (`useDeferredValue`, a memoized year list, a memoized row). Measured with the Event Timing API: dev's input handlers went from 53–71 ms to 2–5 ms; on prod, 1–2 ms per keystroke and no long task.
- **The game line**: Magic as the current page and a disabled "One Piece — soon" pill with "One Piece sets are coming." — the `/precons` markup, not `GameSwitch` (there is no second page), in a `role="group"` named "Game" (LATER row 136 for `/precons`' unnamed one).
- **No `Layers` icon**: every browse index's heading, the Browse menu and the empty states' crest are text and the brand mark; one glyph on one heading would be the odd one out. D0's icon for sets stays unused after X4a and X4b.
- **Metadata**: title "Sets", a description naming Magic sets, `alternates.canonical: "/sets"`. The site has no default OG image — the page inherits the root layout's Open Graph block, as `/precons` does.
- **The Browse entry** is "Sets", right after "Cards", in both menus (`BROWSE_LINKS`). Base UI renders a menu's items only when it opens, so no page's server HTML carries the header's Browse links; `smoke:sets` proves the entry from the header's JS (LATER row 133), and crawlers find `/sets` through the sitemap, where it sits beside `/cards`.
- **Caching**: `revalidate = 86400`, no request data — `loadReleasedSets` runs at build and at each daily revalidation, zero statements per visit; a new set joins the page up to a day after its date (LATER row 130). A row's click is a route change, so `/cards` mounts fresh on the chosen set (LATER row 129 does not fire from `/sets`).

### X5 decisions and deviations from `WAVE3.md` D5 (2026-10-01, `8ead5db`)

D5's change picture shipped as drawn, with the owner's two answers of 2026-10-01: the sign-in refresh changes **the picture only**, and card art's credit is **also one quiet line in the account menu**. What the contract left open, and what was decided:

- **The refresh is Better Auth's switch plus a narrowing hook.** `overrideUserInfoOnSignIn` on both providers rewrites the name, the email and the picture together (proven: `sign-in-refresh.test.ts` (b)), so `databaseHooks.user.update.before` (`narrowSignInRefresh`, `src/lib/auth/user-fields.ts`) drops the name, the email and its verified flag from the one write that carries `image` on `/callback/:id`. Every other user update passes untouched, including the callback's own emailVerified-only write for the same email. The email therefore never moves, and the collision (a provider email equal to another user's, a UNIQUE violation that would make the sign-in throw) cannot happen. Breaking the hook's path check fails both (c) tests.
- **The stored shape**: NULL = the provider picture · `{ kind: "initial" }` · `{ kind: "art", printingId, cardName, artist }`. No URL. `parseAvatarChoice` reads anything unrecognised as NULL, so a malformed row renders as the provider picture, never as an error.
- **A stale art choice never renders broken**: `UserAvatar` tries its sources in order (the derived crop, then the provider picture) and moves on when a load fails, with the initial under both. The credit line follows the stored choice rather than the image that loaded (LATER row 137).
- **The derived crop is the API's crop.** `art.ts`' rule ("source of truth is the API, never a derived CDN URL") gains one reconciled exception, written into its docblock: the picture captures the artist from the API at Save, so a render (the header is a client island on every page) derives the crop from the printing id with no API call. Measured on 2026-10-01: Sol Ring, Delver of Secrets and Fable of the Mirror-Breaker. Each derived URL equals `image_uris.art_crop` minus its `?` stamp, and the two double-faced cards take the front face's.
- **One null, two meanings (Correction 6)**: `lookupScryfallArtMeta` returns `{ meta }` or `{ reason: "no-art" | "unreachable" }` (a 404 is no-art; any other non-OK answer, or a throw, is unreachable). `fetchScryfallArtMeta` is now a wrapper over it and returns exactly what it returned before, so the OG images, the hub banners, the art endpoint and the ambient layer did not move.
- **The route's refusals, word for word**: an unknown or removed card → 404 "That card isn't in the database any more. Choose another card."; One Piece (no declared ambient art) → 400 "Only Magic cards can be your picture."; no live default printing → 404 "That card has no printing to take art from. Choose another card."; no artist → 422 "Scryfall lists no artist for this card's art, and art is only shown with its artist. Choose another card."; Scryfall unreachable → 503 "Scryfall didn't answer, so the art couldn't be checked. Your picture hasn't changed — try again in a minute."; signed out → 401 "Sign in to change your picture." The card and its live default printing are one statement (a LEFT JOIN; `loadDefaultPrinting` does not filter removed printings, so the route does not use it).
- **The limiter**: `profileAvatar`, `profile-avatar:user:<id>`, 20 an hour, after the session and before the body. It is a separate bucket, so picture saves never spend username changes.
- **The card box is a pick mode on `NameSuggest`** (Correction 4): with `onPick` in place of `rowHref`, rows are plain options, and a click or Enter on a highlighted row hands the row over. X2's link mode is unchanged and its 16 tests stayed green untouched. In the dialog, an Enter with no row highlighted does nothing (it would otherwise press Save). Proven in the pane: the popup paints over the modal, and a real click on a row picks without closing the dialog.
- **The preview**: a newly picked card previews as its small full-card image (from the suggest row; the frame carries its own artist line). The crop appears only after Save, because the suggest row carries no printing (LATER row 138). Wherever the saved crop shows in the dialog, its full credit sits beside it, wrapped and never truncated, so the © always shows.
- **The first choice's name (Correction 9)**: "Discord picture" or "Google picture" with one linked provider, "Your sign-in picture" with both. Its refresh is "Refresh now" with one provider, "Refresh from Discord" / "Refresh from Google" with two. Each is the `/sign-in/social` round trip back to `/account` (Better Auth's limit of 3 sign-ins per 10 s per IP applies, and each use leaves a session row that expires in 7 days — LATER row 139).
- **The pencil**: a real `<button aria-label="Change picture">` around the 48 px picture (≥ 44 px target); the `Pencil` badge is `aria-hidden`, at opacity 0 until hover or focus-visible, and always visible on a coarse pointer (measured at 375 px: opacity 1).
- **After Save**: `refetch({ query: { disableCookieCache: true } })`, then `router.refresh()`. The refetch updates the shared session atom (the header changes at once) and re-issues the cache cookie (proven in (e); `disableRefresh` would not). `/account` reads `image` and `avatar` from the users row, as it already read `username`, so the refreshed page is right even while the cookie is stale. Other tabs and devices catch up within the cache's 5 minutes (LATER row 140).
- **The header**: `UserAvatar` at 24 px (`size-6`, `text-xs` fallback), so the initial is the first code point in all three places (LATER row 110 fired). The credit is a `<p>` right after the name row: text, not a menu item, so the arrow keys skip it. "Public profile ↗" comes after "Profile & settings" once the session carries a username (REC-5, LATER row 80 fired). The username form refetches the same way.
- **The privacy page** now says the avatar URL refreshes at each sign-in and the name and email do not, and lists what a picture choice stores (the card's name, the printing, the artist; or the initial). Nothing is uploaded.

### P4.9 decisions (2026-10-01, `cb0e735`): One Piece thumbnails and the `/leaders` image view

LATER row 51 fired: `img.deckwarden.gg` serves the One Piece mirror, and the small boxes R3 and R5a gated on it now show One Piece cards. What was decided:

- **One rule, one gate.** `thumbnailUrl()` answers the mirror's `optcg/small/<KEY>.webp` for an `img.deckwarden.gg` PNG; an r2.dev URL stays null. The search and suggest rows, both printings lists, the deck tiles, the home shelf and the `/leaders` grid all changed through that one function, and no box changed size: the slots already reserved 146 × 204 (tiles, shelves, grids) or 26 × 36 (rows).
- **The rendition** is 146 × 204 WebP q80 with the alpha kept, written by the mirror job with sharp. Measured over twelve mirror PNGs: 10,141 B mean, against 174,375 B for the full PNG and ~11.7 KB for Scryfall's `small`. WebP rather than JPEG because the PNGs' rounded corners are transparent over white on some cards and black on others.
- **The home shelf's One Piece cards** keep the printed id top-left and the name, chips and life below; the image now fills the slot the gradient held, and the gradient stays the fallback. The shelf's statement LEFT JOINs the default printing, as the Magic shelf does, so guest `/` is still 3 statements.
- **`/leaders` gets the List / Grid toggle** through `LeaderIndexView`'s `optcg` flavor. The list is `/leaders`' markup from before P4.9 (name, card number, life, chips, no rank: A to Z is not a ranking). The grid puts the card number top-left, the shelf's corner (C13's rule). One stored preference drives both indexes, and the count line sits left of the toggle. The server HTML is still the list (142 `/l/` links), and the page is still one statement.
- **Not changed**: the sample hand (`toSmallImage` still passes the full PNG through; LATER), the grids, hubs and name previews that already render the full image, and the `/l/` hub hero.

## Addendum — Wave-4 supersessions (added 2026-10-01, Y1 step 0)

The Y-series (`WAVE4.md`, build plan §6e) changes recorded wording and one recorded filter, and will change one R3 state. As with the earlier addenda, the sections above, `WAVE2.md` and `WAVE3.md` stay as written; this addendum says which of their statements no longer hold:

- **Y1 supersedes the W-era evidence and shelf wording.** Every surface that ranks by EDHREC rank says what is counted — decklists, not games: home's shelf ("Popular in Commander decklists"), `/commanders`, the hub staples and footer, the combo totals on hubs and card pages, the Combo Radar, `/cards`' set strip (X4a's "Most played in {set}" becomes "Most popular in {set}"), the evidence tiers and the Autofill sheet's lead (W9b's and X3's "real play data"). Topdeck evidence says "competitive". The Meta Lens ("Most played with {commander}") is exempt — it counts tournament lists — and stays byte-identical.
- **Y1 supersedes the hub staples' strict `<`** (`staples-table.tsx`, W-era): the hub's budget pills read "≤ $5 a card" / "≤ $1 a card" and filter inclusively, the vocabulary Suggestions and Autofill share. A card at exactly $5.00 now shows under the $5 pill.
- **Y1 changes P1.5's Mana sources table**: with a commander, a card that makes "any color" counts only for the colors the commander allows; the table carries a one-line hint saying so.
- **Y2a will change R3's save-slot states**: a fourth state, "Draft", for a deck that has no row yet; and the empty-deck validation reads as progress ("Choose a commander · 100 to go"), not problems.
- **The Warden's approval line stays legality-only for the whole wave.** No bracket, goal or journal copy says "approve" (WAVE4 D0's copy guard, from Y4a).

### Y1 decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D1 (2026-10-01, `dcfcdcf`)

D1 shipped as drawn — every default wording kept. What it left to the package, and what was decided:

- **"competitive" rides the Topdeck `why` lines, all four** (the add side and the three cut-side lines); the scope sentence ("N of M top-16 lists at 16+ player events on Topdeck.gg …") is unchanged, so `recommend-smoke`'s two regexes hold — both were run against the new strings before the edit.
- **The Mana sources hint names Treasure makers**: "Cards that make “any color” count once for each color your commander allows; Treasure makers count through their reminder text." One quiet line under the table (`text-[0.65rem]`, the stat blocks' hint size), from an optional `hint` on the `table` block.
- **The clamp masks every produced bit** (`producedMask & (commanderCi | C)`, the contract's formula), so an off-identity card — already a color-identity error — counts only its on-identity colors. **No commander → today's counting**, pinned.
- **The budget list** is `src/lib/recommend/budget.ts` (`BUDGET_OPTIONS`, `withinBudget`), Autofill's labels; unpriced cards never pass a tier. The hub's count line reads "N of 100 at $5 or less · M at $1 or less", its empty tier "None of these staples fit that budget right now."
- **The share page's owner** is `isDeckOwner(deck, null, sessionUserId)` — access.ts's own rule, so a guest deck is never an owner there without its token, and the private gate inherits `isOwner` from the deck GET. "Open in editor" keeps its place after "Buy this deck"; the visitor row is byte-identical.
- **The Share dialog's Private line** splits on "this browser holds no claim token" (account decks never hold one): "Only you can view it." / "Only this browser can view it." Copy link keeps its name and confirms "✓ Link copied" in a status slot for 1.8 s (the share page's F12 reset), with a failure line ("Couldn't copy — select the link and copy it.") the old dialog never had.
- **Left by the phrase grep on purpose**: the Meta Lens (exempt), `card-search.tsx`'s `most-played-heading` id and `data-slot="most-played"` (identifiers, not copy), and code comments about internal ordering.

### Y2a decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D2 (2026-10-01, `4fd9b10`)

D2's Y2a paragraphs shipped as drawn. What the package decided:

- **The flag is `progress?: true` on `ValidationIssue`**, set by both adapters' `validate` for an under-minimum `ZONE_SIZE` (an empty command zone / no Leader; One Piece's short main zone too) and an under-minimum `DECK_SIZE`. Over the maximum is never flagged. Severity stays `error` — legality is unchanged and the Warden line still needs zero issues. **It does not ride the wire**: the cards PUT maps its `validation` through `toWireIssues` (`src/lib/decks/validation.ts`), so the response JSON is byte-identical — proven by snapshotting the empty / short / no-commander decks of both games before the change and diffing after.
- **One helper owns the words**: `src/lib/decks/progress.ts` — `deckProgress(entries, format)` (`needsLeader`, `toGo`), `progressLine` ("Choose a commander · 100 to go", "Choose a leader · 50 to go", "2 to go", or null) and `toGoPhrase`. The flag says *which* issues are progress; the helper says how far to go — both read the same minimums.
- **`ValidationPanel` takes an optional `progress` line.** Given one (the editor), flagged issues leave the red count and render as one neutral `text-muted-foreground` line with a muted dot (not a live region — it changes on every add); real problems stay red beneath it. **Not given (the share page), every issue renders as before** — a visitor never reads "Choose a commander" (LATER row 162).
- **"· N to go" replaces "cards"** in the summary while under the minimum ("98 / 100 · 2 to go"), computed from the counted-up number so the two never disagree mid-roll; at or over the minimum it reads "100 / 100 cards" and over it says nothing more (the Cut Coach's "Over by N" stays the over-limit face).
- **The draft slot**: "Draft" at rest (`draft && status === "saved"`), titled "Saves on your first change", no check; `data-draft` marks every render without a server row, `data-status` is untouched (all 19 pins green). The first edit reads "Unsaved…" like any other; the minted row ends the draft.
- **"Keep this deck"** sits where Share will be, for `draftSeed !== null` (precon, combo, Surprise me, a chosen leader) once the seed settled with cards on screen, while the slot is at rest. The click is `markDirty()` + `flush()` — a real edit through `ensureDeck`'s single-flight; the button leaves with the "saved" state, so one click = one deck (RTL + once on dev: census 208 → 209 → 208 after the delete).
- **The quiet first screen**: View / Group / Sort render once the list (non-leader rows) has a card; the summary's phone "Add cards" steps aside while the empty state shows its own. **Keyboard hints are a fine-pointer line** (`pointer-coarse:hidden`) — the search pane's keycaps **and the empty leader zone's "or press Ctrl+Enter on a search result"** (found on the dev pass; `EmptyState.hint` widened to a node). ⌘ on a Mac through `src/components/use-is-mac.ts` (`useSyncExternalStore`, server snapshot false — no hydration mismatch). The `?` sheet's "Ctrl is ⌘ on a Mac" line is unchanged.
- **One Undo for removals**: the ✕, the leader's remove and a stepper reaching zero (the pane routes qty 0 to `onRemove`) toast "Removed X" / "Removed 7× Forest" with an Undo through `restoreEntry` (`editor-state.ts`) — the entry comes back with its quantity, tags, printing and position (a partner pair keeps its art leader first); if the zone filled meanwhile, the Undo refuses with "Couldn't undo — Leader is full (max 1 card)." The Cut Coach's cuts keep their own path.
- **`/decks/new`'s fallback** is `new-deck-fallback.tsx`: the editor header's height with the mark → home and two skeleton bars, a still skeleton body (`motion-safe` pulse), `aria-busy` and an sr-only status. It is the page's static HTML for every `/decks/new` URL (picker and editor alike); the route stays `○`.

### Y2b decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D2 (2026-10-01, `bb4b1e2`)

D2's Y2b paragraphs shipped, with one deviation in the snapshot route's body. What the package decided:

- **One door list, one vocabulary**: `src/lib/decks/start-doors.ts` — `startDoors(adapter, format)` in WAVE4's order (Pick a commander / leader · Paste a list · Start from a precon · Surprise me), every door declared by the adapter: Pick from `display.leaderBrowse` (W4); **Start from a precon from a new optional `display.preconBrowse`** (Magic: `/precons` · "Start from a precon"; One Piece declares none); Surprise me where `recommend.autofill` is declared (W9c's gate) and the format has a leader zone; Paste a list for every game. Pinned per game in `start-doors.test.ts`.
- **The picker's game cards grow the doors**: a muted link row under each card ("Other ways to start a … deck"). **W9c's "Surprise me — random commander" becomes the shared "Surprise me"** (home's word). Paste a list and Surprise me land through the chooser's one-shot latches with `replace`, like the card; Pick and the precon door are ordinary page links. The cards dropped `flex-1`, so a game with more doors no longer stretches the other card (found on the 1200 dev pass).
- **The empty draft's doors** follow "Add cards" in the empty state's one wrapping row: Paste a list for any deck (draft or saved), Start from a precon and Surprise me for a draft only, and only while the leader zone is empty — each starts a deck from nothing. **"Pick a commander" is not repeated there**: the empty leader zone right above already is that door (W4's Browse link). Once a leader is set the row reads Autofill a starter shell · Add cards · Paste a list. **No door renders while a URL seed is still landing** (the plain empty state shows, as before), so a door never races a seed.
- **`?import=1`** is a render-time one-shot latch like `?autofill=1` (after any pending seed settles), stripped by the chooser's `replaceState` along with `surprise` and `autofill`; a crafted link carrying both opens the review sheet. A latch set on the picker itself carries into the game picked next (no remount), so `/decks/new?import=1` is a game-neutral door home could use (LATER row 166).
- **Surprise me in the editor rolls in place**, not by navigation: W9c's seeder became `rollLeader`, shared by the `?surprise=1` effect and the door, which is disabled while a roll is in flight. A navigation to `?surprise=1` would do nothing in a draft whose `from=` seed had failed — `from` outranks `surprise` in `draftSeed`. **"Keep this deck" now shows for any draft holding cards it never edited** — `(draftSeed === null || seedSettled)` replaces `draftSeed !== null && seedSettled` — which covers a door-rolled draft; an edit marks dirty and mints the row, so nothing else qualifies (reverting the condition fails the door test).
- **`POST /api/recommendations`**: `{game, format, leaderIds (1–2), entries: [{cardId, qty}] (≤ 500, 1–99 copies — the cards PUT's limits), budget?}`. **Deviation: WAVE4 sketched `entryIds`; the body carries copies**, because the ranker's curve state counts copies and quotes them ("N of the template's ~13 nonland cards at mana value 2") — ids alone would let a draft's answer differ from the same deck's GET (twenty Relentless Rats). Facts come from `loadEntryFacts` (an unknown id answers 400 naming it); the snapshot is exactly `recommendForDeck`'s (the leaders first at one copy, every other entry, the leaders' OR); the answer is `{count, recommendations}` — no `deckId`, and **no `owned`**: a draft never offers "Only cards I own", because the editor learns `hasCollection` from the deck GET (LATER row 165). Its own bucket, `recommendSnapshot`: 30/min plus 200/hour per IP. Nine statements per call (two counter upserts, the facts read, the engine's six); 2.7 s on dev (local machine to Neon), 0.73 s on prod.
- **The draft panel keys exactly like the GET** (`deckStateKey` + budget + owned + nonce), and its request body is a memoized string, so a tag or printing edit never asks again. The key does not change when the row mints — the GET would answer the same snapshot, which `smoke:recommend` now proves on live data (ids, order and evidence identical) — so minting never refetches; the next settled edit GETs.
- **First approval**: `useFirstApproval(approved, deckId)` (`src/components/editor/use-first-approval.ts`) sits one level above the panel's own settle so the deck id can arrive after the approval. It is born at the false → true transition — never at a mount at zero, never on a load's null → true — and lands on a row: **a draft that approves before its row exists offers nothing until the row mints** (an Autofill apply or an Import mints a second later; a seeded precon's step is Keep this deck, and then the link appears). "Per deck" is the deck id once it exists. **The flag is `deckwarden:share-offered`**: a JSON array of deck ids, newest kept, capped at 100 (`src/lib/decks/share-offers.ts`, the appearance store's pattern). The offer is recorded when it is made, so a reload, another tab or a later approval never offers again, while the session that made it keeps the link whenever the deck is approved. The link is a plain underlined button **under** the Warden line — `ValidationPanel`'s new `approvalAction` slot, indented to the line's text; the share page passes none and keeps its single `<p>` — and it opens the existing Share dialog. Both games.

### Y3a decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D3 (2026-10-02, `884c62f`)

D3 shipped as drawn: one migration (`0016`, exactly two `ADD COLUMN`s on `combos`), no route, no UI, no dependency, and nothing reads the new data until Y3b. What the package decided:

- **The pinned Game Changer hash lives in a data-only ruleset module that Y3b grows**: `src/lib/games/mtg/bracket-ruleset.ts`, `BRACKET_RULESET = {version: 1, gameChangers: {asOf: "2026-02-09", count: 53, md5}}`. The md5 is `gameChangerDigest()` (`scryfall-map.ts`): md5 of the sorted oracle ids joined by `\n`, pinned with a literal. The watch compares the md5 only, so a same-size swap still fails (pinned).
