- **The watch** is `pnpm ruleset:watch` (`scripts/ruleset-watch.ts`; the logic is pure in `src/lib/games/mtg/ruleset-watch.ts`). It runs after the backups and before the keepalive, `if: !cancelled()`. The keepalive (`if: always()`) stays the last step, so a red watch never skips it. The watch reads the latest *successful* Scryfall run and fails with D3's sentence verbatim, followed by Scryfall's count, md5 and run, then the ruleset's. It also fails when that run recorded no Game Changers. On a night the Scryfall step fails, it checks the last good run.
- **`game_changer` presence** is asserted on every object the run maps. Non-English printings, tokens, emblems and art cards are skipped before mapping and never reach a row. Anything but a boolean counts as missing (null is not false), and any miss throws before the first card write.
- **The Tagger bulk is read whole, not streamed** (`scripts/ingest/tagger-read.ts`). It is ~6 MB gzipped and ~19 MB of JSONL: one `fetch` and `arrayBuffer`, then a promisified `gunzip`, all under a 2-minute `AbortSignal.timeout`. Every failure (HTTP, network, gzip, a malformed line) lands in one catch. A piped stream's error event could have escaped as an unhandled error and failed the Scryfall step. The index keeps only each tag's child ids and oracle ids, and the roll-up is a cycle-safe walk over `child_ids` (neither pinned tag has descendants today).
- **The fallback.** Before staging, the run reads the identities carrying `mld` / `extra_turn` (`attrs @>` containment, served by `ci_attrs_gin` as a BitmapOr) and the last successful run's `stats.tagger.stale_since`. Per flag, the outcome is one of three:
  - **fresh**: the bulk was read and the pinned tag flags at least one card.
  - **kept**: the read failed, or the pinned tag is missing from the bulk or flags no card. The stored cards are kept and split again by today's overrides file.
  - **disabled**: the file switches the flag off, and it is written for no card.

  `stale_since` is null when fresh or disabled. Otherwise it holds the first stale run's start, carried from run to run. The Scryfall step stays green either way.
- **The overrides file** (`data/mtg/tagger-overrides.json`) is keyed by flag name (`mld`, `extra_turn`). Each flag pins its Tagger tag by UUID (`{id, slug}`; slugs get renamed) and has an `enabled` kill-switch and `disabledCards`. Only `mld` has `clear` and `edge` (oracle id → note).
  - **Both halves of the split are listed** (42 clear + 69 edge = all 111 of our cards on the 2026-10-01 bulk), so a card Tagger tags later is visible. It reads **edge** (your call) until someone reviews it: the honest reading for an unreviewed card, never a firm Bracket 4. The run logs its name and counts it in `counts.mld.unreviewed`.
  - **The split rule** is written in the file's `$comment`:
    - **clear**: played as intended, the card takes away, locks or rewrites the mana of most lands of every opponent, whatever they play. These are Wizards' five examples and the cards that do the same, resets that take every land with them included.
    - **edge**: the land denial hangs on something the list can't see — an ultimate, a mode, one land type, snow or color, a number, a board state, random piles, or one land at a time from repeated triggers.
  - **Validation**: a malformed file fails the run before any write. An oracle id the run doesn't stage fails before the first card write. The check runs against this run's staged identities, so a brand-new card can be listed the night it appears.
- **Spellbook**: `bracket_tag text` and `relevant boolean`, both nullable. `text` follows house style (no `char(n)` anywhere in the schema).
  - **NULL** means not ingested yet; for the tag it can also mean a letter outside R S P O C E, which is counted in `stats.bracket_tags_unknown` with a warning. B rides only on the 1,527 variants Commander bans, which the legality filter drops first.
  - **`relevant`** = any produced feature with status S.
  - **The merge**: both columns join the stage table, the INSERT, the SET and the tuple compare, so the first tagged run rewrote every row once.
  - **The URL** is the documented `https://json.commanderspellbook.com/variants.json.gz`: the same S3 object (identical ETag, Last-Modified and `x-amz-version-id`) behind CloudFront, still `Content-Encoding: gzip`, which `maybeGunzip` already handles.
- **The bulk's `version` and `timestamp`**: `jsonArrayElements` gains an optional `onRootValue(key, scalar)`, which receives the root scalars that come before the array, from the same single pass. Nested root values are skipped without buffering, and an unparsable scalar is skipped. Spellbook's root reads `timestamp`, `version`, `variants`, and a trailing array after `variants` is never read.
- **The keys ride the wire.** `attrs` already travels whole in the search API, the deck-cards wire and card pages' client props. The 228 flagged identities' payloads carry one or two more keys, unrendered: no display or search field reads them, and search fields come from the adapter's whitelist. Y4a's client-side read will need them there.

### Y3b decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D4 (2026-10-03, `3320ad6`)

D4 shipped as drawn: no migration, no route, no UI, no dependency, and nothing renders the read until Y4a. What the package decided:

- **The adapter's declaration** is `brackets?: BracketsMeta` beside `hub?` and `recommend?` (`src/lib/games/types.ts`): `noun` ("bracket"), `levels` (1–5 with Wizards' names — `targetLevel`'s range), `ruleset {version, asOf}`, `questions` (How it plays, by key), `freshnessSources` (`ingest_runs.source` values), and two pure functions, `freshness(runs, readAt)` and `assess(input)`. One Piece declares nothing. Core names stay game-agnostic: a "level", `targetLevel`, `brackets`; the read's types (`BracketRead`, `CompleteCombo`, `BracketFreshness`) live in the contract.
- **The ruleset is Wizards' table as data** (`bracket-ruleset.ts`): per level the Game Changer allowance, land denial, extra turns (`none` / `few` / `any`), two-card combos (`none` / `late` / `any`) and the turns Wizards expects. The engine derives every allowance it quotes from it, so a rules change is one reviewed edit. **`version` stays 1**: Y3a's version 1 was the pin alone, nothing was ever answered against it, and a Game Changers pin move alone never bumps it (no answer depends on the list). The read's `ruleset.asOf` is the newer of the two dates (2026-02-09).
- **The engine** (`src/lib/games/mtg/brackets.ts`, Spellbook's MIT notice verbatim at the top) is Spellbook's deck ladder adjusted both ways, as D4's table says. Our rows keep no mana value needed, prerequisites or zones, so tags are read as stored, never recomputed. Spellbook's result patterns (mass land denial, infinite turns minus an opponent's, control of every opponent) are ported verbatim and matched against `results`.
- **The two-card rule** counts the cards besides the deck's commander (`usesCommander` = a leader-zone card is a piece), relevant, no template. **A combo with your commander is a question only when its tag is E, O or S**: Spellbook rates combos with every commander-eligible piece "arguable", so knowing the commander can only move a combo's two-card call, and C, P and R already count it as definitely two-card or top. D4's row read literally ("any combo that uses your commander") would have asked about combos Spellbook's own algorithm can't move.
- **A combo that needs a template is never counted**: one question ("Does your deck have the rest of this combo?"), whose yes is what the combo would prove with it; a template combo that could prove nothing asks nothing. **Correction to WAVE4 B**: Hullbreaker Horror + Sol Ring (`513-5034--46`, tagged E) needs "Permanent Castable for {C}", so it is a three-piece combo — not the lenient two-card example B cites; the leniency is real elsewhere (Blasphemous Act + Repercussion is C, Maskwood Nexus + The World Tree is C, both read 3 here).
- **"Couldn't check"**: a feed is missing (no successful run recorded it — before Y3a, or never), off (a Tagger kill-switch), or stale — **not refreshed for more than 7 days** at the read time (a kept Tagger flag counts from its `stale_since`). Any of them, a NULL tag or `relevant` on a combo, combo facts that didn't load, or a card whose data didn't load adds a "Couldn't check" line and makes the status `unavailable`: `minimum` is then only a floor. Findings from stale data keep counting — a failing source never lowers the read.
- **Status precedence**: blocked (a card validation calls BANNED or NOT_LEGAL — `legalityIssue` is exported from `validate.ts` so the two never disagree; a preview card never blocks) → draft (any zone or the deck under its minimum) → unavailable → review (an unanswered question whose yes would raise the read) → read.
- **Answers only raise.** How it plays: cEDH → 5, wins or locks before turn 6 → 4, staples and quality → 3, **not theme first → 2** (D4 names only the yes; a no rules out Exhibition), theme first → 1 when nothing else is higher. **Per-question answers** (`answers.calls`, keyed by the questions' stable ids: `land-denial:<oracle id>`, `extra-turns:<oracle ids>`, `combo:<variant id>`) are part of the engine's input now: yes raises to the question's level, no settles it. Y4b stores both. A newer ruleset flags answers (`answersStale`) and still applies them.
- **The sentences are adapter data** — factor sentences, changes, questions, assumptions (D5's fixed text, the Game Changers date from ingest) — so the editor, the share page and Copy for the table say the same words. **D0's copy guard landed now** beside them (`brackets.test.ts`): every string a read can produce, gathered from one small read per rule, never contains "approv" or a Spellbook tag name.
- **Opponents' extra turns**: `extra_turn` means a turn for you. The three Tagger cards that give the turn only to an opponent (Emrakul, the Promised End; Eon Frolicker; Perch Protection) are disabled in `data/mtg/tagger-overrides.json` with their reasons — a reviewed data edit the next nightly applies — the way Spellbook's own combo flag leaves out turns "for … opponent". No engine rule.
- **`loadCompleteCombos`** is one statement that groups the list's piece rows first and joins `combos` on id and piece count: 25 ms on the server for the heaviest precon, 101 ms for the 100 most combo-dense cards (445 combos), half the join-first shape's time and an eighth of its disk spill. **No identity filter**: a combo's identity is its pieces' union (0 exceptions), so the answer depends on the id set alone — D4's "color-fit to the deck" holds by construction. **The freshness loader** (`src/lib/brackets/freshness.ts`) is one DISTINCT ON statement over `ingest_runs` (a 0.2 ms seq scan at 258 rows).
- **Spellbook's tag letters** moved to a client-safe `spellbook-tags.ts` (re-exported from `spellbook-map.ts`): the read runs in the browser, and `spellbook-map.ts` reaches `node:crypto` through `scryfall-map.ts`.

### Y4a decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D5 (2026-10-05, `aa2a29f`)

D5's Y4a half shipped: one route, the bracket line, and the Why sheet's first two blocks. No migration, no dependency. What the package decided:

- **The words stay adapter data.** `BracketsMeta` gains `line(read, ctx)` and `links`. Magic's words live in `src/lib/games/mtg/bracket-line.ts` beside the read's own sentences, guarded by `bracket-line.test.ts`, so core never says "Game Changer" or "Core". Core owns only four things:
  - its fetch lines ("Checking combos…", "Couldn't check combos · Retry");
  - "Why?";
  - the sheet's headings (`src/lib/brackets/copy.ts`, guarded by `copy.test.ts`);
  - the draft's progress phrase: `addMorePhrase` in `src/lib/decks/progress.ts`, the module that "owns the phrase".

  The engine's rules are untouched; `mtgBrackets` gains two fields.
- **The route.** `GET /api/combos/complete?game=mtg&ids=<sorted, comma-joined>` answers `{combos, freshness}` from the two core loaders, in two statements.
  - **One URL per set.** It takes `game` then `ids`, nothing else: at most 200 lowercase uuids, sorted and unique. Anything else answers 400, and so does One Piece.
  - **The check reads parsed parameters.** Next's server re-serializes the query before a handler runs, so a comma arrives as `%2C`. The first build compared bytes and refused every multi-card request on dev, where the unit test had passed. So `%2C` and `,` read the same: one set can hold two edge keys, but the client only ever sends literal commas.
  - **Caching** is X3's: force-dynamic with `s-maxage=3600, stale-while-revalidate=86400`. Prod answered MISS then HIT for the same set.
  - **A bucket, unlike X3's route.** `comboFacts` allows 60/min plus 600/hour per IP. X3's keys are real combos; here any uuid-shaped set is a fresh URL, so a walk through sets would reach Neon unmetered. An edge HIT never reaches the bucket. Each MISS costs 4 statements (two counter upserts and the two loaders).
- **When the editor asks** (`src/components/deck/use-bracket-facts.ts`):
  - **Its own settle, not autosave's.** It waits 500 ms after the sorted id set last changed. The facts depend on the set alone, so a draft that never saves, or an autosave that failed, still gets its read.
  - **The first ask goes at once.** A request for an older set is aborted, and an answer whose body lands after its set moved on is dropped. A failed set waits for Retry. Over 200 distinct cards it asks nothing ("over"), and the read says it couldn't check combos.
  - **Only with a commander.** It is gated on a leader-zone card. With no commander there is no line at all; the progress line already says "Choose a commander".
- **The line's order** (`src/lib/brackets/line-view.ts`): blocked → the facts failed → draft → checking → the read.
  - A draft's line speaks at once: what the card data proves needs no facts, and a combo joins when they land. It never says "Checking combos…".
  - A blocked line offers nothing after it; the problem list above already names the card.
- **Deviation: Why? on a draft.** The draft line gains "· Why?" once the facts are in and the list has found something, a finding or a question. A combo-seeded draft's combo and a draft's Game Changers are then one click away (D11: every finding names its cards). While the facts land, D5's draft row reads exactly as drawn.
- **The lines D5 leaves out:**
  - **A minimum of 2**: "At least Bracket 2 (Core)".
  - **A read that couldn't check everything** leads with its floor and names the gap, with Why? and no Retry (asking again can't refresh a stale feed): "At least Bracket 3 (Upgraded) · Couldn't check extra turns". With nothing flagged it is "Bracket read incomplete · Couldn't check combos". It never says "nothing here goes past Core" without every check.
  - **A card that isn't legal** is never called banned: "· 1 card not legal in Commander".
  - **A pending call** lists every open outcome ("Bracket 1–2 or 4", "Bracket 2, 3 or 4") and names what is open ("one combo", "two cards", "chaining extra turns"). "1–2" splits to "1 or 2" when 2 is an outcome itself.
  - **"1–2" comes from the ruleset**: the brackets that bar every kind of card the read flags (Game Changers, land denial, two-card combos) are Exhibition and Core, and "Core" is level 2's name.
- **The line's look.**
  - Muted text with a gauge glyph (lucide `Gauge`) the size of the Warden's shield, so both lines' text starts at the same x. The glyph sits on the first line's baseline: on touch, the 44 px "Why?" makes that line tall, and with top alignment the glyph floated above the text (found on the 375 px pass).
  - "Why?" and "Retry" are underlined in the foreground color, 44×44 on coarse pointers (`pointer-coarse:min-h-11` plus `min-w-11`).
  - Not a live region: it changes on every settled edit, like the progress line. `data-status` and `data-facts` are there for QA.
- **The sheet** (`src/components/deck/bracket-sheet.tsx`):
  - **Shape.** The title is "Why this bracket?". It leads with the line's own words, plus a note in a draft.
  - **What the cards show.** Each finding, highest first, shows its sentence, what would change it and its linked source. A combo adds its results, its piece count and "How it works ↗", which the facts carry. Then "Your call", highest first: an intro ("The list can't answer these — you and your table can."), then each question with "If yes, it's at least Bracket N (Name)." and no answer controls. A question names its cards only when its reason doesn't already (a chain of extra turns doesn't). Then "Couldn't check".
  - **What this read assumes.** The adapter's seven lines, then "Wizards' Commander Brackets, as of Feb 9, 2026 ↗" (the read's ruleset as-of) and "Combos and their ratings from Commander Spellbook ↗".
  - **Links.** Game Changers go to Wizards' `#gamechangers`. Land denial and extra turns go to the Tagger tag pages, whose slugs are pinned against the overrides file. A combo goes to its walkthrough.
  - **Containers.** A Drawer on phones, with a 44 px Close; a Modal from md. Focus returns to "Why?" through Base UI's default, because the opener isn't the More menu.
- **The copy guard runs in three places**:
  - the adapter's lines, in `bracket-line.test.ts`: every status, no "approv", no tag name, no notation;
  - core's table, in `copy.test.ts`, which enumerates every builder;
  - everything the sheet renders, in `bracket-sheet.test.tsx`, over five reads.
- **A pin moved.** `deck-editor.test.tsx` had three `startsWith("/api/combos/")` filters for X3's "the combo is never fetched". They now match the seed GET only (`/api/combos/<digits>`), because the facts route shares that prefix. The Surprise-beats-combo test went red without the change.

### Y4b decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D5 (2026-10-05, `3f5a6a9`)

D5's Y4b half shipped: one migration (`0017`, exactly one nullable `ADD COLUMN "goals" jsonb` on `decks`, applied before the push with the owner's yes), no route, no dependency. What the package decided:

- **One module owns goals**: `src/lib/decks/goals.ts`, client-safe like X5's `avatar.ts` (zod included). It holds:
  - the stored shape, D5's `{v: 1, targetLevel?, budget?: {perCardUsd?, totalUsd?}, answers?: {rulesetVersion, play?, calls?}, exceptions?}`;
  - its zod, built per deck from the adapter's `brackets` (the target's range from `levels`, the How it plays keys from `questions`, the highest answerable `rulesetVersion` from `ruleset`). With no `brackets` (One Piece) only `v` and a budget pass;
  - the sheet's pure edits: `withTarget`, `withAnswer` (stamps the current ruleset version), `restampAnswers`, `withExceptions` (stored trimmed, ≤ 200, an empty line is none);
  - `publicGoals` and `goalsPatchBody`.
- **The budget is accepted and stored now**, so its owner-only rule is pinned where the wire is built; its controls are Y6a's. `perCardUsd` must be one of the site's per-card tiers (5 or 1, from `budget.ts`'s `BUDGET_OPTIONS`); `totalUsd` is above 0 and at most 100,000.
- **The goals PATCH**: the body `{goals}` carries the whole object, and null clears. Goals that say nothing are stored as NULL. It uses the meta PATCH's per-deck bucket. `updated_at` moves only when another key rides along, so a name plus goals still moves it.
- **The create** carries a draft's goals, checked once `game` is known; a bad shape answers 400 before the insert, and the honeypot answers before goals are read.
- **What visitors see**: the target and the exceptions, never the budget, **and never the answers**. Which answers "changed the read" can only be told from the read (the cards and their combo facts), which no deck route computes. Y5's share page computes it server-side from the full row and shows those. Exposing every stored answer would also tell the list's history: answers to questions about cards since cut. The sheet's notes are "Shown on your share page." under the target and "Answers that change the read are shown on your share page." under the answers. Y5 makes both literal: until it ships, the share page renders nothing of the goals.
- **The share page's props** now carry `goals` (it passes `deckMetaJson` whole): visitors get `publicGoals` (null on every deck today), the owner gets everything. Nothing renders it until Y5, and the visible output is unchanged.
- **The target row's words** (D5's "Your target 2 · the cards say 3+" without the notation D0 forbids): "Your target: Bracket 2 · the cards say at least 3". "Bracket" is said once, so the numbers read as brackets. Every read state keeps that shape:
  - at or above the cards, the same words ("Your target: Bracket 3 · the cards say at least 2", "… · nothing here goes past Core") — the target never hides the read, and never replaces it;
  - with answers, "the cards and your answers say 4";
  - an open call, "the cards say 3 or 4 — one combo is your call";
  - a read that couldn't check everything, "the cards say at least 3 · Couldn't check extra turns", or "Couldn't check combos" alone.

  A draft's and a blocked list's lines don't change; the sheet shows the target there.
- **The "with answers" line with a call still open** (no target): "Bracket 3 or 4 — from the cards and your answers · one combo is your call". Y4a's settled line ("Bracket 4 (Optimized) — from the cards and your answers") is unchanged. "Not sure" leaves a call open and says nothing about answers.
- **"Rules changed since you answered"** appears in two places:
  - on the line, before "Why?" (core's `line-view`), so a rules change prompts a review without opening anything (D11);
  - as a notice at the top of the sheet. **Addition: "Keep my answers"** restamps them; without it the notice could only be cleared by changing an answer. Any answer edit stamps the current version, and How it plays opens by itself when stale answers include its own.
- **A draft holding goals keeps its "Why?"** (a narrow extension of Y4a's draft deviation): a target or answers live in the sheet, so a list cut back under the minimum never hides them.
- **The sheet's order**: the lead, the stale notice, What the cards show (Your call now with answers), Your target, How it plays, then What this read assumes.
  - **The conflict callout** sits inside Your target, right under the control, where the choice was made: "Above your target", then "Your target is Bracket 2 (Core). These put the deck above it:", then each of `read.conflicts` with its sentence and what would change it. The findings list above is untouched (D11). Y7b adds "Swap…" to these rows.
  - **Answers**: Yes / No / Not sure per question, keyed by its stable id, under "Your answer". Each group's accessible name is the question plus its cards, since two template combos can share a question's words.
  - **Unanswered presses nothing.** `Segmented` gained `value: null` (no item pressed, no thumb) rather than pre-pressing "Not sure". "Not sure" is a stored answer ("unsure") that leaves the call open.
  - **How it plays** is a Collapsible (the sample hand's pattern), collapsed, with "· N answered" on its trigger.
- **The target control** is `Segmented` 1–5 · Not set ("Not set" pressed with no target), with the level's name beside it ("Bracket 2 (Core)"). Past four options, `Segmented` narrows its coarse-pointer side padding to 8 px. Six equal segments as wide as "Not set" then fit a 360 px phone (measured: 54 px segments, the 40 px label whole). The existing two- and three-option controls keep 12 px.
- **The exceptions line**: labelled "Table exceptions", with the example as its placeholder. The example is the adapter's (`BracketsMeta.exceptionsHint`, "e.g. one thematic Game Changer, ask me"), so core names no card kind. The input keeps what's typed and goals keep it trimmed. It is 44 px on touch.
- **Where goals save**: inside `save()`, with a third baseline. Goals ride the same debounced burst as the cards and the meta, with their own PATCH, so they get retries, the save slot and the pagehide keepalive for free.
  - `isBlankDraft()` counts goals: a target is something to say (D5). Pinned by a rolled commander, a target, then the commander cut inside the debounce: one create carrying the goals. A target set and cleared first mints nothing.
  - `ensureDeck` sends the goals in the create and moves their baseline, so a draft's first goals edit is one POST plus one PUT, with no PATCH.
  - A goals change never asks for the combo facts again: the card set is the same.
- **jsonb re-sorts object keys** (shortest first), so `goalsPatchBody` is canonical, with keys sorted at every depth. Goals read back after a reload then compare equal to the same goals edited in place. This was found on the dev smoke: two string compares failed on key order alone.
- **Stored answers outlive their questions**: an answer stays when its card is cut, so the question can come back. Answers are capped at 100 entries and 12,000 characters. Past either, the first answers in stored order whose questions aren't on screen go; jsonb's order, so not necessarily the oldest.
  - **Found in review, fixed before the push:** a "Do these extra turns chain?" id joins every extra-turn card's oracle id (37 characters each). The first build capped ids at 200 characters, so any deck with six or more extra-turn cards would have had its answer refused with a 400. Ids may now run to 4,000 characters, and a contract test feeds every id the real engine asks (eight chained extra turns, an edge land-denial card, a combo) through the zod.
- **Deviation: the "checked date"** D5 lists under Your target belongs to the share page (Y5: when its read was computed). The editor's read is live. The rules' as-of date and both links stay where Y4a put them, in What this read assumes, and aren't repeated.
- **The copy guard** reads the new words in its three places: core's two new builders joined `BUILDERS`, along with the adapter's `exceptionsHint` and questions; every adapter line is now also read beside each target 1–5; and the sheet's render guard gained a state with a target, stale answers and How it plays open.

### Y5 decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D6 (2026-10-06, `16ee2ab`)

D6 shipped: the share page's bracket line, "At the table", Copy for the table, the owner's row, and the declared chip on unfurls and tiles. No migration, no route, no dependency; the route table is byte-identical. What the package decided:

- **One read, two surfaces.** `src/lib/brackets/table.ts`' `tableBracket` runs the adapter's `assess` with the same inputs as the editor (the snapshot, the card wires, the facts, the target, the answers). A test assesses one fixture both ways and compares the reads. On dev the five QA precons read exactly Y4a's measured lines.
- **The table's voice** is adapter data (`BracketLineContext.voice: "owner" | "table"`, Magic's words in `bracket-line.ts`):
  - **Deviation:** D6's "Played as Bracket 2 · the cards say 3+" becomes "Played as Bracket 2 (Core) · the cards say at least 3". It drops the notation D0 forbids, and it names the level because strangers read it (the editor's row stays "Your target: Bracket 2 · …").
  - "your answers" becomes "the owner's answers" and "your call" becomes "the owner's call". With no target and no answers, both voices say D5's words (pinned).
  - **A precon keeps the editor's voice.** It has no owner and no goals, and the reader is the one who'd play it ("two combos are your call").
  - A draft reads "Bracket: 34 cards to go · …" to the pod (`cardsToGoPhrase`), not the builder's "add 34 more cards".
  - "Rules changed since you answered" never shows in the table's voice: it's the owner's prompt, and the editor is where it's answered.
- **Where the facts come from.** The page loads `loadBracketFreshness` + `loadCompleteCombos` in its second batch, gated on a commander in the cards wire, so it's exact rather than `leader_ids`. Both are in the second batch because the combos need the card ids. That's +2 statements on a Magic page with a commander and none elsewhere. Measured with `DB_LOG` (warm, dev):
  - Atraxa 6 → 8 statements (1.83 → 2.19 s);
  - a precon 4 → 6 (1.12 → 1.66 s): its second batch was empty, so it pays a whole round trip on dev. On prod it doesn't show: warm, Magic pages and precons answered in 0.51–0.77 s and One Piece in 0.51–0.58 s;
  - One Piece 5 and a list without a commander 4, both unchanged; the private gate 1.

  The combos statement keeps Y3b's plan, a hash join with an in-memory quicksort and no spill: 23 ms server time for Atraxa, 18 ms for Creative Energy and 100 ms for Witherbloom Pestilence (Y3b measured 24.6 ms there, warm). The freshness read takes 0.2 ms. Past 200 distinct cards the combos aren't asked ("over"). A load that fails is left to the client (`{from: "client"}`), which asks the facts route like the editor.
- **The private gate always asks from the client** (`useBracketFacts`, the editor's GET, one ask), so nothing private reaches the server HTML. While it lands the line reads "Checking combos…" and the card waits. Dev StrictMode shows each GET twice; the first is aborted.
- **"The answers that changed the read" are defined as the answers the read used.** That means a yes or a no to a How it plays question, or to one of this read's own questions (`review`). "Not sure" says nothing, and an answer about a card since cut would tell the list's history. D6's own example prints a "no" ("Pace (owner): doesn't usually win before turn 6"), which raises nothing, so "changed" read literally would have hidden it.
  - The filter (`goals.ts`' `tableGoals`) leaves the read unchanged (pinned).
  - It runs on the server from the full row (`shareGoals`), so nothing else leaves it. With no server facts, no answer is sent rather than every answer.
  - **Deviation:** the owner's page carries the same table goals, not Y4b's full owner goals. The page shows what the pod sees, and the editor fetches its own.
- **"At the table"** is a card under the line (`at-the-table.tsx`), shown once the facts are in and the read isn't a draft or blocked. The adapter's rows come first (`BracketsMeta.table`):
  - "Game Changers (Wizards' list)";
  - **deviation:** "Combos (Commander Spellbook)" in place of D6's "Two-card combos". It lists every complete combo the read found or asks about, as its pieces joined with " + ", because a three-card combo can raise the read too;
  - "Land denial / extra turns (Scryfall Tagger)", with flagged cards and the ones whose call is open.

  A feed the read couldn't check says "couldn't check", never "none". Then the owner's lines: How it plays through the adapter's new `questions[].table` words, "Owner's call: <question> No — <cards>", and "Exceptions (owner)". A row the owner said nothing for is absent. Card names are `CardNamePreview` buttons that open the card page. Found on dev: Magic names hold commas, so a row's items split with "; " once a combo or a comma name would run together (`itemSeparator`).
- **Copy for the table** follows D6's shape, with every source named:
  - **Deviation:** the second line is the page's line plus "· checked Oct 6, 2026", rather than D6's "Bracket: played as 3 …";
  - **the checked date** is the facts' `freshness.readAt`, so on a public page it is the render, and in the private gate the facts route's answer (edge-cached up to an hour, a day stale-while-revalidate). It appears in the copied text only: the page itself is computed per request;
  - **"Plan (owner)"** is the description's first paragraph on one line, cut at a word past 280 characters with "…", never generated. It's absent for precons (their description is W8a's product sentence) and when there's no description;
  - the adapter's closing note (`tableNote`), then the link.
- **The visitor's Why sheet** is read-only: Y4a's blocks plus "The owner's answer: Yes/No" on a question the page shows (a "Not sure" says nothing, pinned). There's no target control and no How it plays block, because the line and the card already say both.
- **The owner's row.** The server's `isOwner` covers an account deck and this browser's claim token covers a guest deck (Y1's rule).
  - **Deviation:** Open in editor · Copy ▾ · Share… · **Buy this deck**. Buy stays because the owner is the one most likely to buy the cards. Like, Bookmark and Fork leave the owner's row.
  - Copy ▾ is named after its trigger (R1b) and holds "Copy for the table" only where the page has a read to tell.
  - Status words: "Decklist copied", "Copied for the table", "Link copied", "Copy failed".
  - **Share…** opens `navigator.share` only on a coarse pointer: a desktop share sheet surprises people who expect a copied link. Everywhere else, or when the sheet fails, it copies the link. Closing the sheet says nothing.
  - A guest owner's row swaps in after hydration (the server can't know a claim token). The visitor row is server-rendered first (LATER).
- **`useCopyToClipboard` + `CopyStatus`** (`src/components/copy-status.tsx`) are adopted by the share page, the Share dialog and the Export dialog. Export now has a failure line ("Couldn't copy — select the list and copy it."), clears after 1.8 s, and keeps its button's name; it used to swap to "Copied ✓" for good. `buy-deck-menu.tsx`'s blocked-clipboard toast names no button: "Your browser blocked clipboard access. Allow it for this site, then try again."
- **Declared, never computed.**
  - **Deviation:** the unfurl's declared target is a second kicker line ("BRACKET 3 (DECLARED)"), not a third stat pill. Beside the wordmark, a third pill ran into the art credit's chip on dev ("100 cards", "$148", "Bracket 3 (declared)"), and one combined kicker line wrapped mid-phrase.
  - The OG select reads one jsonb path (`goalsTargetLevel`, a non-number reads as none). `og/labels.ts` holds the words, pinned equal to core's `declaredLabel`; a test checks that the route and its reads import no adapter, engine or bracket module.
  - Tiles show "Bracket 3 (declared)" in DeckTile's badge slot when no badge is passed (a precon's "Precon" wins). It comes through `deckCollectionSelect` (home, Continue building, `/u`, `/f`, the hub's shelves, where `/c/` is ISR so a change shows within the hour), `/account`'s full row, and the guest list's wire. A level the game doesn't have, and One Piece, show nothing.

### Y6a decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D7 (2026-10-10, `fb435ec`)

D7's Y6a half shipped: `applyGoals` after ranking, the plumbing, the lines and Suggestions. No migration, no route, no dependency; the route table is byte-identical. What the package decided:

- **Rank all → goals → slice.** `rankCandidates`' `limit` is optional now (absent = every candidate). The engine ranks all, and `applyGoals` (`src/lib/recommend/goals.ts`) walks them in rank order against the deck's goals, stopping once the list holds `limit` cards. **"Hidden" means the cards the goals took out of the list**: the ones that ranked above its last row. A card ranked below it would never have shown. The engine-level pin: 50 candidates whose 25 best are Game Changers, at a target of 2, answer the next 25 with those 25 hidden. Scores and evidence are untouched: pinned in Vitest, and on live data the no-goals answer's ids, scores and evidence equal the pre-Y6a answer byte for byte (dev and prod).
- **The rules are the adapter's.** `BracketsMeta` gains `flagPaths` and a pure `impact({...BracketInput, read, card, completes})` → `BracketConflict[]` (`rule`, `level`, `source`, `why`).
  - `flagPaths` are single-segment attrs paths, like `RecommendMeta.exclude`'s `jsonbPath`; Magic's are `game_changer`, `mld` and `extra_turn`.
  - Magic's `mtgBracketImpact` sits beside `assessBracket` and reuses its helpers: `gameChangerLevel`, the land-denial, extra-turn and chained-turn levels, `readCombo` and the stable question ids. The engine itself is unchanged.
  - A test runs every rule against the engine on the list plus the card, and agrees on the new minimum and on every question the card would open. Core names no rule.
- **What hides, with a target.** Each card is judged against the list as it stands; `applyGoals` gives every bracket conflict severity "hide".
  - **Game Changers**: one past the target's allowance (the list's own count).
  - **Mass land denial** at targets 1–3, clear or edge ("Possible mass land denial, your call").
  - **Extra turns**: any extra-turn card at target 1. At 2–3, a second one — **decided: hide, as D7 says**. It opens "Do these extra turns chain?", and WAVE4 A says a target-2 suggestion must never turn the line to "your call".
  - **Combo completions** whose own reading is above the target: firm (two-card, a tag, the results) or a question (an S or O range, a combo with the commander). **A template combo counts at what its yes would mean (decided)**, like any question above the target, though it never counts toward the read.
  - A question the player already answered counts as answered: no settles it, yes makes it firm. One line per rule, firm before a question. A list already above its target still hides the card: judged on the card, not on whether the read moves.
- **Without a target nothing hides.** A card that would raise the line gets a flag. The line here means the answers' level, else the minimum, the same yardstick `suggested` uses. The flag reads "Would make this deck at least Bracket 3 — a Game Changer (Wizards' list)", or for a question "Could make this deck Bracket 4 — … your call". A card that raises nothing says nothing.
- **D7's lines without notation** (D0), each naming its source:
  - "A Game Changer (Wizards' list) — your Bracket 2 target allows none";
  - "A fourth Game Changer (Wizards' list) — your Bracket 3 target allows up to three";
  - "Mass land denial — Wizards expects none at Brackets 1–3 (Scryfall Tagger)";
  - "A second extra-turn card — Brackets 2 and 3 avoid chaining extra turns (Scryfall Tagger)";
  - "Completes a two-card combo — Wizards expects none at Brackets 1 and 2 (Commander Spellbook)";
  - "Completes a combo that alone makes a deck at least Bracket 4 (Commander Spellbook)" (D7's "Bracket 4+");
  - "Completes a combo that may make a deck Bracket 4 — your call (Commander Spellbook)".

  The copy guard reads every line in both voices (`bracket-impact.test.ts`).
- **Where the read comes from on the server (measured, decided).** The engine runs the adapter's own `assess` over the list's entries, their `attrs` holding the declared flags alone.
  - The facts ride `ReadFacts` on `loadDeckEntries` and `loadEntryFacts`, the same statements, only wider.
  - One more statement, `loadCompleteCombos`, runs beside the candidate gather.
  - There is no freshness read (it moves a read's status, never a level) and no legality (it blocks a status, never a level). Pinned: the read and the impact are identical over full attrs and over the flags alone, for every rule.
  - Measured on dev (Creative Energy, warm, signed out): the GET went from 11 to 12 statements (3.23 → 3.36 s), the draft POST from 11 to 12. On prod, warm: 0.62–0.69 s for the GET, 0.82 s for the POST.
  - The alternative was per-card impacts without the list's combos. It would tell a deck a combo already holds at 3 that a Game Changer "would make this deck at least Bracket 3".
- **The flags in SQL.** Every candidate row carries one column, `jsonb_strip_nulls(jsonb_build_object('game_changer', attrs->'game_changer', …))` (`{}` for most). Each key passes exclude's check before `sql.raw`.
  - `CandidateCard` gains `externalKey` (question ids) and `flags`.
  - `CandidateCombo` gains its `key`, `tag`, `relevant`, `pieceCount` and `usesLeader`.
  - `DeckComboView` gains its `tag` and `relevant`, so the Radar's JSON grows two fields per row (Y6b reads them).
  - `loadComboSignals` passes the scan's `truncated` through. Both routes answer `combosTruncated`, and the panel says "Combo checks stopped at the most popular combos near this deck — rarer ones weren't checked against your goals."
- **The budget.** The deck's per-card tier (owner-only) flags a card over it: "Costs $45.00 — over your ≤ $5 a card budget", or "No known price — your budget is ≤ $5 a card" (unpriced never fits, as in `budget.ts`). It never hides a card. It only shows when the panel's own tier is wider than the goal, because the panel's tier still filters in SQL, as it always has.
- **Who gets which goals.** The GET reads them off the row it already loads. The owner gets them whole; a visitor gets the public subset (the target, with the exceptions riding along and doing nothing), never the budget or the answers. The draft POST takes `goals` checked by `goalsSchema(adapter.brackets)` (400 "Invalid goals"). Both answer `{…, recommendations, hidden, combosTruncated}`, and every row carries `conflicts` (empty when none).
- **The panel.**
  - **The goals line** sits on top: "Your goals: Bracket 2 (Core) · ≤ $5 a card · Change". It names the level, as the sheet's target control does (D7's example didn't).
    - With neither goal it reads **"No bracket target yet · Set one"**. That is LATER row 175's door: a list that has found nothing offers no "Why?".
    - The action opens the Why sheet at Your target, with focus on its choice (`BracketSheet.openAt: "target"`, through Base UI's `initialFocus`; `Modal` gained the prop).
    - A game without `brackets` would show its budget alone, or nothing.
  - **The budget** starts from the deck's goal; a pick of the owner's own wins until it is saved.
    - "Save as this deck's budget" is a link-button under the control. It shows only while the pick differs, and writes `withBudget(goals, …)` (a total stays) through the editor's goals edit: one PATCH, `updated_at` unmoved (checked on dev). The control then follows the goal again.
    - **In a draft, saving mints the row like a target**: one POST carrying the goals, one PUT, no PATCH (pinned).
  - **"N hidden by your goals · Show"**: Show lists them right there in rank order, dashed, each with its reasons and an Add (it is the owner's deck); Hide folds them. **The toggle lasts the session**: a reload hides them again, because goals are the default.
  - **Flags** ride each row under its sources, with a gauge glyph on bracket lines.
  - **The fetch key carries the goals** (canonical, the exceptions left out), so a goals change asks again once it has saved. A draft sends its goals in its body.
- **Undo toasts.** Suggestions' adds go through `handleSuggestionAdd`: the panels' path plus `notify("Added X")`, whose Undo sets the previous quantity back (a real edit). `useResolvedAdd` takes `announce: false`, so the live line keeps only failures.
  - **Deviation: the Combo Radar keeps its notices**, though D7 names its adds too. Its combo-piece adds already toast once per batch, and a per-card toast would stack on top. That goes with Y6b's Radar work (LATER row 179).
- **The Why sheet** shows the owner's goals line under its lead when a target or a budget is set; it is the budget's one place in the sheet. Its "Change" brings Your target into view and focuses it.

### Y6b decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D7 (2026-10-10, `4381110`)

D7's Y6b half shipped: goals in Autofill and badges on the Combo Radar, with LATER row 179 folded in. No migration, no route, no dependency; the route table is byte-identical. The planner's fixed-seed golden landed first (`a9a4ea8`, recorded on `fb435ec`). What the package decided:

- **The caps are Y6a's rules, run against the growing list.** A pure gate in core (`src/lib/recommend/shell-goals.ts`, `shellGoals`) answers one question before the planner takes a card: would it sit above the target in the list as it stands — the leaders, the kept cards and every pick so far? It asks the adapter's `impact` with that list (and `assess` over it, as `BracketImpactInput` documents), so core names no rule. That yields WAVE4's caps without a caps table:
  - Game Changers: none at targets 1–2; three in all at 3, the kept ones counting ("3 minus the deck's current count");
  - land denial: none at 1–3, clear or edge;
  - extra-turn cards: none at 1; one at 2–3, a kept one counting;
  - combos above the target: never, firm or a call — a template combo counts at what its yes would mean, as in Y6a.
  - A question the player answered counts as answered (no admits, yes is firm), like Suggestions.
  - At targets 4 and 5 nothing is above the target: the shell is the no-goals plan (pinned in the route test).
- **Goals filter before sampling; the sampler is untouched.** Tier C's window is built only from cards that fit *together* — each judged against the picks so far and the window cards admitted before it — so whatever the batch sample takes stays inside the target. No re-sample, no running cap after the sample; `sampleWeighted` still consumes one `rand()` per window card, and `Math.random` never runs (pinned). Tiers A and B, borrowed slots and lands are checked one card at a time. With goals absent (or a check that admits everything) the windows, the stream and the shell are exactly the old ones: the golden for seeds 42 / 1,234,567 / 7, and four live shells byte-identical before and after (dev), prod's on the new code equal to dev's on `fb435ec`.
- **Two picks that complete a combo together are caught.** The one-away scan only knows combos one card from the kept list. With a target, the route loads every combo complete within keep ∪ the candidate pool — one `loadCompleteCombos` statement beside the tournament read (16 → 17 statements, only with a target). Measured: 904 ids → 52,631 piece rows → 341 combos in 77 ms server-side, sorts in memory; 89 ms at 1,000 ids. Basic lands are never a combo piece (measured 0), so fillers can't complete one.
- **The lock tier off at targets ≤ 3** (WAVE4 F) is `AutofillMeta.lockMinTarget` — Magic declares 4 (Optimized, the first bracket without a Game Changer allowance; pinned against the ruleset). Below it a measured staple is ranked like any card; the weights are unchanged. The base pool's lock order goes with it. No note: nothing is "skipped" by it.
- **The notes count against the same seed's no-goals shell.** The route plans that shell too and walks it against the gate in pick order: a pick that would sit above the target counts under each rule it breaks and stays out of the list the next ones are judged against, so a cap counts only what passed it. The words are the adapter's (`BracketsMeta.shellNotes`), one line per rule in the read's order, each naming its source: "Skipped 4 Game Changers — your Bracket 2 target allows none (Wizards' list)", "… allows up to three", "Skipped 1 land-denial card — Wizards expects no mass land denial at Brackets 1–3 (Scryfall Tagger)", "Skipped 2 extra-turn cards — Bracket 1 expects none" / "Skipped 1 more extra-turn card — Brackets 2 and 3 avoid chaining extra turns", "Skipped 3 cards that would complete a combo above your Bracket 2 target (Commander Spellbook)". Guarded with D0's copy rules in `bracket-shell.test.ts` — the shortfall notes keep their own pins.
- **WAVE4 E's acceptance, pinned as "never past the target"** (deviation in wording). A target-2 shell's read reads "Your target: Bracket 2 · nothing here goes past Core" — or "· the cards say at least 2" when the one extra-turn card Bracket 2 allows was picked, as on dev (Gonti's Aether Heart). Pinned in Vitest with the real adapter's `assess` and `line`, seen live in `smoke:autofill` (Atraxa) and in the editor on dev.
- **The body and the sheet.** The POST takes `goals`, zod-checked by `goalsSchema` like the Suggestions draft POST ("Invalid goals" 400 before any read); the sheet sends `suggestionGoals(goals)` — target, budget, answers — only when there are any, so a deck without goals sends today's exact body (its `toEqual` pin unchanged). The budget filter stays `budgetUsd`, the sheet's own control, which now starts from the deck's per-card goal; the goal's budget in the body filters nothing. The sheet is one-shot: no "Save as this deck's budget". With a target or a budget, a read-only goals line ("Your goals: Bracket 2 (Core) · ≤ $5 a card") sits above the controls — no Change: the Why sheet is another dialog.
- **The Radar's badges** (deviation in wording: D7's "Bracket 4+"). `BracketsMeta.comboBadge(combo, commanderIds, answers)` reads one combo exactly as `readCombo` reads it in a list (pieces standing in for cards — a combo's reading never takes their flags) and is pinned against `assessBracket` over a finished list holding it:
  - firm: "This combo alone makes a deck at least Bracket 4"; a two-card combo says so ("A two-card combo with your commander alone makes a deck at least Bracket 3");
  - a call: "This combo alone makes a deck Bracket 3 or 4 — your call"; a template or a commander raise alone: "This combo could make a deck Bracket 4 — your call";
  - a rating not ingested: "Couldn't check how this combo is rated"; a combo that raises nothing and asks nothing gets no badge.
  - The panel appends " — Commander Spellbook" (D0's credit) and core's "above your target" (amber) when either level is above the deck's target, with the Suggestions flags' gauge glyph.
  - Computed client-side from the deck route's `tag` and `relevant` (Y6a put them on the wire), so no route changed. **Badges never filter**: the rows are the route's, membership identical with and without a target (pinned), and the Cut Coach reads the same `inDeck`. The commander rows carry none — the card route's public `ComboView` has no rating (LATER row 180).
- **LATER row 179 fired.** The editor announces the Radar's adds: one card through `handleUndoableAdd` (Y6a's `handleSuggestionAdd`, renamed for its two panels), the live line silent on success; a combo's pieces through `handleComboPiecesAdd` — one toast, "Added 2 combo pieces · Undo", whose one Undo sets each piece back to the quantity it had (per piece, so an edit made meanwhile survives). Suggest full list still adds quietly.
- **One equivalent mutant, disclosed.** Of 27 mutation checks 26 were caught. The survivor drops the picks from the gate's per-list combo set: that set only feeds `assess`, whose read `impact` uses for its no-target line, and the gate only ever runs with a target. Kept exact anyway, because `BracketImpactInput.read` is documented as `assess` over the same list.

### Y7a decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D8 (2026-10-10, `c269d84`, `6ca1f3b`, `b81bbde`)

D8's Y7a half shipped: Tagger roles in `attrs`, `POST /api/alternatives` and the Card tab's Alternatives. No migration, exactly one new route, no dependency. What the package decided:

- **The whitelist, measured on the 2026-10-10 bulk** (4,571 tags, all `oracle` type). Seventeen roles in `src/lib/games/mtg/roles.ts`, each a Tagger tag pinned by UUID with our key and our label: ramp, mana rock, mana dork, land ramp, card draw (`draw`), tutor (`tutor` minus `tutor-land`), creature / artifact / enchantment removal (`removal-*`), **spot removal** (`spot-removal`, beyond D8's list: it tells a Swords from a Wrath), board wipe (`sweeper`), counterspell, burn, protection, recursion (`recursion` minus `recursion-self` and `recursion-land`), token maker (`repeatable-token-generator`) and sacrifice outlet (`repeatable-sacrifice-outlet`; the root's one-shot sacrifice costs aren't outlets).
  - Every role holds 350–5,300 commander-legal ranked nonland cards. 17,627 of the 35,373 live identities hold at least one (one role 8,525; two 4,605; three 3,072; four or more 1,425), 0.65 MB of role text.
  - An `except` tag subtracts its whole roll-up, not just its node: `reanimate-self` sits under both `reanimate` and `recursion-self`, so a node skip kept Gravecrawler as "recursion".
  - Parents and children are both roles (every mana rock is also ramp). A card holds every role it reaches, sorted and deduplicated, so a quiet night writes the same array.
- **The overrides file** gains a sibling `roles` key that lists every declared role with `enabled`, the kill-switch; `flags` stays exactly `mld` + `extra_turn` (the "unknown flag" pin is unchanged). The UUIDs live in the adapter module (D8: "declared in the Magic adapter by UUID"), the switches in the file. `reviewed` moved to 2026-10-10. No per-card review for roles yet (LATER row 182).
- **The fallback per role** is D3's: fresh / kept / off. A failed read, a tag missing from the bulk or tagging nothing, or a missing except tag keeps that role's stored cards and carries its `stale_since`; off writes it for no card. `stats.tagger.roles` (status, stale_since, counts, cards, error) sits beside the flags' fields, so the bracket freshness reader never sees a role. The roles never reach the bracket read: `flagPaths` is still `game_changer`, `mld`, `extra_turn`.
- **The wire carries the roles** (attrs travel whole, as D3's flags do): about 39 bytes per role holder. Only the route's answer uses them.
- **The match in SQL** is one `attrs @> {"roles": [key]}` per role, OR-ed, so `ci_attrs_gin` serves each as a bitmap scan (`?|` couldn't use it). For Sol Ring in a WUG precon the plan is a BitmapOr over the two roles (2,407 + 392 rows) AND-ed with `ci_browse` (game + the cost window): 410 heap rows, 4.1 ms, 1,731 buffer hits. The pool orders by the shared-role count first (`candidatePoolOrder`), then popularity.
- **The scope whitelist's range**: `{column: "cost_value", op: "between", min, max}` with integers and min ≤ max, else it throws; `scope` now takes a list, AND-ed. The window is the card's mana value ±1, floored at 0, and absent when the card has no cost. Offline over the bulk, the gold set read 76% at ±0, 87% at ±1 and 82% at ±2.
- **The candidates**: `candidateConditions` as Suggestions apply them, plus the adapter's nonland scope, the window and "shares a role". The role pool needs a popularity rank, like Suggestions' pool; the tournament and combo sources may bring unranked cards that share a role. The card itself and the list's other cards are never offered.
- **Lands are never offered** (`swap.offers`): a land swaps on color and count, which roles can't see, and a land-for-spell swap changes the land count. LATER row 31's trigger stays unfired. Candidates are nonland too.
- **The order (deviation in detail)**: shared roles first, then `rankCandidates`' score over popularity, tournament play and combos. **The curve template is left out** (D8's list doesn't name it): measured once the roles landed, it put Chrome Mox, Mox Amber, Mox Opal and Mox Diamond above Fellwar Stone and Mind Stone for Sol Ring in a precon with no zero-drops (`6ca1f3b`). A "fewest extra roles" tie-break was measured and dropped: it moved the gold set by 0.8 points and buried Path to Exile under obscure exact matches.
- **A swap is not an add.** The gather, the one-away combo scan and the goals read all run on the list without one copy of the card (`withoutOneCopy`), so a Game Changer for a Game Changer at target 3 stays in and a combo the card was part of isn't "completed" by its replacement. The complete-combo read runs once, on the whole list, for both the tradeoff and the goals read: Magic's read counts a combo only while every piece is in the list, so the card's combos leave with it (a filter for that was an equivalent mutant and was removed).
- **The evidence**: each row leads with "Both: ramp · mana rock — community-tagged on Scryfall Tagger" (source `scryfall_tagger`, confidence medium, credited and linked in `sources`), then the ranker's own lines. `shared` rides each row so the UI links every role to its tag. The words never claim sameness (guarded in `swap.test.ts`).
- **The tradeoff** is `rankCuts` over the whole snapshot, the card's lines only: a combo it breaks, its popularity and tournament keep lines, curve slack. Never the user-tag role line (the snapshot carries ids and copies only). `null` when the card has none. Tournament presence follows the combos route's rule.
- **Goals**: `applyGoals` against the read of the list without the card; the budget flags as in Suggestions. Hidden cards are counted and shown on request ("1 hidden by your goals · Show": dashed rows with their reasons and a Swap).
- **The route**: body `{game, format, leaderIds, entries, cardId, goals?}` (the precedents' `entries`, not D8's `entryIds`). 400 for One Piece, a card outside the entries (leaders included), unknown ids, bad goals or a malformed body. Honest empties carry `reason`: `no-roles`, `not-offered`, `goals`, `none`. Each row carries its CardWire with legality. Bucket `alternatives`, 30/min + 200/hour. Eight rows (`ALTERNATIVES_LIMIT`). Twelve statements warm — the two bucket upserts, the facts, the role pool, the combo scan's three, the complete combos, the tournament reads, the wires' two; a land answers in three.
- **The Card tab**: "Alternatives" sits between the footer line and Printings, only for a main-list card the adapter offers (never a commander, a land, a search preview, or anything in One Piece). It is closed by default and asks on open; each answer is kept per list (a list edited while it is open asks again); the trigger reads "Alternatives · N" once answered. Without a leader it says "Add a Commander first — alternatives follow its color identity." and asks nothing.
- **The swap (deviation)**: one copy out, one in, in place (`swapCard`). The row keeps its position and the new card arrives without the old row's tags or printing; the count never moves. **The Undo is per card** (Y6b's per-piece rule), not `applyListSwap`'s whole-list restore: the new card goes back to its quantity and the old row comes back exactly (tags, printing, place), so an edit made in between survives. The toast reads "Swapped A → B · Undo", and the pane then shows the new card.
- **Chips**: "Mana value N" and "Game Changer (Wizards' list)" / "Not a Game Changer" are the adapter's (`swap.chips`); "$0.60 less" / "$1.16 more" / "Same price" are core's.
- **A key collision found in the dev pass**: the section first shared `card.id` with TagEditor, its sibling, so React logged a duplicate key for every main-list card (`b81bbde`). The editor test now fails on that log.
- **Thirty-five mutation checks, all caught.**

### Y7b decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D8 (2026-10-10, `053130f`)

D8's Y7b half shipped: a swap made from where the player already is, so a full deck never passes through 99 or 101 — "Swap…" on deck rows, "Swap in…" at the maximum, and "Swap…" on the conflict callout's cards. No migration, no route (the route table byte-identical), no dependency. What the package decided:

- **A sheet of its own, not the Card tab.** A row's "Swap…" and the callout's "Swap {card}…" open the swap sheet ("Swap X": Y7a's ask and rows, now shared as `useAlternatives` + `AlternativesContent` in `alternatives-section.tsx`); an add at the maximum opens the swap-in sheet ("Swap in X"). Both live in `src/components/editor/swap-sheet.tsx` and take the editor's dialog slot (`"swap"`, `"swap-in"`) — a bottom Drawer on phones, a Modal from md, the Why sheet's split — so the hotkeys stand down. The Card tab was the cheaper reuse, but on a phone two Card panes are mounted (the Tools tab and the card sheet), and the Alternatives block sits under a ~400 px card image at every tier. The Card tab's Alternatives are unchanged.
- **Row "Swap…"**: a ⇄ icon button (`aria-label` "Swap X…", `title` "Swap…", `data-swap-row`) between the pips and the remove ✕, revealed on hover / focus like remove, always visible and 44 px on touch. Only main-list cards the adapter offers get one (Magic: never a land or the commander); a refused row keeps a same-width spacer so the pip column stays aligned. The share page passes no handler, so no row there has one. **The grid gets none**: its top corners carry the badges, its bottom edge the artist / © line, and a card's click already opens the Card tab's Alternatives (LATER row 190). Measured cost at 390 px: an editable row's name keeps ~94 px (it had ~142) — LATER row 189.
- **"Swap in…"**: the trigger is the list at (or past) the format's maximum (`deckSizeCount ≥ deckSize.max`) in a game declaring both `swap` and `cuts`. Search's Add reads "Swap in…" (`aria-label` "Swap in X…"); Enter and a double-click open the sheet too, and the hint reads "Enter swap in". Suggestions' Add and the Radar's one-card Add read "Swap in…" and open the same sheet; their tab stays put (the panels' quiet preview). **Unchanged at the maximum**: the commander button (a second commander changes the colors — LATER row 186) and the Radar's "Add N pieces" (N in need N out — LATER row 187); both still go over, where the Cut Coach's "Over by N" takes them. A typed quantity swaps one copy in ("One copy at a time while the deck is full."), and a card already in the list says it adds another copy.
- **The cut partner** (`planSwapIn`, `src/lib/recommend/swap-in.ts`, pure): `rankCuts` over the list (the user's tags included, the leader zone excluded) → the first cut in the incoming card's curve bucket that isn't a complete-combo piece; the bucket clamps to the last ("7+") in core as well as in Magic's `bucketOf`. The sheet names it under "Suggested cut" with its tradeoff's lead line and sources, focus on its Swap — Enter, Enter from the search box. "Choose another card": the bucket's other cards ("Also at mana value 2", combo pieces marked and last), then "Show the other N cards" — every main-list card in the Coach's order, the cards it can't rank after them by name ("No signal data"). The incoming card is never its own partner.
- **No partner, said plainly**: an incoming card without a bucket names none — Magic's `swap.unmatched`: "A land gets no suggested partner — which land to cut depends on its colors. Choose the card to cut." (LATER row 31 stays unfired) or "X has no mana cost to match — choose the card to cut." A bucket without an ordinary cut says "No suggested cut at mana value 1 — choose the card to cut." Either way the whole list shows at once. `SwapMeta` gained `bucketName` ("mana value 3") and `unmatched` for these words.
- **Combo protection without a request**: the bracket line's combo facts (Y4a's GET, already fetched for the read) are mapped into the Coach's combo input exactly as the alternatives route maps them (`cutCombosFromFacts`). Without facts the sheet says so in the Coach's words: "Checking combos — this suggestion doesn't protect them yet." / "Couldn't check combos — this suggestion doesn't protect them." / "Combo warnings need a Commander — this suggestion doesn't protect combos yet." Tournament play isn't read (the combos route's block is a saved-deck read) — LATER row 185.
- **One swap, one toast, one Undo** — every door lands in `swapOne` (Y7a's `handleSwap`, generalized to an incoming `EditorCard`): one copy out, one in, in place; the toast reads "Swapped A → B" (out → in) whichever way the swap was started, and its one Undo puts the incoming card back to its previous quantity and the outgoing row back exactly — tags, printing, place. Live on dev: a swap-in and its Undo left a precon list identical to the precon's own, all 85 rows in order. `applyListSwap` is untouched (Import's and Autofill's pins green).
- **The callout**: one "Swap {card}…" per card a conflicting finding names that the editor can swap (a main-list card the adapter offers — never the commander, even a Game Changer commander, nor a land), small outline buttons under the finding's "what would change it"; the findings list above gets none. It closes the Why sheet and opens the swap sheet for that card, under the deck's goals. **Not inside the Why sheet**: a modal keeps the toast's Undo out of reach (Base UI's modal blocks outside pointer events), and the bracket line beside the list shows the new read at once.
- **Focus returns after the closing commit** (`closeFocusRef`, resolved in an effect when the dialog slot empties), not through Base UI's `finalFocus`: Base UI resolves its target in the closing commit, before the DOM is final — its "Why?" vanished a moment later as the line went to "Checking combos…". A row's sheet goes back to the row's "Swap…" (cancelled) or to the new card's row "Swap…" (swapped) — named, since a click doesn't focus a button everywhere (Safari). A search swap-in goes back to the box (cleared once a swap lands, as an add clears it). The callout's goes to "Why?", or — while the new list's facts are checked — to the bracket line itself (now `tabIndex={-1}`, one Tab before "Why?"). A panel's swap-in keeps Base UI's default.
- **Empties**: the swap sheet shows Y7a's honest ones — "No cards tagged like this one fit your goals.", and "No community roles for this card yet" (Seedborn Muse, a Game Changer with none of the 17 roles, is such a dead end at a Bracket 2 target).
- **Pins rewritten, not dropped**: three older tests added a card to a 100-card list — now "Swap in…". Y6a's Suggestions add and Y6b's Radar add step one Wastes out first (below the maximum an add is still "Added X · Undo"); W8b's precon test makes its first edit a swap-in (one create + one PUT, 100 cards, both printings kept).
- **Forty mutation checks, all caught** — four survived the first pass. Three needed new pins: the core's bucket clamp (Magic's own `bucketOf` clamps, so a game-ignorant curve pins it), the partner's initial focus (a Modal's first tabbable is the partner's Swap anyway; the phone Drawer, whose Close comes first, pins it) and the callout never offering a Game Changer commander. The fourth was a redundant guard, folded into one.

### Y8 decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D9 (2026-10-10, `c12c6c8`)

D9 shipped: the deck total joins goals, "Fit my budget" fits a deck under it through reviewed swaps, and every goals line reads the running estimate. No migration (`totalUsd` was in the schema and zod since Y4b), no route (the route table byte-identical), no dependency. What the package decided:

- **The total is set in the Fit sheet, and only there.** "Deck total": a whole-dollar field with presets ($50 · $100 · $150 · $250, `TOTAL_PRESETS_USD` in `budget.ts`, beside the per-card vocabulary), committed by Fit, Enter or a preset — typing commits nothing. A committed total is the plan's target, not yet the deck's: **Apply saves it with the swaps** (one autosave burst), "Save $150 as this deck's total" saves it alone when the deck already fits, and "Remove this deck's total" clears it. Not in Suggestions' budget row (already full at 320 px, and the per-card tier is a filter there, not a target) nor the Why sheet (the bracket's sheet; it opens only once the list has a finding). Opening and reviewing never write: a draft mints its row on the apply, exactly once, the total in its create.
- **The goals line reads the total everywhere it appears** — Suggestions, the Why sheet, the Autofill sheet: "Your goals: Bracket 3 (Upgraded) · ≤ $5 a card · est. $212 of your $150", "(+3 unpriced)" when cards lack a price, whole dollars except a near miss ("est. $150.40 of your $150"). The phrase is built in `src/lib/recommend/fit.ts` and handed to `goalsParts` ready-made, because the bracket copy guard bans "est." — a price is not a bracket word. The Why and Autofill sheets now show the line for a total alone. The budget stays owner-only: `publicGoals` and `tableGoals` drop it (pinned), and the share page's sheet shows no goals line.
- **The doors**: More → "Fit my budget…" (games declaring `swap`; One Piece shows nothing), and Suggestions' goals line gains "Fit my budget" while the estimate is over the total. The More list pin gained the item after Autofill….
- **No route reads the total — and none is sent it.** `suggestionGoals` now drops `totalUsd` with the exceptions line, so every snapshot body (Suggestions, Autofill, Swap Lab, the Fit sheet's own asks) is byte-identical with or without a total, and a new total never asks anything again. Autofill's own total budget stays deferred (LATER row 83).
- **The estimate** (`deckEstimate`): Σ qty × `cheapestUsd` over the format's counted zones, the commander included (`deckOwnership`'s arithmetic with an empty owned set); owned copies $0 **only with a collection**; a card without a price is left out and counted beside the sum, never $0.
- **Which cards are considered** (`fitConsidered`, priciest first): main-list cards the adapter offers (no lands — LATER row 31 unfired; never the commander), with a price, holding one of the adapter's roles **read off the card wire** (so a card the route would answer "no-roles" is never asked about — 5 of the Angels precon's 8 priciest cards hold none), not owned (with a collection), not a piece of a complete combo in the bracket line's facts (no request of its own — Y7b's rule; without facts the sheet says combo pieces aren't kept out), and run as a single copy. The pricey cards it skipped are named ("Not looked at: Serra Ascendant (no community roles yet), Thassa's Oracle (a combo piece)").
- **The asks (no new route)**: Swap Lab's `POST /api/alternatives`, one card per POST, **three at a time, priciest first, only while the checked swaps fall short**, nine cards per open at most (`FIT_ASK_LIMIT` — under a third of the 30/min bucket). Why not a batch body: per-card answers are already 12 statements and ~0.6 s warm on prod, a batch of three lands in about one round trip, and a batch body would hold one serverless invocation for up to 108 statements and need rate accounting of its own. A 429 stops the asking with Y7a's words and a Try again that asks only the failed cards. Every open asks afresh (LATER row 188, annotated).
- **The options per card**: the route's rows in its order (shared roles, then the evidence) that are priced, **cheaper** than the card and **inside the per-card budget** (a row flagged "budget" is never offered; hidden rows never reach the sheet) — the first three. A bracket flag (no target) shows on its option.
- **The picks**: the fewest swaps of the priciest cards that reach the total — each takes its **best-ranked** option, and the sheet stops checking once the estimate fits. **When the best-ranked picks can't reach the total, each card takes its cheapest of the three instead** — said aloud ("The best-ranked swaps can't reach your total, so each card takes its cheapest alternative.") — but only when that gets closer. Measured on the Angels precon at $150: $222 by rank (dev), $214 with the fallback (prod). The player's checks and choices win; the sheet fills around them (unchecking one swap checks the next card). An alternative is never picked twice — "picked for X", disabled — a singleton deck can't hold two.
- **The running total** in the footer: "est. $148 of your $150", or "Gets you to $172 of your $150" when the swaps can't reach it. Incoming cards count at their price — the sheet doesn't know which alternatives you own (LATER row 191).
- **The apply is ONE `applyListSwap`** (D9's word, and the right one: a reviewed batch is a whole-list edit): every checked card out and its pick in, in place (`fitList` over Swap Lab's `swapCard` — the row keeps its position, the new card comes without the old row's tags or printing), one toast "Swapped N cards", **one Undo that restores the whole list exactly** — Import's and Autofill's pins unchanged. The total stays saved after an Undo (a goal, not the list). Apply waits while cards are still being asked.
- **Honest ends**: "No suitable alternative under your goals." (D9, word for word) when nothing can be swapped down — asked cards with nothing cheaper, or no card worth asking about; "Already within your budget — est. $132 of your $150."; "No cheaper alternative under your goals for A, B."; the no-commander sentence. Touch rows are 44 px on a coarse pointer; the Drawer focuses the field only when no total is saved.
- **Live on dev**: Forces of the Imperium (est. $201) at $150 → three asks, three swaps, est. $145; the draft's apply minted exactly one deck (POST 201 carrying `{goals: {v: 1, budget: {totalUsd: 150}}}`, then one PUT); a total saved alone was one goals PATCH with `updated_at` unchanged; a $100 fit (four swaps, the price fallback) and its Undo left the saved list identical (83 rows).
- **Focus**: from More, Base UI returns focus to More; from the goals line, focus comes back to the line after the closing commit (Y7b's `closeFocusRef`) — its "Fit my budget" on a cancel, its "Change" once the deck fits and the door is gone.
- **Sixty-one mutation checks: sixty caught.** The survivor was an equivalent guard — the ask loop's `asked.length < FIT_ASK_LIMIT` (the batch slice already empties at the limit) — folded away. Six pins were added before the run: a land or the commander holding a role is never considered, a plan landing exactly on the total fits, Apply waits while cards are asked, no "Save" for the deck's own total, and the Why and Autofill sheets' lines read the total.

### Y9a decisions and deviations from `WAVE4.md` D10 (2026-10-11, `99c3621`)

D10's Y9a half shipped: a logged game always knows the exact list it was played with. Migration `0018` (`deck_games`; `deck_versions.list_hash` and `kind`, plus a `kind` CHECK the owner approved with the SQL), two routes (the route table gained exactly `ƒ /api/decks/[id]/games` and `ƒ /api/decks/[id]/games/[gameId]`), no dependency. What the package decided:

- **The list hash** (`playableListHash`, `src/lib/decks/list-hash.ts`, pure and isomorphic): the canonical `[zone, cardId, qty]` payload — duplicate keys summed as `diff.ts` sums them, sorted by zone then card in code-unit order (locale-free) — digested with cyrb128 (bryc's public-domain 128-bit string hash) and prefixed `h1:` (35 characters). Not `node:crypto` (the editor may hash the screen in Y10) and not Web Crypto (async). Non-cryptographic on purpose: a hash only ever meets the other versions of one deck. Pinned: equal exactly when `isEmptyDiff` says no card changes (2,000 seeded random pairs, both sides exercised), a printing or tag change keeps it, and the digest itself (two vectors — a refactor that moved it would make every log mint a fresh snapshot).
- **Lazy hashing**: `insertVersion` stamps every new version; every log also stamps, in its own transaction, any of the deck's versions not under the current prefix (the pre-0018 NULLs, and any future `h2:` change) — one `UPDATE … SET list_hash = CASE version WHEN … END`. The versions read returns `cards` only for those rows (`CASE WHEN list_hash LIKE 'h1:%' THEN NULL ELSE cards END`).
- **Reuse**: the NEWEST version with the hash, saved or played. A played snapshot is minted only when none matches, noted "Played {the game's date}" ("Played Oct 10, 2026" — formatted as a calendar date, never shifted by a time zone).
- **The caps**: `insertVersion` counts its own kind — `saved` against the 50 cap (Save version, Restore's safety snapshot, a fork's baseline: unchanged behavior otherwise), `played` against the allowance (100 per deck, `src/lib/journal/limits.ts`, import-free so the dialog shares the number). At the allowance a game still logs: `version` NULL, `list_hash` kept, `capped: true`.
- **A version a game uses is never deleted from History**: the version DELETE runs under the deck lock with a `NOT EXISTS` over the deck's games and answers 409 "A logged game was played with this list, so it stays while the game does." History counts saved versions only ("1 / 50"), lists played snapshots with Compare and Restore but no Delete (they go with their last game). Grouping them under their games is Y10's.
- **The routes**: the gate in D10's order (`requireJournalDeck`) — session 401 "Sign in to keep a game journal" → the per-user buckets → 404 / owner 403 → a guest deck 400 with the same sentence (a signed-in holder of an unclaimed deck's key — signing in on `/account` claims it) → a game without the declaration 400 → zod → the adapter's checks. **Buckets** (windows ≤ 1 day): writes `games:user` 30/min + `games:user-day` 200/day (a game night is 10–20 writes; the day lid bounds a scripted account's snapshots), reads `games-read:user` 60/min. Caching intent: `force-dynamic` + `no-store`.
- **POST** is one transaction: lock → the live list (frozen) → its hash → the versions read (+ the stamps) → reuse or mint → the game insert. Measured with `DB_LOG` on dev: 10 route statements when it mints (2 buckets, the deck, then lock, list, versions, count, bump, version insert, game insert), 7 when it reuses; GET 4 (bucket, deck, the games and one counts statement in parallel); DELETE 6. Every read is index-served and under a millisecond (`deck_games_deck_played`, `deck_versions_deck_version`); dev's 1.5–3.5 s wall time is round trips from the dev machine to Neon. **Response**: `{game, version, minted, capped}` (D10's three plus the game the toast needs; the hash stays server-side).
- **PATCH** changes the details only (date, result, players, turn, table level, seat, opponents, notes — never the version or the list); the adapter's checks run over the whole game after the change ("Seat must be 1–3" when the pod shrinks past the stored seat); `null` clears a detail. **DELETE** (the toast's Undo now, Y10's edits later) removes the game and — under the deck lock — its played snapshot when no other game uses it; a saved version a game reused is never touched. That is D10's "the snapshot it minted if no other game uses it", stated as the general rule (by the invariant, a played snapshot's games are exactly the ones that reused it).
- **The adapter's `journal`** (`JournalMeta` in `types.ts`): `pod {min, max, default}`, `tableLevel?` (Magic reuses `brackets`' noun and levels), `seats(podSize)` → `{seat, label, phrase}` ("2nd" on the choice, "went 2nd" in a line; One Piece "Went first" / "went first"), `opponentLeaders` (2 partners / 1). Core checks a game against it (`journalIssue`, `src/lib/journal/check.ts`); opponents are external keys checked by shape only (Y9b's suggest box picks them).
- **The Games dialog** (`src/components/editor/games-dialog.tsx`, the dialog slot `"games"`; the swap sheets' `SheetShell`, now exported — a bottom Drawer on phones, a Modal from md): "Log a game" at the top — Players (Magic's 2–8 as a Segmented, remembered per browser and game in `deckwarden:journal-pod:<game>`; One Piece shows none), then Won / Lost / Draw, each logging at once; "More details" folded — Date (the device's day; the server takes up to its UTC day + 1), Turn it ended (outside 1–99 it's said in the dialog — "The turn it ended is a whole number from 1 to 99." — and nothing is sent), Bracket (Magic; "Not set" pressed until picked — never stored unless picked), Seat (a Segmented while it fits a 390 px phone, a select for Magic tables of 6+), Notes (500). Opponents wait for Y9b. Below: the allowance line ("Each game keeps the exact list you played — reused when you've saved or played that list before. History keeps up to 100 played lists per deck; they never count against your saved versions."; at the allowance: "This deck keeps 100 played lists — the most it can. A game on a list it hasn't kept yet logs without one.") and the ten most recent games ("Oct 9 · Won · 4 players · went 1st · v7", "no list kept" at the allowance).
- **Flush first**: the editor's `beforeLog` awaits the autosave flush and logs only when nothing is left unsaved; a save that fails logs nothing and the dialog says "Your latest changes didn't save, so the game wasn't logged. Try again." — no fallback to the last saved list (the snapshot must equal the screen). Live on dev: an edit then Won sent `PUT /cards` before `POST /games`.
- **A log closes the dialog** for the toast "Logged a win · Undo" (+ "This deck already keeps 100 played lists, so this game keeps no list of its own." when capped): a modal keeps a toast's Undo out of reach (Y7b's lesson). Focus returns to More (Games… opens only from More). A refused log stays open with the route's words.
