### U9 decisions and deviations from `WAVE5.md` D9 (2026-10-16)

D9's copy policy shipped as one code commit (the pinned list and the unpinned copy together, the owner's choice at pre-flight), then the docs. No migration, no route (the `pnpm build` route table byte-identical), no dependency, no layout change beyond two line breaks named below. What the package decided:

- **The census is rendered lines, not a grep.** A DevTools helper (`.claude/u9-evidence/census.js.txt`, an iframe per route) walks every text node under `main` (and the header and footer apart), groups the nodes by their nearest block container (a row-flex container holds its inline segments as one line; hidden, `display: none` and `.sr-only` text is skipped, as `innerText` would), and counts each line's em dashes, en dashes and middle dots. Over the 15 lab routes plus `/privacy` and `/account`, signed out, dark, at 1440 and 390: **em dashes 64 → 31, en dashes 16 → 12, lines with two or more middle dots 199 → 0 at 1440 (181 of them precon tiles) and 51 → 0 at 390**, the footer's dash on every route gone. Every remaining dash is data or notation, listed: nine deck names on home and one `/d/` H1 ("Talrand — Budget Drake Patrol"), the two hero type lines (Scryfall's "Legendary Creature — Phyrexian Angel Horror"; the One Piece "Leader — Fish-Man / East Blue / Arlong Pirates", the adapter's composition of printed fields in the printed shape), the lone "—" in empty cells (18 on the card page's printings, 1 on the event page's standings) and the W-L-D records "0–2–0" (ten on the hub's finishes, two on the event page), kept as notation with their `title="wins–losses–draws"` legend (LATER row 260). The `<title>` shapes and the OG titles stay by the owner's F3; the site, manifest, `/precons`, `/sets`, `/leaders` and `/tournaments` meta descriptions moved (copy, not titles; seo-smoke pins substrings without the dash). The One Piece posture line's first sentence is Bandai's and stays; its second ("No official card-data API exists … how we source this data") is ours and takes a colon. The footer's Fan Content sentence: Wizards' own template is two sentences, so the dash was ours ("…permitted under the Fan Content Policy. Not approved or endorsed by Wizards of the Coast."), now pinned by a new `site-footer.test.tsx`.
- **The forms, per string, never mechanically.** A period where a finding meets its consequence or a problem its remedy ("Too many requests. Try again shortly.", "Couldn't copy. Select the link and copy it.", "A Game Changer (Wizards' list). Your Bracket 2 target allows none", "Skipped 4 Game Changers. Your Bracket 2 target allows none"); a colon where a label meets its explanation ("Public: on your profile", "Not found: pick a match or leave to skip", "Over by 3: rank cuts", "Untitled: click to name your deck", "Would make this deck at least Bracket 3: a Game Changer (Wizards' list)", "Optional: answer what you know"); a comma for an aside inside a sentence ("as commander or in the 99, not how often it's played", "a share image that unfurls in Discord, with the commander's art for Magic", "Bracket 3 or 4, your call", "one card goes out, so the deck stays at 100"); and **parentheses, a fourth form beyond D9's three**, where the dash set off an aside that the three would flatten: a combo's result ("Combo: Mycosynth Lattice + Vandalblast (mass land denial). Brackets 1 to 3 expect none."), a credit ("{why} (Topdeck.gg)", "{badge words} (Commander Spellbook)", "No community roles for this card yet (Scryfall Tagger)"), the copied table's date ("Bracket 1 to 2 · nothing here goes past Core (checked Oct 5, 2026)"), the disabled game pill ("One Piece (soon)"), the set header ("Bloomburrow (the 102nd expansion set)") and a printing's year ("CMM #410 · Unc. (2023)"). Ranges are "to" everywhere: "Bracket 1 to 2", "Brackets 1 to 3 expect none", "36 to 38 for most decks", "Seat must be 1 to 4", "Usernames are 3 to 20 characters", "rows must be 1 to 10,000"; the validators' open-ended "60–∞" became "at least 60". The home capabilities: "Deal an opening hand in the builder or on any share page, then mulligan until it reads right. Seven cards for Magic, five for One Piece." (two sentences in place of two dashes).
- **The bracket line keeps its one dot and loses the dash; a control follows after a gap.** "Bracket 1 to 2 · nothing here goes past Core", "Bracket 4 (Optimized) · from the cards and your answers", "Bracket 3 or 4 · one combo is your call", "Bracket 3 or 4 · from the cards and your answers, one combo is your call", "Your target: Bracket 2 · the cards say 3 or 4, one combo is your call", "Your target: Bracket 2 · the cards say at least 3. Couldn't check combos", "Played as Bracket 2 (Core) · the cards say 3 or 4, one combo is the owner's call": the number, one dot, the explanation; the lead the editor sets forward is the text before the dot alone (`line-view.ts`'s `LEAD_SEPARATORS` lost its dash), the stale note joins as a sentence ("…your answers. Rules changed since you answered"). Why? and Retry, and the goals line's Change, Set one and Fit my budget, follow their line after a space with `ml-1 underline underline-offset-4`, never after a middle dot: the one-dot ration left no room for a separator before a control, and a text button after a plain space read as prose, so U3a's quiet Why? keeps its muted color and gains the underline (LATER row 262; the mutation check that put the dot back turned 16 tests red). The goals parts join with commas ("Your goals: Bracket 2 (Core), ≤ $5 a card, est. $212 of your $150"). The copied table text: "{deck} · {format}" as its header, "(checked {date})" after the line, "Owner's call: Do these extra turns chain? No: Temporal Manipulation, Time Warp"; the autofill smoke's "Skipped N Game Changers. Your Bracket 2 target allows none (Wizards' list)" regex moved with the string and its "Your target: Bracket 2 · (nothing here goes past Core|the cards say at least 2)" regex held (neither form carried a dash).
- **Tiles take two meta lines (D9's "the tiles drop the dot before Updated", read as a line break):** the chip, the badge, the format and "· {visibility}" on the first, "Updated {date}" or "Released {date}" and "· ≈ $N" on the second, one dot a line, pinned in `deck-tile.test.tsx`. The compact precon row below `md` grows by its second line (the badge alone on the first); the before/after page heights are in the ship note once measured on prod. The account page's bookmark rows take the same shape in one line ("Commander, by Pat · Updated Oct 3").
- **The finishes rows (both hubs and `/tournaments`)**: "1st of 32 at Big Event ↗" (a preposition where a comma would read as a list; the only word U9 added), then "{date} · {record} by {player}, with {partners}": the record keeps its notation and legend, the player follows it after a space, partners after a comma; pinned in the hub page test. The event page's meta line: "{date} · {N} players, top cut {n}, on Topdeck.gg ↗" (the date and count are tournament data by D0; the separators were ours). The `/l/` hero puts the One Piece stat line ("5000 Power · 4 Life") on its own line under the type line, the second line break of the package.
- **Chains became comma lists**: Spellbook's combo results on the card page, the hub, the Combo Radar and the Why sheet ("Infinite card draw, Infinite draw triggers, 2 cards · How it works ↗"; the names keep Spellbook's case, LATER row 261), the suggestion rows' source labels ("Commander Spellbook, Curve template"), a swap's shared roles ("Both: ramp, mana rock. Community-tagged on Scryfall Tagger"), the alternatives' chips, the autofill sheet's source line ("41 from Topdeck.gg, 21 from EDHREC, 37 from Land template"), the diff summary ("+1, -1, 1 qty"), the collection summary ("1,234 cards · 1,500 printings, 1,600 rows, updated Oct 3"), the history row ("v3 (Oct 5, 2026) · 100 cards, 4–1–0") and the journal's game line ("Oct 10 · Won, 4 players, went 1st, v12"). The editor's search hints are a wrapping row with 12 px gaps and no separators (`flex flex-wrap gap-x-3`). The printing caption is "CMM #410 · Unc." (the set code and number read as collectors write them). The One Piece curve's no-cost bucket is labelled "0" (cost-0 and costless cards share it), not "–".
- **The 429 sentences keep "shortly"**, not D9's "in a minute": the limiter's window runs from seconds to an hour and the sheet that knows its wait says it ("Too many autofill requests. Try again in 7 seconds."); the avatar route, whose window is a minute, says "in a minute".
- **Not copy, left alone**: `lib/auth.ts`'s and the ingest scripts' dev-facing errors, `ruleset-watch.ts`'s nightly message, the Spellbook result patterns, the OP adapter's type-line join and the comments that don't quote copy. Comments that quoted a moved string moved with it (`bracket-line.ts`, `copy.ts`, `line-view.ts`, `goals-line.tsx`, `recommendations-panel.tsx`, `bracket-sheet.tsx`, `types.ts`, `brackets.ts`, `printings.ts`, the autofill sheet). `autofill-sheet.tsx` is one of the four source files with a NUL byte: plain `grep` skips it and the candidate list missed its seven lines until the test for its 429 sentence failed (`grep -a`, or `tr -d '\000' | grep`).
- **Pins moved and written**: 91 source files, 55 test files and `scripts/autofill-smoke.ts`; the test pins moved by a mechanical pass (the differing core of every plain-string edit with 12 characters of context, plus regex rules for the template-built lines) and 14 hand edits where the pass fell short; three pins written (the tile's two lines, the finishes row, the footer); five mutation checks caught (the tile's second dot, the hub's "at" back to a dash, the copy table's draft note, the bracket line's dot before Why?, the footer's dash). `warden-copy.test.ts`, the H1, every `<title>` shape and the hubs-smoke, combos-smoke and seo-smoke strings held; `smoke:autofill` (63), `smoke:seo` (92), `smoke:hubs` (51) and `smoke:combos` (24) green on dev after the pass.
- **The dialogs' census**: the Share, import, Games and Why sheets and the 429 toast render through their RTL suites with the new strings (`share-dialog.test.tsx`, `export-dialog.test.tsx`, `import-export` pins in `deck-editor.test.tsx`, `games-dialog.test.tsx`, `bracket-sheet.test.tsx`, `autofill-sheet.test.tsx`'s 429 through its fetch mock); no throwaway page was needed, and the grep over non-test source after the pass found no dashed sentence outside data, notation and dev-facing messages.
- **Left for later**: the editor's leader captions (LATER row 259), the lone "—" cells and the W-L-D records as notation (260), Spellbook's capitalized result names inside comma lists (261), the underlined inline controls against U10's primitives (262); rows 214 and 258 annotated.

### U10a decisions and deviations from `WAVE5.md` D10 (2026-10-16, `540ad26`)

D10's form primitives shipped as one code commit (no U10a-2 split: the arrivals, the dialogs' inputs, the tables, the spinner and the search boxes together), then the docs. No migration, no route (the `pnpm build` route table byte-identical, 83 lines, against a baseline built from the stashed tree in the same run), no dependency, no string move. What the package decided:

- **Six arrivals as hand copies, with headers.** `ui/field.tsx` (Field, FieldLabel, FieldDescription, FieldError, FieldContent), `ui/textarea.tsx`, `ui/checkbox.tsx`, `ui/table.tsx` (the eight parts), `ui/spinner.tsx`, and `ui/input.tsx` kept with a header. Each header names the registry file (`apps/v4/registry/bases/base/ui/<part>.tsx` at `c257f68`, 2026-09-04) and the file the class strings come from (`new-york-v4/ui/<part>.tsx` at the same commit): the base registry's components carry `cn-*` classes that resolve only in the registry's own stylesheet, so the structure and slots are the base files' and the Tailwind is new-york-v4's, with the house rules applied on arrival — the edge token (`--border-control`, U1) in place of `border-input`, no `shadow-xs`, Input's radius, padding and dark fill, and the 44 px coarse-pointer floor (`pointer-coarse:min-h-11` on Input and Textarea, a `pointer-coarse:after:-inset-3.5` hit area around the 16 px Checkbox). Never `shadcn add`.
- **Field renders the registry's parts over `@base-ui/react/field`** — the one structural deviation. The registry's Field is plain markup (a `role="group"` div, a `<label>`, a `<p>`) that leaves `for`/`id` and `aria-describedby` to each site, which is how twelve recipes grew. Over Base UI's Field, a FieldLabel associates by itself, and every FieldDescription and FieldError lands in the control's `aria-describedby` (`form-primitives.test.tsx` pins both, for an Input, a Textarea and a Checkbox). Measured in a throwaway probe: with no ids, the label's `for` and the control's `id` share the generated id in the server HTML; an explicit `id` on an Input inside a Field renders the Field's id until hydration (LATER row 266), so the sites pass none; a Checkbox keeps an explicit id in SSR, and `/sets`' "Main sets only" sets `htmlFor` and `id` both for its server HTML (`sets/page.test.tsx` and `scripts/sets-smoke.ts` now pin `role="checkbox" aria-checked="true"` there in place of the native `<input type="checkbox" … checked="">`: a smoke pin moved, against D10's "no smoke pin moves"). No `role="group"` (Base UI's choice), no `*:w-full` on the vertical stack (an Input is `w-full` by itself and a caller's `w-20` must win).
- **The house density, not the registry's.** A vertical Field puts 4 px between a 12 px muted label (`.type-label-xs text-muted-foreground`, the editor's and the dialogs' caption, F4) and its control, where the registry has 12 px and a 14 px medium label; a horizontal Field (a Checkbox and its words) is the 14 px row the sheets and `/sets` had, 8 px apart, the label `font: inherit` from the row (the Suggestions toggle keeps its pane's 12 px by passing `text-xs` to the Field). FieldLabel is `block`, not the registry's `flex items-center gap-2`: a label here is prose, sometimes with an inline code span ("Type **delete my account** to confirm:"), and a flex label turns its text nodes into gapped items. FieldDescription is the muted 12 px line (`.type-meta`) D1 named; FieldError is the destructive 12 px line with the registry's `role="alert"`, rendered only with content, always when there is (Base UI's `match`); the sites own their strings and timing.
- **Checkbox is a native `<button type="button" role="checkbox">`** (`nativeButton`, the registry renders a span), so `disabled` is the real attribute the `disabled:` variants and the tests read (the Suggestions test's `toggle.disabled` held), with Base UI's hidden `<input type="checkbox">` beside it for forms. The box draws the edge token in every state: the registry moves the border to `primary` when checked, which is 1.84:1 on the dark page (W1's reason for the buttons' gold hairline), so the fill is primary and the line stays the token (3.49:1 dark, 3.36 light); an indeterminate box shows a minus (the autofill group box, which used to set `indeterminate` through a ref). The pins that read `.checked` moved to `aria-checked` (`sets-index-view.test.tsx`, `fit-sheet.test.tsx`).
- **Textarea is a Field control** (`Field.Control` with `render={<textarea/>}`, what Base UI's Input is underneath), so a FieldLabel reaches it and a FieldError describes it, and the import dialog's `ref` still focuses it on open. The registry's `field-sizing-content` is left out: every site sets `rows` (a paste area invites a paste at twelve lines, not four), the notes keep `resize-y`, the import and export areas keep their 12 px mono (`text-xs md:text-xs`).
- **Table** keeps the staples' density (`py-1.5 pr-3`, a muted header row, the last column flush) in place of the registry's `p-2`/`h-10`, and leaves out the row hover tint, `transition-colors` and `"use client"` (read tables on page-like surfaces, D0; the hub's rows are ISR HTML); cells don't force `whitespace-nowrap`. `<tr hidden>` passes through: the hub page test's 101 rows with 75 hidden and "Show all 100" held once its regex allowed TableRow's attributes before `hidden`. Taken: the hub's `StaplesTable`, its sibling `MetaLensTable` on the same page (one look per page, beyond D10's two), and the analytics `DataTable` (every `kind: "table"` block: Mana sources and the One Piece blocks, at their 12 px, 2 px rows). Left: the card page's printings table and the event page's standings (LATER row 263).
- **Spinner** is lucide's `Loader2Icon` with `animate-spin` — one CSS rotation, linear by nature: a spinner turns at a constant rate and an eased turn stutters, so it is the documented exception to U2's four curves; its reduced-motion form is the icon at rest (`motion-reduce:animate-none`) with the button's label still saying what it waits for. Inside a labelled busy button the caller passes `aria-hidden` and no `role` (a screen reader hears "Loading…" once). Sites: the editor's Surprise button while it rolls (`deck-list-pane.tsx`, `aria-busy`) and the card page's "Show all N printings" while the tail loads. The home Surprise door is a link whose inner HTML seo-smoke pins to its label exactly: no spinner there.
- **D1's reason and the 429 wait as FieldDescription.** The Suggestions "Only cards I own" toggle is a Checkbox in a horizontal Field, `disabled` on the Field without a collection; the reason its `title` carried on hover ("Sign in and import a collection on your Account page to filter by cards you own") renders as a FieldDescription under it with the Account link inside the sentence (the link text "import a collection" byte-identical, the old "· import a collection" fragment gone), on every pointer rather than D1's coarse-only: the site already showed a visible hint on every pointer, and the description is in the toggle's `aria-describedby`. The autofill sheet's Retry keeps its `title` and, for as long as the 429 lock runs, a FieldDescription beside it with the same reason, `aria-describedby` on the button (LATER row 216 closed: a control change during the window can replace the 429 sentence while Retry stays locked, and the line under Retry now always says why). The 429 sentence itself stays the sheet's 14 px destructive line, not a FieldError: it is the sheet's state, not a field's. The username form's "Saved. Your profile is live." is a FieldDescription (`aria-live="polite"` kept), its error a FieldError; the delete-account confirmation and the import paste area take FieldError (`role="alert"` in place of `aria-live="polite"`; no test pinned the live region).
- **The census, both ways.** `grep -rn -a '<input' src --include='*.tsx' | grep -v test` found 27 tags in 18 files at `aa61bf1` (the prompt's 23 in 14 missed `/leaders`' hidden field, the delete-account box, `/cards`' Traits box and the card pane's tag pill). Recipes folded onto Input: 11 `<input>` boxes in 10 files (delete-account, username, the folder rename, the new-folder box, the Games dialog's date and turn, the Share link, the History note, the editor's card search, `/cards`' Traits, the tag pill); three native `<select>` recipes (the Games dialog's Choice, the folder visibility, `/precons`' sort and year) and `/cards`' shared `FIELD_CLASS` (the name box, the Set picker, the Type select) moved onto Input's recipe at their heights; `ui/autocomplete.tsx`'s copied class string (X2's precedent) followed Input onto the token and the 44 px floor — U1 had moved Input alone, and the name boxes on `/commanders`, `/leaders`, `/cards`, the picture chooser and the Games dialog's opponents field measured 1.47:1 until now. Five textareas, six checkboxes. Left bare, listed: two hidden form fields, seven radios and the collection's file picker (LATER row 264), the deck-name title box in the editor header (borderless by U3a's design; LATER 264). The recipe grep (`className` with `border` and `rounded` on an `<input`) returns none; the tag input is `Input` in the tag pill's shape (`h-auto w-auto rounded-full border-dashed …`, the edge token's dashed line; LATER row 265 for U10b's Chip). `border-input` survives only on the Tabs trigger's dark active state and in comments.
- **Measured** (Chrome for Testing through the DevTools MCP, the `forms.js.txt` census over every text control, textarea, checkbox and select, and `qa-forms.js.txt` driving a throwaway fixtures page through the Games, Details, Share, Import, Export and History dialogs, the account forms, the picture chooser and the delete-account dialog; dev, both themes, 1440 fine and 390 touch). Before: the dialogs' and forms' boxes drew `border-input` at **1.19:1** on a dialog card and **1.47:1** on the page in dark (the `/sets` Input alone read the token at 3.49), 22 to 34 px tall at 1440 and the same on touch, 12 to 14 px text, `rounded-md` 9.6 px, no `aria-describedby` anywhere, the folder-name box with no label at all; the native checkbox a 16 px box with no border and the browser's focus outline. After: every box reads the token — **3.49:1 dark and 3.36 light on the page, 3.36 dark and 3.46 light on a dialog card** — at 32 px (36 on `/cards` and the editor's search, their page heights) and **44 px on touch**, 16 px text on phones (iOS zoom) and 14 at `md`, 12 px radius, placeholders 6.08 to 7.69:1, the focus state the ring on the border; every label associated (`for` on the Fields', `aria-label` where there was one, the folder box named "Folder name"); the Checkbox a 16 px button on the token with a 44 px hit area. Lighthouse accessibility 100 on `/sets` and on the Games dialog open (snapshot). U9's rendered census over nine routes (home, `/commanders`, `/cards`, `/sets`, `/precons`, the hub, the card page, a share page, the editor) identical line for line to U9's after: 29 em, 10 en, 0 multi-dot lines across the nine (the 17-route total was 31 / 12 / 0).
- **Tests**: `form-primitives.test.tsx` (13: the token and the floor on Input and Textarea; a Field's label, description and error wiring for an Input and a Textarea; the horizontal row and its disabled description reaching a Checkbox; the Checkbox as a button with `aria-checked`, indeterminate and disabled, named by a wrapping label; the Table's slots, density and a hidden row; the Spinner at rest and quiet). Pins moved: `sets-index-view.test.tsx` (two `.checked` → `aria-checked`), `sets/page.test.tsx` (the server HTML's checkbox), `fit-sheet.test.tsx` (three), the hub page test (the hidden-row regex), `scripts/sets-smoke.ts` (the server HTML's checkbox). Held: the search pane's wire pins, every label, placeholder and error string, the games, share, export, history, delete-account, change-picture, autofill, recommendations and staples suites unchanged. `smoke:sets` and `smoke:hubs` green on dev during the session, `smoke:seo` (92) after the owner's OK, with `pnpm db:size` 288.0 MB and the census 211 decks = 16 account + 13 guest + 182 precons, 1 user, read before and after it in one `read only` transaction, identical; `pnpm check` 2,353 / 217 / the same 6 warnings.
- **Left for later**: the printings and standings tables (LATER row 263), the radios, the file picker and the deck-name title (264), the tag input's pill shape against U10b's Chip (265), the explicit-id SSR quirk (266); rows 216 closed, 245 and 262 annotated.

### U10b decisions and deviations from `WAVE5.md` D10 (2026-10-16, `8c62acd`)

D10's stepper and chip shipped as one code commit (the owner chose no U10b-2 split at pre-flight), then the docs. No migration, no route (the `pnpm build` route table byte-identical against a baseline built at `1518f54` in the same session), no dependency, no string move. What the package decided:

- **Two arrivals, hand-written headers.** `ui/button-group.tsx` (ButtonGroup, ButtonGroupText) is the registry's `bases/base/ui/button-group.tsx` structure at `c257f68` (a `role="group"` root with `data-slot` and `data-orientation`, ButtonGroupText over Base UI's `useRender`) with `new-york-v4/ui/button-group.tsx`'s class strings, U10a's rule for the `cn-*` classes. `ui/chip.tsx` has no registry source (the registry ships no Chip): it is written over `useRender` the way the registry's own badge is, says so in its header, and carries no `"use client"` (the index pages render their chips on the server; `useRender` calls no hook there). Never `shadcn add`.
- **The group owns the joined shape.** The first child keeps its leading corners, the last its trailing ones, every inner corner is square, a child after the first drops its leading border, a focused child rises to `z-10` — so the radius lands on the group's outer corners only, and the Button sizes keep their own `rounded-md` (D1's "the group brings its own"). Measured: the stepper's minus 9.6 / 0 / 0 / 9.6 px, plus 0 / 9.6 / 9.6 / 0; a hovered minus tints as the left end of one control. The rounded squares with one group edge (the other option D10 left open) would draw a line round every stepper, always visible on touch: 69 lines on the lab's fixture, against D4's lines to space.
- **ButtonGroupText without the plate.** The registry's text is a boxed label (`border bg-muted px-4 shadow-xs`) for outline groups; the house's one group is ghost buttons around a count on every editor row, so the house text is the count's type (14 px medium, tabular, centred) and the stepper passes `w-6`. A caller's `data-slot` wins over the text's own (Base UI merges state attributes before props), so the count keeps `data-slot="qty"`, the slot every pin and the census read. Left out: `ButtonGroupSeparator` (nothing here divides a group) and the registry's Radix hidden-select rule (the house Select is Base UI's, which renders an `<input>`).
- **The reveal stays per button.** D10 asked whether the whole group reveals: it cannot, because the count is the group's middle and never hides (R3, pinned). The two buttons keep `REVEAL_CLASS` (shared with Swap… and remove), 24 px and 44 on touch; the group itself carries no opacity. The group is unnamed: each button already names its card.
- **One Chip, two shapes, one height.** `shape="filter"` is `rounded-md` (9.6 px, the small controls' radius), `shape="tag"` is `rounded-full`; never a third. Every chip is 24 px (`h-6`) and 44 on a coarse pointer; `size` sets only the label (14 px on the pages, 12 px in the editor and the applied filters). Before, chips measured 20 (tags), 22 (presets, the tag input), 24 (filters, year strips, applied filters), 28 (the opponents, the hub links), 29 (One Piece swatch chips) and 30 px (the game switch), with the hub's links 28 px on touch. `variant="outline"` is a line on the edge token with no fill; `variant="muted"` is a fill with no line (an applied tag, an applied filter, a chosen opponent: the value is chosen; its × is the control).
- **The edge token on every chip that draws a line**: 1.47:1 dark and 1.28 light (the default `border`) → 3.49 and 3.36 on the page, in every chip on every route measured. The tag input already read the token (U10a) and keeps the dashed line.
- **The press, scoped.** U2's press on the chip (LATER row 219 closed): a button or a link scales to 0.98 on the settle-120 curve, `motion-safe:` only, with its hover fill held on `:active` (the touch channel); the hover tint and the press are written `[&:is(a,button)]:` so a static span (the current game on `/precons` and `/sets`, an applied tag) neither tints nor squeezes. Measured from the CSSOM: `scale: 0.98` inside `(prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` on every link and button chip, nothing on a span; with reduced motion emulated the media is unmatched, the backstop zeroes the transition (1e-05 s) and the held fill is all that remains. ChipRemove takes the icon-xs press (0.96).
- **One pressed look.** `pressed` is R5a's look for every chip: the muted fill, medium weight and a 2 px ring in the game accent, keyboard focus an offset outline in the same accent. The game switch (`/commanders`, `/leaders`, `/cards`, `/tournaments`) and the static game pills on `/precons` and `/sets` used an inverted fill at 30 px beside the color chips' ring at 24: two "current" looks in one filter block. They take the chip's. The site keeps the ARIA (`aria-current="page"` on links, `aria-pressed` on toggles); `/precons`' "All" button gained `aria-pressed` (it showed the pressed look without saying so).
- **A chip is a control or a removable token, never a label.** The four status pills among D10's 21 recipes (the Cut Coach's and the Swap sheet's "in combo", ConfidenceChip's high / medium / low, Combo Radar's complete / incomplete) are Badges: `Badge variant="outline"` with their tones at 12 px weight 400 (the editor header's precedent), 20 px tall where they were 18, the radius still a full pill. `ui/badge.tsx` is untouched (D10's fence). Their tone lines (1.47 to 1.87 dark, 1.28 to 1.61 light) are not control edges: the word carries the state. The page-level labels outside the editor (the `/l/` hero's color and Standard pills, the card page's legality list, the printings' "Default") stay hand-rolled: LATER row 267.
- **The tag input stays Input** (LATER row 265 closed): a text box keeps Input's focus ring on the border, its 16 px phone text and its 44 px floor; it takes the tag Chip's 24 px (`h-6 py-0`, was 22) so the tags row is one height.
- **U9's inline controls** (Why?, Retry, Change, Set one, Fit my budget): neither primitive is a text button, so they keep their own classes (LATER row 262 annotated).
- **The chip grep, both ways.** D10's first cut over `src/components`: 27 hits at `1518f54` (11 in editor and deck, plus `color-chip`, `game-switch` and `card-search` outside them; the prompt's "14 in editor and deck" counted those three in) → 18, 4 of them in editor and deck, none a chip: the Share dialog's radio rows, the tag Input, the ambient art's credit, the grid view's quantity overlay, the rest `ui/` primitives' own items (menu rows, tabs, tooltip, avatar). The lab's recipe grep (`rounded-(md|full) border` in editor and deck): 21 at `b141432`, 11 at `1518f54` after U10a's inputs → 6, none a chip (the Swap, Fit and alternatives option boxes, the Segmented frame, the completion ring, the tag Input). Every chip-shaped control renders `data-slot="chip"` (the opponents keep `opponent-chip`) with `data-shape`.
- **Tests**: `stepper-chip.test.tsx` (8); pins added in `deck-text-view.test.tsx` (the three parts in one group, remove outside it), `game-switch.test.tsx` (the pressed look), `precons-index-view.test.tsx` ("All" says `aria-pressed`), `swap-sheet.test.tsx` ("in combo" is a Badge). Held, unmoved: the stepper's aria-labels, reveal class, `pointer-coarse:size-11` and button order, `color-chip.test.tsx`'s `ring-accent-game` pins, `card-search`'s filter labels, `games-dialog`'s `opponent-chip` read, `segmented.test.tsx`. `smoke:sets`, `smoke:hubs` and `smoke:seo` (92) green on dev; the census read in one `read only` transaction before and after `smoke:seo`, identical.

### The site header on the editor routes (2026-10-17, the owner's call)

- **Amends §2 "Site shell"'s "The editor keeps its own workspace header (mark plus deck controls). The new-deck chooser renders `SiteHeader` itself above the picker."** The site header stands on every page, the editor routes included: a new `src/app/decks/layout.tsx` renders `SiteHeader` above `/decks/new` (the picker, its Suspense fallback and the draft editor behind `?game=`) and `/decks/[id]/edit`; the picker and the fallback no longer render a header of their own. The editor's workspace bar keeps the deck's controls — the game · format chip, the name, the save slot, Keep this deck / Share, Tools, More — and loses its two duplicates of the site header: the mark → home (the site header's is the way home) and the appearance menu, on every tier (U4a's Appearance group inside the phone's More included; the `phone` prop is gone). The bar is a labelled `<section>` ("Deck toolbar"), not a `<header>`: one banner landmark per page, the site header's. On phones the bar's first row is the name and More over a two-column grid; the status row and a fork's credit line are as U4a left them.
- **The editor's root is the viewport minus the header.** `EditorLayout`'s root goes from `h-dvh` to `h-[calc(100dvh-var(--site-header-height))]`; `--site-header-height: calc(3.5rem + 1px)` (globals.css) mirrors `SiteHeader`'s `h-14` row and its hairline. The document still never scrolls under the workspace, so the header stays put without being sticky; the phone's tab bar, the toast viewport and the credit chip keep their bottom insets. The editor's loading and error states fill the rest of the body (`flex-1`) instead of a full screen under the header.
- **Cost, stated.** A phone's editor gives 57 px of its height to the site header (the name row, the status row and the tab bar stay as they were); a desktop's 57 px sit above the 56 px bar. `BuildLink`'s `aria-current` rule is unchanged (exact paths, U8): Build is the open page on `/decks/new`, a draft's URL, and names nothing on `/decks/[id]/edit`.

### The home intro, once per visit (2026-10-22, the owner's call of 2026-10-20; the commit after `b3b546f`)

- **Amends `WAVE5.md` D6's "no entry animation" and `DESIGN-LAB.md` D4's motion plan ("no entry animation on load") for this one moment, and nothing else they say.** The owner asked on 2026-10-20 for a home intro that plays once per visit, not on every return to home; `DECKCHECK-STUDY.md` §2.1 measured deckwarden's home and prototyped the gate. The purpose is hierarchy: the headline lands first, the sentence after it, the actions are already still. Home's motion stays about one step up the taste dial (hover lifts, plus one 460 ms settle on a tab's first load); deckcheck's 3.2 s, sixteen-animation choreography is the thing not copied.
- **The gate is a pre-paint inline script in the root layout, not a React gate and not a cookie.** The study's prototype rejected a `useSyncExternalStore` gate with a "play" server snapshot (every reload and back replays from the server HTML and hydration cuts it: the H1 at opacity 0.775 and 6.6 px low one frame before the snap) and a session cookie (shared by every tab, so a new tab would not play; sent with every request; one more line on the privacy page). The script (`src/lib/home/intro-gate.ts`, 1,255 bytes inline, `"use strict"` then the two functions' own source called with the constants, next-themes' pattern) is the first child of `<body>`, so it runs during parsing before the hero exists and before first paint, which is what makes the first painted frame the intro's from-state (the 4x filmstrip at 842 ms shows the dimmed, lowered headline, never the final state first); it sits in the ROOT layout because a client navigation never re-creates it, where a page's `<script>` would be inserted inert on every client return with a development error from React 19. `<html>`'s `suppressHydrationWarning` already covers an attribute set before hydration, as it does next-themes' class.
- **The rules, in order:** off `/` → skip (the script runs on every document); reduced motion → skip; the tab's marker (`sessionStorage` `deckwarden:intro` = `1`) → skip; storage that throws on read or write → skip, because an intro the tab cannot remember would replay on every load, the thing the owner asked to avoid. On play the marker is written FIRST, then `data-intro="play"` goes on `<html>`. The attribute is removed when the hero's last `dw-in` ends (an `animationend` listener on the document, by name and inside `[data-slot="home-hero"]` only), with a 250 ms poll from the first frame after `DOMContentLoaded` as the fallback; an animation still play-pending counts as running, because at 4x CPU the two animations sat pending from first paint until the main thread freed (created at 701 ms, running at 823 in the trace). Removing the attribute changes nothing on screen: `dw-in` has no `to` keyframe, so it ends at the elements' own styles. A `window.open` copy and a duplicated tab inherit the marker (Chrome copies `sessionStorage`; recorded, not fought); a document load of another page followed by a client navigation to `/` writes no marker and plays nothing (the intro is a landing moment of a document load of home).
- **The choreography, U2's tokens only:** the H1 and the sentence (`[data-slot="home-hero"] h1` and `h1 + p`, the section's new slot) run `dw-in 400ms var(--ease-settle-400) both` from `--dw-opacity: 0.5` and `--dw-y: 0.3em` (14.4 px on the H1, 5.4 on the sentence), the sentence delayed 60 ms; one hand-written rule in `@layer components` beside `.home-fan`, inside `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`, keyed on `:root[data-intro="play"]`. No blur (the from-state holds while hydration keeps the main thread, and a blurred LCP headline at 390 would sit unreadable for that window; U2 keeps motion to transform and opacity). Never opacity 0 on the H1: at 390 it is the LCP element, and text painted at half opacity is visible, so LCP stays at first paint. The fan does not move: the intro plays on a tab's first load, usually a cold cache, where production paints the fan's images 500 to 740 ms after the H1, after a 460 ms intro has ended (a decode-gated fan motion is LATER row 298; the study's sampler found a uniform rise safe for the artist and © strips). No rotation or scale on anything in the fan, ever: a turned card's box is the LCP element at 1440. The buttons never move and are usable from the first frame; nothing blocks input, nothing is announced, Skip to content stays the first focusable.
- **Measured** (Chrome for Testing through the DevTools MCP, local production builds of `b3b546f` and this tree on :3000, dark, signed out, the day's fan Ghalta (center), Sheoldred (left), Urza (right); 1440×900×1 and 390×844×3 touch; every hero reading is `[data-slot="home-hero"]`'s `getAnimations({ subtree: true })`, never the page's). Before: 0 hero animations on every row of the matrix. After: a fresh tab's first load plays at both widths (the first sampled hero frame at 394 / 385 ms is already the from-state; `animationstart` 508 and 515 / 479 and 486 ms, `animationend` 846 and 911 / 813 and 879, the attribute removed at 912 / 880); client return (hero "Browse commanders", then the wordmark) 0, the H1 at rest on all 313 sampled frames from the one the hero came back on; reload 0 from the first frame; back 0 (a fresh load, never bfcache: `notRestoredReasons` unchanged); a new tab plays; a `window.open` copy carries the marker; another page first then a client navigation to `/` 0 with no marker written; reduced motion 0 with no attribute and no marker, both widths; storage that throws 0 with no attribute. LCP element unchanged at both widths: at 4x CPU and Fast 4G, cache bypassed, 1440 cold 1,542 ms before and 1,542 after (the right turned card, 72,632 px²; FCP 833 / 842; the two `dw-in` runs 823 to 1,222 and 1,280 ms), 390 804 before and 834 after (the H1; the study's band 829 to 939). CLS 0.00 both ways (the one 0.00002 entry is the desktop nav's Geist nudge, as before). The artist and © strips: 0 of 124 points covered on each card at both widths, before and after. Lighthouse (navigation, desktop and mobile): accessibility 100, SEO 100, best practices 96 with the one failure the known CSP eval-probe issue; the console carries that issue (count 2) and nothing else. `smoke:seo` and `smoke:engagement` green on dev; `pnpm check` 2,752 tests / 265 files / the same 6 warnings (2,734 / 264 / 6 before); the route table identical (74 app-path entries).
- **Pins:** `src/lib/home/intro-gate.test.ts` (the decision over raw strings and the three pathnames; the serialized script run through `new Function`, so a closure over module scope would throw as it would in the page; the marker written before the attribute, and no attribute when the write throws; the attribute removed on the last `dw-in`'s end inside the hero only, by the poll when nothing is running or pending, at the first frame when there is no hero, and when the animation list throws; the stylesheet's rule inside one no-preference block beside the fan with `data-intro` nowhere else, `dw-in 400ms` on `settle-400`, the two parameters, the 60 ms delay, and the keyframes on opacity and transform only); `layout.test.tsx` (the script first in `<body>`, Skip to content still the first focusable); the home page test (the hero's slot once, the sentence the `<p>` right after the `</h1>`). Five mutation checks run red: the marker written after the attribute, the pathname check dropped, the reduced-motion check dropped, the attribute never removed, the rule outside the media query.
- **Found on the way:** `AnimationPlayState` has no `"pending"` (TypeScript caught the prompt's wording); play-pending is the `pending` boolean, which the gate reads. The minified server bundle turns block-level function declarations into names that shadow the outer function's, which strict and sloppy scripts treat alike only by Annex B's grace, so the gate's helpers are `const` arrows and the serialized text starts with `"use strict"`.
- **Left for later:** a decode-gated fan motion (LATER row 298), a per-word H1 reveal (299); rows 246, 249, 251 and 285 annotated; row 230 stays closed.

### Three speed facts beside the bracket read (BRACKET-SPEED, 2026-10-22; `DECKCHECK-STUDY.md` §2.2 and §2.3; the commit after `ac981e3`)

- **Amends nothing in the read.** `assessBracket` and `MTG_BRACKET_FLAG_PATHS` are byte-identical and the assumption line "Tutors and fast mana don't change the read" stays true. What is new is said beside the read, never in it: fast mana, tutors and the curve with the land count, as counts anyone holding the list can check (the calibration of 2026-10-21: AUC 0.997 / 0.992 / 0.991 / 0.983 against 182 precons and 11,556 cEDH-named lists), and the owner's existing cEDH question asked when two of them look tuned. No score, no tier, no combined number.
- **The adapter owns the facts and their words; core owns the gate.** `BracketsMeta.speed?` is one optional member: `facts(deck, cards)` returns the facts already said (`rows`, `line`, `tuned`, `nudge`), `label` names the table line's source, `asks` names the How it plays question. The names of card kinds (fast mana, tutors, lands, cEDH) never enter core, which decides only whether the owner sees the nudge. One Piece declares no brackets and so no speed. The numbers themselves live in `src/lib/games/mtg/speed.ts` with the calibration's definitions copy for copy (the main zone is every zone but the leader zone; a land is a FRONT-face type line containing "Land", never `primaryType`, so an artifact land and Dryad Arbor count; fast mana is a nonland card at mana value 1 or less with a ramp, mana-rock or mana-dork role; tutors are the tutor role; the average is over nonland copies, two decimals) and its cuts as named constants with the numbers in the comment (fast mana 6 or more, lands 33 or fewer: 0 of 182 precons past either). Parity with the calibration's own per-list axes: 182 of 182 precons, 904 of 904 sampled archived lists.
- **"How fast it looks"** is the sheet's third section, after What the cards show and before Your target, for owner and visitors alike: "Fast mana: 4 (mana sources costing 1 or less)", "Tutors: 0 (cards that search for other cards, land tutors aside)", "Curve: average mana value 2.95, 37 lands", then "These show how fast the list looks. They don't change the bracket." "At the table" gains one line after the evidence rows and before the owner's: "Speed (Scryfall Tagger roles): 4 fast mana, 0 tutors, average mana value 2.95, 37 lands", in the copied text too. **Decision:** the label's parenthetical names the external source as the other rows' do (Wizards' list, Commander Spellbook, Scryfall Tagger); the counts are the list's own, as every row's are.
- **The nudge is the owner's, on a full list, until the question has any answer.** When the facts cross a cut, How it plays opens itself with the adapter's sentence first, "11 fast mana and 27 lands look like a cEDH list. If it is, say so below." (naming only what crossed), and the cEDH row carries `data-nudged`; Yes goes through the same `withAnswer` and the same goals PATCH as any How it plays answer, raising the read to Bracket 5 as it always did. **Decision, disclosed:** a draft is never nudged, because every unfinished list has 33 or fewer lands (the read's own `draft` status is the gate; the facts still show). **Decision:** the nudge sits inside How it plays rather than as a callout above it, so the question it points at is one Yes away and nothing is said twice. Visitors never see a question: the share page's sheet has no How it plays, and its server HTML carries no nudge.
- **Curve shown, never used to ask.** The calibration's best curve cut (2.96 or less) has a known false positive, a Bracket 2 Marchesa list at 2.95; tutors (4 or more) pass 3 precons. So the two cuts are fast mana and lands only, as the study decided; the test pins the other two never asking.
- **Fixtures gained lands.** The test lists (`list()`, `spec()`, `gcCards`) were all-artifact 100s, which read "0 lands" and would have nudged every owner's sheet; they now carry 36 basic lands (`basicLands`), and the moved pins (`table.test.ts`'s copied text, `deck-share-view.test.tsx`'s `data-line` list and copied text) say so in the same commit.
- **Measured** (dev, DB_LOG on; the built-in pane and Lighthouse's CLI, because the DevTools profile was held by a sibling session): three share pages went from three table lines and two sheet sections to four and three, each speed line equal to the calibration's axes for that list to the digit; statement counts 8 (account deck) and 6 (precon) unchanged; the imported cEDH list's editor showed the nudge, one PATCH took the Yes and the lead moved to "Bracket 5 (cEDH)"; Lighthouse accessibility 1.00 before and after. `pnpm check` 2,812 / 266 / the same 6 warnings; the route table identical (74).
- **Left for later:** the facts on `/precons` tiles (LATER row 300: the tiles' loader selects the flags only, and every precon reads slow anyway), a "speed" answer for the `fast` question (row 301: only cEDH intent was calibrated); row 297 names the parity script as the re-run's starting point.

### The Commander brackets guide page (BRACKETS-PAGE, 2026-10-23; `DECKCHECK-STUDY.md` §2.2; the commit after `f75b5f9`)

- **One public page, `/brackets`, built from deckwarden's own data.** Brackets were explained only inside a deck's Why sheet; a visitor who searches "Commander brackets" now lands on a page that says what each of the five levels allows (off the versioned table the read applies), lists the Game Changers as the database last flagged them (53 small full-card images, each linking `/cards/<id>`), says what the read assumes and what it asks the player, and shows every precon's read as the honest example, ending in a door to the editor's Paste a list import ("Check a deck" → `/decks/new?game=mtg&import=1`) and "Start from a precon". No list input on the page and nothing from another site's guide (the study's boundary). The owner's answers at pre-flight (2026-10-23): not in the Browse menu (LATER row 302 holds the entry), the title and h1 "Commander brackets", the door "Check a deck"; nothing posted, so no beta round went first.
- **The words are the adapter's where they name card kinds; core lays them out.** `BracketsMeta.guide?` is one optional member (`BracketGuideMeta`): `levels` (each level's rows, label and value, straight off `BRACKET_RULESET`'s fields: "Game Changers: Up to three", "Two-card combos: None before turn 6", "Games last: Any number of turns"), `levelsNote` (what a list with none of those cards reads, from the line's own unflagged pair), `list` (the Game Changers: the flag path `["game_changer"]`, one of `flagPaths`; the freshness feed that dates it; the reviewed revision and count; Wizards' page; the allowance sentence "Brackets 1 and 2 allow none, Bracket 3 up to three, Brackets 4 and 5 any number."), `rulesAsOf`, `assumptions(freshness)` (the read's own lines through the same `assumptionsFor` every read calls, so the page and the sheet cannot disagree) and `reasons(read)` (what puts a read past Core: "A Game Changer", "A two-card combo", "A combo Commander Spellbook rates Bracket 3 or higher", "Mass land denial", each kind once, from the factor ids the read itself writes). The level words live in `src/lib/games/mtg/bracket-guide.ts` (no import of `brackets.ts`, so no cycle); `brackets.ts` adds the two functions and joins it as `mtgBrackets.guide`. **Decision:** every allowance is a table field said plainly, never Wizards' prose restated as ours; the page links Wizards' text with both dates (the rules as of Nov 5, 2025; the list as of Feb 9, 2026). A reviewed ruleset change moves the page with the read (pinned by a page test that mocks the table).
- **The list comes from the database, by containment.** `loadBracketListCards(adapter)` (`src/lib/brackets/list.ts`, core IO, one statement) reads `attrs @> {"game_changer": true}` with the document bound as a parameter (the `ci_attrs_gin` jsonb_path_ops shape; `?` would scan), the game's live identities only (previews kept, as the read counts them), each with its default printing's `small` rendition through `embeddablePrintingImageUrl` and `thumbnailUrl` (a full card, never a crop), name order. Measured on the dev branch: 53 rows, a bitmap scan on `ci_attrs_gin` (55 candidates, 53 kept), 45 ms with the 53 default-printing lookups. The page states the count it found beside the revision's count and the last check from the shared freshness ("Scryfall flagged these 53 cards at the last check, Oct 20, 2026."); a stale feed is said to be stale with the list still shown, and an unread one says so.
- **The precon spread is counted at render, never hard-coded.** `preconSpread` (beside `preconBracket` in `precon-brackets.ts`, which now also returns the `read`) counts every precon's line exactly as its tile and share page say it, lowest band first, then the most precons, a blocked list last, and tallies the adapter's reasons over each read that names a level (a banned list's cards never count). Today, on the dev branch's data: 182 precons in twelve lines, led by "Bracket 1 to 2 · nothing here goes past Core" (141) and "At least Bracket 3 (Upgraded)" (17); 29 read higher than Bracket 1 to 2, holding a Game Changer (18), a two-card combo (12), a combo Commander Spellbook rates Bracket 3 or higher (1) or mass land denial (1), three of them more than one. The page says Wizards no longer ties precons to Bracket 2 (Core), so a precon is read like any other list, and never claims a bracket for precons as a class.
- **Links in.** The Why sheet's What this read assumes gains "How brackets work" (`links.guide` on the adapter, Magic's → `/brackets`) on a line of its own, in the same tab, because the sources line below already holds its one middle dot; the `/precons` Bracket filter's row ends with the same link after a gap. The sitemap lists `/brackets` (twelve static paths); `smoke:seo` checks the page, its canonical, a card link, the door's href and the sitemap entry.
- **Layout:** the model pages' shell (`max-w-browse`, `data-game="mtg"`, `.type-title`, `.type-section` h2s, `.type-small` h3s, prose at `max-w-reading`). The five levels are an ordered list of cards, five across from `lg`, two from `sm`, one a row on phones, where each rule sits beside its value so a card stays about 200 px tall (the stacked form made the phone page 360 px longer). The Game Changers are the index grids' full-card thumbnails (the G2 frame, lazy, name captions; an image-less card's box repeats the name `aria-hidden`), nine across at 1440 and four at 390. The spread and the reasons are the `Table` primitive. The page's own credit ends in the Fan Content sentence, as `/precons`' does.
- **Caching:** ISR, revalidated daily, no request data: four statements per revalidation (the list one, the precon facts three with the freshness shared), zero per visit; `○ /brackets 1d 1y` is the route table's only change. The prerender is 181,524 bytes of HTML (25,051 gzipped) and 94,239 of RSC payload (13,474), so about one six-unit ISR write a day, against `/precons`' 51,802 gzipped.
- **Measured** (the dev branch; the local production build in the built-in pane, the DevTools profile being held by other sessions' MCP servers; Lighthouse 13.5.0 through its CLI): 0 contrast failures at 1440 and 390 in both themes (205 and 202 text elements), no horizontal overflow from 1440 down to 360, accessibility 100 and SEO 100 on desktop and mobile, best practices 96 (the known CSP item), LCP 1.0 s desktop and 5.0 s simulated mobile on the lede (`/sets` 4.9 s, `/privacy` 4.6 s the same way), CLS 0; four statements per render under DB_LOG; `smoke:seo` green (97 checks). `pnpm check` 2,847 / 269 / the same 6 warnings.
- **Left for later:** the Browse menu entry (LATER row 302: the owner said no at pre-flight; the trigger is search traffic in Vercel's analytics) and a breadcrumb JSON-LD (row 303: when the owner asks); row 151 annotated with the spread's new home.

### The declared bracket, offered where the owner shares (DECK-PREVIEW-BRACKET, 2026-10-23; `DECKCHECK-STUDY.md` §2.4; the commit after `3d8a36a`)

- **The unfurl stays declared-only.** The owner kept the rule of 2026-09-30 at GATE 2 (tiles and link previews show the declared bracket, never the computed read; `WAVE4.md:35`, `src/lib/og/labels.test.ts`), so nothing about the image moved: `opengraph-image.tsx`, `og/data.ts`, `og/labels.ts` and their tests are byte-identical. What changed is where the declaration is offered. Before, a target could be set only inside the Why sheet (or from the Suggestions goals line), and at pre-flight 1 of the 24 shared Magic decks on the dev branch had one.
- **One line in the Share dialog, under the link**: "Link previews show Bracket 3 (declared)." with Change, or "Link previews show no bracket." with Declare a bracket. The words are core's `declaredLabel` over the adapter's noun, the same label the image draws (`share-dialog.test.tsx` pins each level against `deckOgLabels`' declared stat), and a level the game doesn't have reads as none, as on the image. The new strings sit in `BRACKET_COPY` (`previewShows`, `previewShowsNone`, `declare`; Change is the goals line's `changeGoals`) under D0's copy guard, with no dash and no promise about previews already posted: the image URL did not change when the target did, so a chat app that fetched it keeps the old picture.
- **Where the line shows:** a deck whose game has brackets and whose Why sheet can open (a commander in the list), while the deck is public or unlisted. **Decision:** none while private, because a private deck's preview is the generic image (the private line speaks; Unlisted in the same dialog brings the line in); none without a commander, because its action would open nothing (a stored target on such a list still unfurls, and the dialog then stays silent rather than wrong); none for One Piece.
- **The action opens the Why sheet at Your target** (the Y6a path: focus on the pressed choice, the Drawer on phones), one dialog at a time. **Decision:** closing that sheet brings the Share dialog back with focus on the line's action, so the owner reads what previews now show and copies the link; Escape walks out one dialog at a time, so nothing traps focus. When that Share dialog closes, focus goes to the header's Share, because its first opener is gone by then (also when it was first opened from "Share this deck"). A Swap… taken from that sheet keeps Y7b's focus rule, and a sheet opened from Why? afterwards closes to the deck. The target saves as any goals edit: one `PATCH {goals}` a debounce later, `updated_at` unmoved.
- **Measured** (Chrome for Testing through puppeteer-core and Lighthouse's user flow, the DevTools MCP's profile being held by another session; before = a worktree of `3d8a36a` on the same port): the dialog grew 28 px for Magic decks (512 × 301 → 329) and stayed 512 × 301 for One Piece; Lighthouse accessibility in snapshot mode on the open dialog 1.00 with no failing audit, before and after; declaring Bracket 3 from the dialog sent one PATCH and the local preview image gained "Bracket 3 (declared)" as its second kicker line; at 390 with touch the line fits one row and its action is 44 × 44. `pnpm check` 2,859 / 269 / the same 6 warnings; the route table identical.
- **Left for later:** the same door on the share page's owner row (LATER row 304), a re-count of declared targets on shared decks a month after the deploy (row 305, holding the before), and Your target's note naming link previews as well as the share page (row 306).
- **Row 306 fired the same day** (the owner asked; the commit after `1450e52`): Your target's note in the sheet reads "Shown on your share page. Your target also shows in link previews and where the deck is listed." **Decision:** two sentences rather than the row's "Shown on your share page, deck tiles and link previews.", because the table exceptions show on the share page only while tiles and previews draw the target alone, and the site's copy never says "tiles". On a private deck the note leaves link previews out ("Your target also shows where the deck is listed."), for the reason the Share dialog hides its line there: the preview is the generic image. The sheet's `linkPreviews` is the editor's `share?.visibility !== "private"` (a draft mints unlisted). Row 304 stays parked by the owner's call, waiting for row 305's count; that row now holds a production before (1 of 14 shared Magic decks declaring, 2026-10-23 21:19 Z), which replaces the dev branch's as the number to compare.

### Truth-up (2026-10-23): what changed since these records were written

The records above stay as written, except the progress tracker's closing paragraph (line 501) and the Wave-2 addendum's W4 bullet (line 510), which this truth-up updated in place. These lines come from the 2026-10-23 docs truth-up against `5f5d021` and say which of their facts no longer hold; each was checked with `git log`, `git show` and `sed -n` on `LATER.md`.

- **LATER row numbers in this file are historical labels** (§6's own rule, line 302). The drifted ones (cited at lines 120, 167, 282, 302, 303, 306, 328, 365 and 493) now sit at: multi-step undo 60, card stacks 61, One Piece quick-add by card number 62 (FIRED in P4.8, `8508830`, so line 365's placeholder gap is fixed), the One Piece null-cost row 63, the claim nudge 64, the `/c/` build CTA 65 and the R1b ISR row 71. ⌘K has had a row since 2026-09-27 (`6e70a7d`, now line 111), so line 348's "no row written yet" is out of date.
- **Closed since the addenda listed them open:** row 143, the sample hand's full One Piece PNG (U6, `58cc253`; line 637); row 218, `tw-animate-css`, which the dependency triage (`f8cd25b`) took out of `package.json` and the lockfile (line 1108); and rows 244, 245, 248 and 250 (the touch sweep, `61a72a1`; lines 1196, 1200, 1204, 1217, 1221, 1241 and 1244).

Every place this file records `/cards/[id]` as ISR (`●`, `x-nextjs-prerender: 1`, "ISR intact", or line 89's hourly revalidate; lines 89, 328, 418, 430, 445, 456, 493, 1218, 1242, 1267 and 1281) is a record of its day. `/c/` and `/l/` stay ISR.

The hand-written `meter.tsx` (line 335), the installed-and-unwired lists (lines 336, 349, 396, 415, 437 and 458), the Select trigger's pin (line 1096) and the unused `sheet.tsx` and `navigation-menu.tsx` (line 1109) describe the old tree; of the rest the last list names, `input` and `popover` have call sites now and `toggle` is still reached only through `toggle-group`. S8 also spelled the four raw NUL map-key separators as `\u0000`, so no file under `src/` but the two icon images holds a NUL byte, and lines 1101 and 1297 no longer hold.
