The other six had no row until this truth-up; they are LATER rows 307 to 312: the Warden character illustration, animated ambient backgrounds, custom artwork uploads, draggable panels, drag-and-drop editing and extra theme families.

### A fixed card-type order, and a Tokens list outside the 100 (DECK-TOKENS, 2026-10-24, the owner's ask; `DECK-TOKENS-session-prompt.md`)

- **The groups read in one fixed order, Magic's `display.typeOrder`**: the commander's zone, then Creatures, Sorceries, Instants, Artifacts, Enchantments, then the two the ask left out (Planeswalkers, Battles) ahead of Lands, then anything rarer by name and typeless cards last (`src/lib/games/type-order.ts`'s rank, shared with the adapter). Before, the groups sorted biggest first, so Lands led most decks and the sections swapped places while a deck was built. The editor, the share page and the "Card types" chart read the same constant, in text and grid and under every sort. One Piece declares none and keeps biggest first (LATER row 320). **Decision:** display only. Which group a card is in stays ingest's `primary_type`, so an artifact land is an Artifact in the list and in the Lands stat alike (row 321).
- **The Tokens section** is the format's `holds: "tokens"` zone (`countsTowardSize: false`), rendered after the card groups as one more group whose heading is the disclosure: Base UI's Collapsible trigger inside an h3 like the groups' h3s, the label beside its count, a chevron that turns, `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls` from the primitive. **Decision:** closed until opened, everywhere. The editor remembers the choice with the view preferences (`tokensOpen` in `deckwarden:deck-view`); the share page never stores it (its toggles read nothing during render). The editor's section waits for the first card of the 100, like View / Group / Sort (Y2a's quiet first screen), unless the deck already holds a token; the share page shows it only when the deck holds one.
- **The rows follow the pane's View.** A text row is the main list's row (quantity, steppers and remove in the editor) with the token's caption beside the name: `display.tokenCaption`, Magic's "1/1 white, flying", because token names don't tell tokens apart (fifteen Soldiers, thirty-one Elementals). **Decision:** commas, not a middle dot, since a row may already hold one under the copy policy; the name keeps its width and the caption truncates first (390 px measured "An…" for Angel before that rule). **Decision:** a grid tile is the printed token with no caption badge, because the badge covered the token's name bar and the printed P/T box and frame already tell the tokens apart. **Decision:** `tokenCaption`, not `idBadge`, because a declared `idBadge` also means "show printed keys" to the Games dialog (`games-dialog.tsx`).
- **"From your cards"** lists the tokens the 100's cards make or use (Scryfall's `all_parts`, ingested as each card's `attrs.tokens`) that the section lacks, each row naming the cards that make it ("Add Soldier (1/1 white), from Elspeth, Sun's Champion"), one click to add, Add all for the rest. The closed heading says how many ("TOKENS 0", then "5 from your cards", no dot between) from the cards the editor already holds, so no request is made until the section opens; opening asks POST `/api/cards/resolve`'s id pass once for the missing keys. **"Add a token"** searches the token catalog alone (GET `/api/cards/search?tokens=1`, the best-match order), each result captioned and previewed on hover. A single add goes through the editor's `handleAdd` (its "Added X" toast and Undo); Add all is one whole-list swap with one Undo, like Import. Tokens never count toward the size, so a full 100 never turns an add into Swap in….
- **Outside the 100 everywhere it matters:** the size, validation, the bracket read, analytics, the export, Suggestions, Autofill (its Apply carries the tokens through), Swap Lab, the Cut Coach, the panels' refetch keys and the combo facts read the playable list (`src/lib/decks/playable.ts`: leader or counted zones, One Piece's leader included); versions, restore, forks, the wire and both views carry the zone. The save route and restore refuse a token in the 100 and a card in Tokens.
- **Measured** (the dev branch after a full Scryfall run with the token catalog: 972 tokens, 3,116 printings, 4,873 linking cards, +2.9 MB; Chrome for Testing through the DevTools MCP, the built-in pane being hidden): a guest deck built with Adeline, Smothering Tithe, Elspeth, Divine Visitation, Sol Ring, Swords and a Plains read Commander, Instant, Artifact, Enchantment, Planeswalker, Land, Tokens; "From your cards" offered Angel, Elspeth's emblem, Human, Soldier and Treasure in one resolve call; Add all saved through one PUT (200) with the deck still 7 / 100, no new combo-facts request and no token in the legality line; after a reload the five tokens, the open section and the Grid view came back; "Add a token" for "soldier" listed twelve Soldiers, each captioned differently; Lighthouse snapshot accessibility 100 with no failed audit on the editor and on the share page with the section open; at 390 with touch no horizontal overflow, the steppers 44 × 44 and the disclosure and field 44 px tall.
- **The token search reads a token's words** (follow-up, 2026-10-24, the owner's report: the Tarkir: Dragonstorm Commander Elemental, 4/4 blue-red, couldn't be found). "elemental" matched 39 tokens, the box showed twelve, and that one came thirtieth. Now the adapter reads the box (`tokenQuery`, Magic's `mtgTokenQuery`): a P/T, color words and keywords become the search's own filters and the rest is the name, so "Elemental 4/4 blue red" finds that one alone, and the placeholder says so ("Name, P/T or colors: Elemental 4/4 blue red", `display.tokenSearch`). A broad search says "Showing 12 of 39." with how to narrow it, under a "Show 12 more" button, and namesakes read by P/T, then colors: tokens are unranked, so the best-match order had left them in oracle-id order. Measured on the dev branch: the narrowed search returned the one token; the broad one paged 39 unique rows in four pages with that Elemental 17th; in the editor it went into Tokens with the deck still 2 / 100, took the TTDC #27 printing and came back after a reload.
- **Left for later:** rows 320 to 330 (One Piece's order, multi-type groups, tokens in export and import, precons' box tokens, the purge's "empty", a tokens-only deck's View control, helper cards on non-token layouts, suggestions that follow Scryfall's printed token, One Piece's 51-card count off the editor, the token search's order by use, a card-search pointer to the Tokens section).

### The Tokens list fills itself from the deck's cards (TOKEN-FILL, 2026-10-24, the owner's ask; `TOKEN-FILL-session-prompt.md`)

- **Derived, never stored.** The Tokens section shows the tokens the deck's cards make (Scryfall's links, `attrs.tokens`) as part of the list, without a click, on the editor and on every share page — precons included, so "Calling All Angels" now lists its Angels, Clue, Spirit and The Monarch. One pure module (`src/lib/decks/token-fill.ts`) derives the set over the playable list; the editor hydrates the token cards through one resolve call when the section opens, the share page loads them in one statement with the hidden ones already out (`token-fill-load.ts`).
- **Two kinds of row, told apart.** The player's own tokens (the search's adds) are rows of the zone with a count, steppers and remove, and read first. Under them an eyebrow, "From your cards", and the derived rows: the name and caption as the text view sets them, no count (the count column is left empty, not zero), the makers right-aligned and muted ("from Dockside Extortionist, Smothering Tithe"), and in the editor a Hide (an eye-off icon in the row's reveal slot, like Remove). In the grid the derived tiles follow the deck's own with no ×N badge. A stored row of the same key supersedes the derived one, so a token never reads twice. The heading counts both.
- **Hidden, not gone.** Hide persists on the deck (`decks.hidden_tokens`, its own PATCH body, never a bump of `updated_at`, never copied on fork) and the hidden tokens the cards still link show as chips under the rows — "Hidden: Treasure", each a Show, plus Show all — so the choice is visible and reversible without a trick. A hidden key whose makers left the deck shows nothing. The share page applies the owner's choices on the server: the pod sees what the owner sees, and a visitor's wire never carries the keys.
- **Copy.** The empty section now reads "No tokens yet. The ones your cards make show up here; they don't count toward the deck's size." The "N from your cards" heading hint is gone: the tokens are in the list, and the count says it.

### What a target pick changed, said where it happens (TARGET-CHANGES, 2026-10-24, the owner's ask; `TARGET-CHANGES-session-prompt.md`)

- **The ask, and what it really asked.** "When you set a target bracket the changes that it makes pops up and tells you what it auto changed." A target never edits the list: it moves which findings the read marks above it, what the tools that follow it leave out, and what the deck says where others see it. Every reader of `targetLevel` was traced (the read's `conflicts`, the bracket line, `applyGoals`, `shellGoals`, the alternatives, `comboBadge`, the share page, the tiles, the link preview, the Share dialog's line) and none writes `deck_cards`, so the callout leads with that: "Your cards didn't change."
- **A callout under the choice, not a toast or a second dialog.** Picking a level pops it in under Your target's `Segmented` (U2's `dw-in`, motion-safe only; it scrolls itself into view in the phone Drawer, focus staying on the choice). It says, in order: the title ("Target set: Bracket 3 (Upgraded)", "Target changed from Bracket 2 to Bracket 4", "Target cleared") with Undo after it; "Your cards didn't change."; the list against the new target in the conflict callout's own words ("1 thing in the list puts the deck above it. See Above your target below.", "Nothing in the list puts the deck above it (before, 1 did)."); what the level allows, as the adapter's guide rows verbatim (all of them for a first target, only the rows that differ on a change: "Game Changers: No limit (was None)", none when cleared); which tools follow it, only those the adapter declares, by the names the editor shows them under ("Suggestions, Autofill and Alternatives leave out cards above it, and the Combos tab marks combos above it."); and where it shows, by `targetShown`'s rule (link previews only when the deck isn't private).
- **Computed, not described.** One pure core module (`src/lib/brackets/target-change.ts`) turns the pick into a change: the count is `read.conflicts.length` of the read the sheet renders after the pick, beside the count captured at the pick; tests pin it against the real Magic `assess` for every pick between Not set and 1 to 5. The callout renders only while the target is the one the pick set, so a pick the goals never took says nothing.
- **Safe to try.** Undo puts the previous target back through `withTarget` on the goals as they are then (answers and exceptions untouched); Dismiss takes the callout away; both hand focus back to the choice. The level already set does nothing. Only a short status (the title and "Your cards didn't change.") is announced; the callout itself is a labelled group. It stays until dismissed, replaced, or the sheet closes.
- **Deviations from the session prompt.** The prompt named the tools "Swap Lab" and "Combo Radar"; no screen shows those names, so the callout says "Alternatives" (the Card tab's section, with its Swap… doors) and "the Combos tab". The prompt's count noun became "things", the word the conflict callout already uses ("These put the deck above it"), since "finding" appears in no rendered copy. No card list can prove Bracket 5 (cEDH is the player's call), so the engine pins run fixture lists that read 1 to 4 against every target.
- **Measured in the pane** (a precon draft, Abzan Armor, at least Bracket 3 with one Game Changer): set 2, change to 4, Undo back to 2 (focus on "2", the row holding `{targetLevel: 2}`), cleared, set 1 at 375 px and 320 px (the callout inside 16 px gutters, its Undo and Dismiss 44 by 44 under touch, no sideways scroll), and change 1 to 3 in the light theme.

### A card search in the header, on every page (HEADER-SEARCH, 2026-10-24, the owner's ask; `HEADER-SEARCH-session-prompt.md`; LATER row 111's first half; the commit after `089f7b0`)

- **What it is.** `HeaderSearch` (`src/components/search/header-search.tsx`) puts X2's `NameSuggest` over every card inside a real GET form to `/cards` (one search landmark, "Cards", around one combobox, "Search cards"): suggestions from two letters, each row opening its card page, and Enter with nothing highlighted, or the "Show every card matching" row, opening the full results. The form works before hydration. Nothing is asked until the reader types, no request data is read (the island reads the path only, like the nav beside it), and the route table is byte-identical.
- **Phones.** Below md a 44 px search button opens a full-width row laid over the header row (absolute, the same height: the editor's toolbar stays at 57 px), its input focused inside the tap (`flushSync` the open, then `.focus()`), because iOS raises the keyboard only for focus inside the gesture and Base UI's popups move focus a frame later. Close, Esc with the popup shut, and a route change close it, and focus returns to the button; the covered controls are hidden while it's open, so Tab never lands under it. The button takes Appearance's slot: below md the Appearance trigger hides and its items (U4a's `AppearanceItems`) end the phone Menu, so the wordmark keeps LATER row 250's 360 px step. Measured at 390 px with touch: the wordmark row 147.8 px and the cluster 159.7, so the row is 355.5 px and fits from 360 with 4.5 px to spare; with Appearance kept inline beside the button it was 403.5 px, and the wordmark would have had to go on every phone up to 416 px.
- **Tablets.** From md the box sits inline before the account slot, up to 24 rem. Below lg the Appearance label and the account name show as their icons (both were `max-sm:sr-only`), so at 768 px the box keeps its placeholder and `/` keycap: 229 px measured signed out, where the first cut left it 152 px and bare (under 10 rem the placeholder and keycap step aside, a floor no measured row reaches now).
- **Both games.** The box searches the open page's game, read off `data-game` on `#main` or an ancestor when the reader focuses or types (never an element inside it: `/account`'s first one is a deck tile), and Magic otherwise. When that game has no match, `NameSuggest`'s opt-in `fallback` asks the other game once and says so: "No Magic cards match. 8 One Piece cards." (short names; GameSwitch's "Magic: The Gathering" reads badly mid-sentence). Enter and the footer follow the game that answered (a hidden `game=optcg`). Every other `NameSuggest` caller is unchanged.
- **The keyboard.** `/` jumps to the box (and selects its text) from any page but the editor routes, where `/` stays the editor's own search; not while typing, with a modifier, or inside a dialog or menu (`isTextTarget`). The keycap shows only where `/` works, on fine pointers.
- **Weight.** Measured on production builds of `6ade698` and this tree: a static page's scripts went from 385,088 to 408,225 bytes gzipped (+22.6 KiB, 6%; `/privacy`, `/legal` and `/brackets` alike), and `/decks/new` +5.8 KiB, since the editor already loaded most of it. Nearly all of it is Base UI's combobox, which only the indexes loaded before; loading it on first use is LATER row 337. A log of what people search is row 336 (a privacy call).
- **Deviations from the session prompt.** Appearance moved into the phone Menu instead of the wordmark hiding up to 416 px (the first cut's answer to the 390 px overflow); the tablet squeeze, which the prompt didn't foresee, is met by icons below lg; `data-game` is read from `#main` and its ancestors only; the fallback line uses short game names.
- **Measured in the pane** (dev, 2026-10-24): at 390 px the tap focused the input in the same task and "sol ri" listed Sol Ring first, Enter opened `/cards?q=sol+ri` and closed the row; at 1280 px `/` focused the box without typing a slash, "luffy" on home made exactly two requests (Magic, then One Piece) and set `game=optcg`; on `/leaders` (768 px) "zoro" asked One Piece once with no fallback line; on the editor `/` focused the editor's "Card search", not the header's; a row click opened Rhystic Study's page; 320 px had no sideways scroll and the Menu listed Dark, Light, System and Background art; both themes.

### Swap to fit, the fewest swaps to a target (BRACKET-FIT, 2026-10-24, the owner's ask; `BRACKET-FIT-session-prompt.md`; the commit after `25b9749`)

- **The ask, built as the fewest swaps.** "Once you set a target bracket after your deck is built can we have a button to swap all the suggested cards all at once to make it that desired bracket." The callout's per-card Swap… buttons name every card a finding holds, but the read's own lines already say the minimum ("Remove 1 of them to fit Bracket 3", "Remove A or B to fit Bracket 2"): swapping every listed card changes the player's deck for nothing. So the one button proposes the fewest swaps, each card out replaced by one that shares its job, and the deck keeps its size.
- **The door.** "Swap to fit Bracket 2…" at the head of Your target's conflict callout, wherever the adapter declares `brackets.fixes` and Swap Lab and the read has a conflict. Until 2026-10-27 it also needed a card that could go out, and the owner, setting a target on a deck whose only Game Changer had no community roles (Seedborn Muse in Abzan Armor), found no button and no word why; now it always shows, and when no card can go its sheet leads with "None of the cards that put the deck above Bracket 2 can be swapped here, but you can still remove or replace them yourself.", lists each card's reason under "What swaps can't fix", asks nothing, and only closes ("Done", no "Swap 0 cards"). The per-card Swap… stay. It closes the Why sheet the way Swap… does; focus comes back to "Why?" (or to the bracket line while the new list's facts are checked). Dialog slot `"target-fit"`, the Fit sheet's shell: the full-height Drawer on phones, the wide Modal from md.
- **What each conflict needs is the adapter's.** `BracketsMeta.fixes(read, input)` returns `{factor, remove, from}` per conflict: at least `remove` copies of `from` must leave. Magic's: the Game Changers past the target's allowance, every clear land-denial copy (Brackets 1 to 3), every extra-turn copy (Bracket 1), one non-commander piece per combo. A commander is never in `from`; its share is named, never swapped. Pinned against the real `assess` at targets 1 to 3: taking `remove` of `from` for every fix leaves no conflict, and one fewer from any fix leaves its factor. Core names no rule.
- **The plan is the exact minimum.** A pure core planner (`src/lib/brackets/target-fit.ts`): forced cards first, then a greedy (the most unmet need, so a Game Changer that is also a combo piece counts twice) whose size bounds an exact branch and bound. The greedy alone missed the minimum on 5 of 4,000 generated instances (by one card each); the planner equals brute force on all of them and on every dev deck at every target. Each card out takes its best-ranked Swap Lab option, judged in its place under the target (none hidden by the goals, none over the per-card budget, no incoming card twice: a matching re-routes before a card goes without). A card with no option is planned around ("Remove A or B": A has none, so B goes).
- **The asks.** Swap Lab's route with its own body (the target rides every ask), plan order, 3 at a time and 9 per open (under a third of the 30-a-minute bucket). A 429 stops the asking and says so; Try again resumes; closing aborts. Opening, asking and reviewing write nothing.
- **The proof inside the product.** The list after the checked swaps goes to `useBracketFacts` (unchanged, one GET for its id set) and the adapter's `assess` with the incoming cards' wires. The footer says "After these swaps, nothing in the list puts the deck above Bracket 2." only when that read finds nothing above the target; otherwise it quotes the read's own sentences. The polite status says the verdict as a whole sentence ("After these swaps, 2 things still put the deck above Bracket 2."). Swap stays the player's call either way.
- **The price of the change.** "The cards coming in cost $8.23.", each card's cheapest printing, the cards the player owns left out (the editor's one owned set: a collection's identities and an owned precon's, PRECON-OWNED).
- **What it can't fix, named.** "What swaps can't fix" lists every card that stays, with its reason: the commander, alternatives not offered (lands), several copies, no community roles yet, no alternative under the goals, kept by the player, or not looked at past the ask limit ("Didn't look at 2 more cards. Swap these first, then open this again for more."). When a fix can't be fully met, the plan still swaps what it can and names the rest, so the player can finish by hand (the owner's call after the ship, 2026-10-24: keep it so, rather than swap nothing when the target is out of reach).
- **Apply.** `fitList` in place (each new card where its old one was, every other row untouched), ONE `applyListSwap`, "Swapped N cards" with one Undo for the batch. A draft mints one row carrying the target.
- **Measured.** The dev corpus (211 Commander decks: 182 precons, 29 user decks), a flagless stand-in for every card that can go: fixes equal `read.conflicts` every time, and `assess` on the after-list leaves exactly the fixes the plan named short; at target 2, 42 decks are above it, 25 fit fully and 17 keep a named fix. Through the real Swap Lab, in process: 19 of the 29 precons past Bracket 2 fit at target 2 (28 asks, at most 3 per precon, 21 distinct cards in); every miss is a card with none of Swap Lab's 17 roles or a land; none of the 21 incoming cards is a Game Changer, by our flags and by Scryfall's live `is:gamechanger` search (53 cards). The editor's client chunks grew by about 11.4 KiB gzipped with PRECON-OWNED (722,563 to 734,214 bytes over every chunk); the route table is byte-identical.
- **In the pane and the simulator** (dev, 2026-10-24): Everyone's Invited! at target 2 (7 conflicts: a Game Changer and six two-card combos) planned 4 swaps for $8.23 and named Arcane Adaptation (no roles) and The World Tree (a land) as what swaps can't fix; Peer Through Time fit with one (Cyclonic Rift for Ugin's Binding, $0.78), and its Undo restored the list and "You own 100/100 · nothing missing"; at 320 px nothing in the Drawer overflowed and the door, every option row and Swap measured 44 px under touch. In the iOS Simulator (iPhone 15, Mobile Safari) Timey-Wimey's sheet swapped Farewell for Austere Command ($0.33) and the line then read "Your target: Bracket 2 · the cards say at least 2".
- **Deviations from the session prompt.** An exact search replaces the greedy alone (the brief's bar was the exact minimum); a card answered with no option is never a candidate rather than last in the tie order; "Coming in for X." sits on its own line (Fit's inline "· picked for X" risked sideways scroll at 320 px); the status sentence and the owned set were wired at integration.

### "Do you own this precon?" (PRECON-OWNED, 2026-10-24, the owner's ask; `PRECON-OWNED-session-prompt.md`; migration `0021`; the commit after `5c3815f`)

- **The ask, and the gap under it.** "If you are starting from a precon it asks Do you own this deck? so that it can calculate the price once you make changes and it would minus the cost since you own the whole precon." The precon draft forgot its precon at its first save (the create carried the name only), so nothing could ask later or change the answer. The deck now remembers it.
- **The deck remembers its precon.** `decks.precon_deck_id` (uuid, a foreign key to decks ON DELETE SET NULL) is set once by the server: from the create's `fromPrecon` slug, which a precon draft's first create always carries whatever edit mints it, or by a fork of a precon. A fork of a user's deck inherits nothing. `decks.owns_precon` (boolean, NULL = not asked) is the answer, saved through its own PATCH body `{ownsPrecon}`, which never moves `updated_at`; on a draft the answer is the edit that mints the row, in its create.
- **One line, under the leader zone.** "Started from Breed Lethality, a precon. Do you own it?" with Yes and No; answered, "Started from Breed Lethality, a precon you own." with Change, which asks again in place. The name is an apposition because some products begin with "The". The line arrives with the list (the owner's GET, or the draft's own seed), so nothing on screen moves; its controls sit after a gap, underlined, 44 px on touch; focus follows the answer.
- **A Yes counts copies, not identities.** The precon's own quantities are owned at $0 in the ownership line (shown with a collection or an owned precon), every goals line's estimate, the Fit sheet and Swap to fit's price line, which all take one owned set. 14 Forests from a 12-Forest precon cost two; a card cut and added back is owned again; the commander counts like any card. The rows' "In your collection" mark stays the collection's.
- **Private.** Only the owner's GET carries `deck.precon` (`{slug, name, owns, cards}`, one indexed read of the precon's counted cards); `deckMetaJson` names neither column, so the share page, link previews and every other wire stay as they were. The precon's row id never leaves the server.
- **The price line wraps on phones** (integration): at 320 px the truncated line hid its end, the price ("nothing missi…"); below md it now wraps, and from md it still truncates where the row must not wrap.
- **Measured.** All 182 dev precons read "You own 100/100 · nothing missing" on Yes. Three staples swapped into Breed Lethality read "missing ≈ $149" ($149.10), the sum of their `cheapest_usd`, equal to the lowest `prices.usd` Scryfall's API gave for each (Cyclonic Rift $29.57, Rhystic Study $66.76, Smothering Tithe $52.77). The No estimate equals `/precons`' figure on 182 of 182 and the share page's "est. price today" on 179 (the share page rounds a float sum down on three half-dollar totals: LATER row 348). In the pane: a precon draft answered Yes minted one row with one POST (`fromPrecon` and `ownsPrecon` in the create) and one PUT; No and Change each sent one PATCH; Rhystic Study in for one card read "You own 99/100 · missing ≈ $67". `pnpm smoke:decks` passed its precon chain (create, owner GET, PATCH, visitor GET, a deck from no precon) on the dev branch.
- **Production migration** (the owner's yes in chat, 2026-10-24): applied through Neon's SQL editor before the push, in one transaction (the two `ADD COLUMN`s, the foreign key, the journal row); read back: both columns nullable, the key `ON DELETE SET NULL`, 0 of 203 decks set, journal id 22 (hash `63ea4e5c…`), the same row `pnpm db:migrate` wrote on the dev branch.

### Upgrade toward a target, Suggestions in (BRACKET-UP, 2026-10-27, the owner's ask; `BRACKET-UP-session-prompt.md`; the commit after `b34957b`)

- **The ask, BRACKET-FIT's upward twin.** "Why cant the button show up if i want to upgrade a precon to bracket 4 and have the whole suggested list apear for the swap button." Swap to fit only fixes cards above a target, so a precon reading Bracket 2 aimed at Bracket 4 had no button. The approved shape: "an Upgrade toward Bracket N button for when the target is above what the cards show. The Suggestions list comes in, each card paired with the weakest card it replaces, in one review sheet with the check of the list after, the cost of the new cards, and one Undo."
- **The door.** "Below your target" in the conflict callout's place, when the target is set, nothing is above it, and the target is above the band the read's floor (`read.suggested ?? read.minimum`) is in, by the adapter's own `bands` (`belowTargetBand`, `src/lib/brackets/target-bands.ts`). A list nothing flags reads "1 to 2", so a target of 2 is met (integration: the subagent's floor rule offered it an upgrade). A pending call is no floor: on "Bracket 3 or 4 · one combo is your call" the door toward 4 shows, and the box says an answer can raise the read (integration, a second pass: a gate on every band the line names hid the door on 17 of the 181 dev precons below 4 with no word why, the way Swap to fit's door once hid itself). A read whose line names no level shows no box: a list under the format's size, or one blocked by a card the format doesn't allow (2 dev precons), whose line already says what it needs first. The box reads "Your target is Bracket 4 (Optimized). The cards show at least Bracket 3 (Upgraded)." (what the cards and the answers say when answers raised the read; "Nothing in this list raises the bracket." with nothing flagged), then "An answer under Your call can raise it." while a call is open ("Nothing in this list raises the bracket unless an answer under Your call does." when nothing else is flagged), then "Upgrade toward Bracket 4…". The box shows only with its door, which the editor passes where the adapter declares Swap in's swap and cuts (One Piece: none). It closes the Why sheet the way Swap to fit does; focus comes back to "Why?" (or to the bracket line while the new list's facts are checked). Dialog slot `"target-up"`, the Fit shell.
- **The asks.** ONE `POST /api/recommendations` with the editor's live list, the deck's goals and its per-card budget goal (the panel's 25 rows), for a saved deck as for a draft, since the GET would read a target the autosave may not have written yet; the route's doc comment says so, its behavior is unchanged. ONE `/api/cards/resolve` for the names, with the panel's id guard: a name that resolves to another card drops its row, and the sheet says so. A 429 says so with Try again. Opening, asking and reviewing write nothing.
- **The pairs.** A pure core planner (`src/lib/brackets/target-up.ts`) over Swap in's machine. Each card, in Suggestions' order, takes the Cut Coach's cheapest ordinary cut at its mana value, the best-ranked card coming in taking the weakest card; once every row has had its own, a card with none left takes the weakest ordinary cut at another cost, and its row says so ("No suggested cut at mana value 2, so the weakest card at another cost goes."): Suggestions fill the curve's gaps, so with its own cost alone two rows in three came back unpaired. And the plan cuts on its own only from the deck's weaker half by the Coach, or its weakest as many as the rows that pair when that is more (integration: on Breed Lethality at target 4, Carrion Feeder came in for Sol Ring, the 1-drop slot holding nothing weaker once Viscera Seer took Hardened Scales; now the 25 cuts are the precon's filler, the read and the price the same). Never the commander, a land (but the player's own pick for a land coming in), a complete-combo piece, a card the Coach can't rank, a card coming in, or one card twice. The chooser offers the card's cost first, then the rest, in the Coach's order; a card another row holds shows as "Mind Stone (out for Arcane Signet)", and taking it re-plans that row, unless that row's own choice holds it. The player's choices win and never move; unchecking a row never moves another row's card.
- **What starts checked.** A paired row, in rank order, unless the adapter's `impact` says its card would put the list above the target once the checked rows above it are in; such a row starts unchecked with the judge's reasons. Suggestions are ranked one card at a time, so with every row checked, precons with no Game Changer would have taken up to 8 of them at a target of 3, and 83 of the 151 would have gone above it. The judgement is live (unchecking a Game Changer above lets the next one in), and the player's own check wins.
- **The proof inside the product.** The list after the checked swaps goes to `useBracketFacts` (one GET for its id set) and the adapter's `assess` with the incoming cards' wires. The footer says "After these swaps, the cards show at least Bracket 4 (Optimized). That's your target." only when that read reaches the target, with the read's own sentences for the floor; short of it, "Cards can only show a floor. If the deck plays at Bracket 4 (Optimized), say so under How it plays." (no list proves cEDH); anything that would put the deck above the target, a combo two cards coming in complete together included, is named in the read's sentences, with a whole-sentence verdict for screen readers.
- **Price and apply.** BRACKET-FIT's price line over the editor's one owned set ("The cards coming in cost $1,853.32.", Goblin Storm's upgrade at 4; `costOf` and the Fit shell are exported from the Swap to fit sheet). Upgrades run past $1,000, so the line groups thousands since integration, for Swap to fit too. `fitList` in place (each new card where its old one was, every other row untouched), ONE `applyListSwap`, "Swapped N cards" with one Undo for the batch. A draft mints one row carrying the target.
- **Measured.** The dev corpus, 182 precons through the real engine in process (Suggestions with the target's goals, the resolve's first pass, the sheet's own plan and judge, `fitList`, then `assess` with the after list's complete combos), at the shipped door: it shows on 151 precons at target 3 and 179 at target 4 (the other two below each are blocked by a card the format doesn't allow; 6 and 17 of those shown have an open call). At 3, 149 reach Bracket 3 after 23.3 swaps on average and none goes above it (the judge leaves 1.4 rows unchecked on average, up to 8; with every row checked, 83 would have gone above), and the two Breed Lethality precons stay at 2 with no Game Changer coming in. At 4, 112 reach Bracket 4 after 24.7 swaps, and the other 67 show at least 3 and say so. ### Home's opening in the owner's chosen look (FRESH-UI-HOME, 2026-10-30, the owner's ask; `FRESH-UI-HOME-session-prompt.md`; the commit after `3815f98`)

- **Amends `WAVE5.md` D6 section 1 (the split hero and the fan) and U5's "The fan's geometry", and nothing else D6 says.** The owner commissioned a fresh UI from a Codex session on 2026-10-30 (four generated references; an implementation left uncommitted in a worktree, preserved as `caf788f` on `codex/fresh-ui-2026-10-30`), then on 2026-10-30 pointed at the home reference: "this specific look I like". The opening is that look, built at the source: a full-bleed band (`.home-band`, a cast of the primary green over the page's background, deeper at the top and still greener than the page at its lower edge, so the edge reads) with the H1 at the left and the day's three commanders standing upright at the right on a green-lit stage (`.home-fan` keeps its name and slot: a three-column grid whose boxes come from `aspect-ratio`, so nothing shifts when the cards land; the glow is the grid's own `::before`, a radial gradient of the primary at `--home-stage` inside the grid's stacking context, so it never paints over the text; the band clips it, since it overhangs the grid by 12 % and widened the document to 1,320 px at 1280 before the clip); under the band the doors row (`.home-doors`: the `hero` Button size, 56 px with a 16 px label and the arrow as `data-icon="inline-end"`, the width of its column, to `/decks/new`; a ghost "Browse commanders" with an arrow; and `nav[aria-label="Choose your game"]` with a tile per game, each a field of its accent over the card tone, 12 % at rest and 18 % on hover, `data-game` setting `--accent-game`, the serif name on `.type-section`, the format line on `.type-label-sm`, DeckTile's 2 px lift, to that game's draft); then the Magic region in its gallery form (`GameCard headline`: "Find your next commander." on `.type-headline`, the blurb, the six cards, then the region's four doors with their hrefs byte-identical), the One Piece field as U5 left it, and D6's hairline rhythm from the field on.
